# Runner-direction panel — 2026-09-22

**The human's words, verbatim (st189, 2026-09-22):** "critique this direction with a panel, make an artifact, and convince me this is the right direction."

**Process:** adversarialPanelReview (D0187), full nine-persona roster, dispatched in parallel per round with one shared brief (Appendix 0); from round 2 each panelist received the revised artifact plus a digest of every panelist's prior position and an invitation to rebut. Every panelist verified against the repository at HEAD `f365cb7a`, never against the artifact's own assertions. **Independence note (permanent, D0187):** same-model subagent panelists are a rigor framing, NOT independent critics; their critiques record `critiquedBy = aiModel` and satisfy no critic-independence requirement. This record claims convergence pressure, never independence.

**Verdicts by round** (tallied by `scratchpad/panel_tally.py` over the reports; the round-2 line was first under-counted by a regex that missed two panelists' severity spellings — issue672):

| Round | ACCEPT | OBJECT | BLOCKER | MAJOR | MINOR |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2 | 7 | 3 | 47 | 33 |
| 2 | 0 | 9 | 0 | 36 | 35 |
| 3 | 3 | 6 | 0 | 28 | 27 |

**Outcome:** convergence was not reached in three rounds; per `aprConverge` the irreducible residue went to the human as seven held Decisions (D0543–D0549), each a tab of the published brief: https://claude.ai/artifact/3e35AtqqdYE7sVPK3fpfkP. Every author-fixable round-3 finding is landed in revision 4 (§R3 there). Charter: D0542. Disclosure: one panelist (PESSIMIST-2, round 3) briefly wrote the repository root and reversed it in the same turn; the author verified `git status --porcelain` matched the session's opening state; recorded as issue671 with resolver `dcPanelistsAreDispatchedIntoAnIsolatedTree`.

**Reading order:** Appendix D4 is the final direction artifact. Appendices D1–D3 are the revisions it grew from, each carrying its finding→resolution map. Appendices R1–R3 hold every panelist report verbatim, nine per round.

## Appendix D1 — direction artifact, revision 1, verbatim

### Direction under review: keel owns the AI task — a runner-protocol design (revision 1, 2026-09-22)

**Status: a direction, not a Decision.** Nothing here is built, no spec file exists, the design is unapproved (brainstorm HARD-GATE). This document is the artifact the adversarial panel attacks. Panelists verify every claim against the repository at `C:\Users\WilliamWeatherholtz\claude_code\sysmlv2-ai-toolkit` (HEAD `f365cb7a`), never against this document's own assertions.

#### 0. The human's direction, verbatim (Statements st181–st188, `.tracking/intake/intake-2026-09-1*.sysml`, `intake-2026-09-2*.sysml`)

- st181: "Let's make a branch of keel where the tool owns AI tasks instead of the reverse. We mentioned this earlier, a-la Jira, Linear, etc. I want to use the great stuff we've made so far, just with absolute deterministic control as a starting point. And strong customizable processes with clear inputs, outputs, hooks, and definition"
- st182: "C, but I'm interested in going fully browser UI, I think and effectively ditching the cli. It's brought more issues than solutions (how many EOL bugs again?). … I do want a full client-server architecture here that gives AI agents much more defined and narrow scope which can run in parallel and have more definite inputs and outputs. And keel can still detect discrepancies and lapsed guards, etc. I envision it working thusly - a process is defined which develop processes. That process is to define inputs, outputs, and steps. At the start/end of a step, what goes into or out of keel? What goes into or out of an agent? Then chain together steps ad nauseum until the process is defined. And a process can be made to use other processes nested-like. Is that defensible? Is it free from critique?"
- st183: "Let's just do both. A constrained container option and and an unconstrained agent option. Or is that not a good idea? … Though the bash issue still seems insurmountable"
- st185: "Yes I meant a keel subsystem/mode, though I might make it a totally separate project because I feel like it's quite distinct architecturally."
- st186: "I think (ii) is right where feasible." — (ii) = runner daemons claim leased steps, versus (i) the server spawns agents.
- st188: "browser only for the user, runner-facing (i.e. CLI, yes?) protocol is fine too. as far as what the runner daemon actually is, etc., do some more research for best practices with existing frameworks, lookup complaints from 50 threads in forums to see user-identified weaknesses of their approach, and let's set ourselves apart."
- st189 (this review's trigger): "critique this direction with a panel, make an artifact, and convince me this is the right direction."

#### 1. The direction in one paragraph

keel today is a work-tracking engine whose truth is SysML v2 text in git and whose state is computed (CLAUDE.md §1). Today the AI agent owns its task: it reads the frontier, decides what to do, edits the tree, records its own results, and 98 lines of prose working rules (CLAUDE.md §4, lines 82–180) plus hooks and guards try to keep it honest. The direction inverts that: **keel assigns and owns the task**, the way Linear or Jira own a human's task. Processes are chains of typed **steps** with declared inputs and outputs; a frozen meta-process defines processes; processes nest. Each step is run by a **runner daemon** that claims it under a lease, materialises exactly the declared inputs into a disposable sandbox, runs the agent, and lets the server **harvest only declared outputs** after exit. A step is **done when its gate is green**, never when the agent says so. Two step classes: **CONSTRAINED** (sandbox, no credentials inside, egress only to declared providers through a proxy holding the one key) and **UNCONSTRAINED** (full-tool agent in a real checkout, whose outputs are proposals that must pass a constrained/deterministic step before landing). The **human's only surface is a browser**; the runner-facing surface is a versioned JSON protocol with thin CLI clients. It may be a keel subsystem/mode or a separate project (st185, open).

#### 2. Why this direction — the claims the panel should attack

C1. **The current shape has a structural defect that no rule fixes.** The agent that does the work also records that the work is done. keel's mitigations (the `// RAN:` receipt refusal at `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:88`; `proposed` verdicts for AI-examined passes, D0312; hooks in `.keel/metrics/hooks.jsonl`; 66+ guards in `.engine/docs/guards.md`) are each a control added after a specific defect (D0047). The issue ledger shows the pattern repeating: issues 082–085 (CLAUDE.md rules ignored), 255/256 (tool output captured into a governance record), 543 (evidence through a shell arg), and the 2026-09-20 landscape review's D14 thread ("The rule file was loaded into every context window of the session. It was read. It was quoted. It governed nothing."). Claim: inverting ownership removes the class, not the instance.

C2. **The design sits in an empty quadrant, and that is why nothing can be bought.** The 2026-09-20 landscape review (`docs/reviews/agent-harness-landscape-2026-09-20.md`, D0540, 94 tools) found that every process-defining tool (Spec Kit, OpenSpec, BMAD, Kiro, Symphony, Camunda, Kestra, Argo, Temporal) lets the agent or a returned function say when a step is done, and every tool that computes done outside the agent (Harbor, SWE-bench, Inspect, Dagster asset checks, in-toto) owns no persistent work item. Nobody materialises declared inputs into an agent sandbox and harvests only declared outputs — that is the build-system norm (Bazel, REAPI) and absent from every agent harness.

C3. **The pieces the design lacked exist and converge.** Egress proxy holding the one credential (six sandbox vendors, GitHub, GitLab, Buildkite); reserve→acquire→heartbeat lease (Buildkite, Temporal, Conductor, Symphony, Linear, Paperclip); in-toto MATCH as the drift guard; version-pinned in-flight runs (Restate, Vercel, DBOS, Camunda). Nothing is invented from scratch except the combination.

C4. **The field's runners fail at seams a work-tracker can hold.** The 2026-09-21 review (`docs/reviews/runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md`, D0541, 122 user threads, 67 ADDRESSED / 45 VULNERABLE / 9 N/A) found a third of all complaints (35) are one defect — a stored flag standing in for a live fact — which keel's founding invariant (state computed, never stored) answers by construction. Nobody in 122 threads renews the job credential on the heartbeat, kills the child on lease loss, records claim refusals for a human, or budgets human attention. Each is a fact about the *work*, which a tracker can hold and a CI runner cannot.

C5. **The human keeps what they said is best-in-class** (st182): computed state, typed edges, guards, decisions, verbatim-words acceptance, the knowledge records. The direction reuses the engine; it changes who owns the task and where the agent runs.

#### 3. The runner-protocol design section (proposed, for approval)

1. **Identity.** Enrolment credential = organisation API key or federated identity only; a personal subscription token is refused at enrolment. `enrol` negotiates protocol version first (`low/high`, structured refusal) and yields a per-process **session token**. `acquire` yields a signed **run token** bound to the lease.
2. **Claim.** `reserve` → `acquire` is an atomic checkout; a conflict is a coded response, never retried; readiness is recomputed at acquire; every write carries the run id.
3. **Lease.** The claim response carries `lease_seconds` and `heartbeat_seconds`, both server-chosen and revisable on every heartbeat reply. Maximum lease and wall-clock deadline are declared on the step type, never global. The heartbeat runs in its own process, hits the cheapest endpoint, echoes `sent_at`/`received_at`, carries progress; its reply may carry `cancel`, `quiet`, `terminate`, `retire_at` and a new cadence. **A rejected heartbeat → the runner kills the step's process group and reports `expired`.** The run token is renewed on each accepted heartbeat; the final report is accepted under the token the lease last issued.
4. **Step states** include `awaiting-human` (not leasable) and `stalled` (heartbeats without progress), distinct from `expired`. A poison-pill counter after N re-leases raises an Issue. An unclaimed ready step past a declared interval is a fault.
5. **Run.** Startup is a timed phase with its own failure class. The egress proxy holds the credential, enforces per-step declared providers and operations, names the blocked host in a denial, stores the provider-returned model id (mismatch fails the step), meters tokens and cost against the step's ceiling — cost is a second lease bound.
6. **Harvest.** A server pull after exit, idempotent per run token, fenced against expired or superseded tokens, one write with completion. The receipt is REAPI-shaped: input digests, output digests, worker id, queued/start/exit/harvest timestamps, model id. Sandboxes and worktrees are reaped on lease expiry.
7. **Retire.** Per runner version: `current / ramping / draining / drained`; in-flight runs pinned; no self-update; an idle runner is told why it is idle.
8. **Human budget.** Sign-off queue depth and human-minutes per landed step are computed indicators; a cap stops assigning new UNCONSTRAINED steps while the queue is full.

#### 4. Open forks (stated, not decided — the human owns them)

- **F1 — UNCONSTRAINED steps.** (a) run in a scrubbed environment inside a worktree snapshotted before start; or (b) the human states the accepted class of loss in that mode. Author's recommendation: (a).
- **F2 — gate set.** Are static analysis and secret scanning bound gates in the frozen meta-process, or project habits? Author's recommendation: bound.
- **F3 — subsystem or separate project** (st185). Open; the author has not recommended.

#### 5. Known vulnerabilities the author already concedes (from D0541)

V1. The UNCONSTRAINED step in a real checkout is exactly the surface behind the loudest destructive-action threads; "outputs are proposals" protects `main`, not the working tree, `.env`, or the developer's `gh` session.
V2. Human attention is unbudgeted until the queue cap (3.8) exists.
V3. Gates are only as strong as the tests bound; a regression with no bound test is green under keel exactly as under GitHub.
V4. 98 lines of prose working rules a runner cannot enforce = the D14 failure waiting to happen.
V5. The daemon itself is the one long-lived process; nothing says it reports its own RAM/disk.
V6. "The bash issue still seems insurmountable" (st183): the shell-writes-code class (D0309/D0491, two hook denies) is not addressed by this direction unless CONSTRAINED steps have no shell at all — which is not stated.

#### 6. What the author asks the panel

Attack C1–C5 (is the direction right?), §3 (does the protocol hold?), and the forks (is anything pre-resolved that should not be?). VULNERABLE is a welcome verdict; a manufactured objection is not. If a claim here is refuted by the repository, say where.

#### 0. The human's direction, verbatim (st181–st183, st185, st186, st188, st189; files `.tracking/intake/intake-2026-09-20.sysml`, `-21.sysml`, `-22.sysml`)

#### 1. The direction in one paragraph

keel is a work-tracking engine whose truth is SysML v2 text in git and whose state is computed (CLAUDE.md §1). Today the agent that does the work also decides what to work on, edits the tree, and records that the work is done; prose rules (CLAUDE.md §4, 98 lines, 83–180 blanks included), ten hook kinds and 77 guards (`keel version`) keep it honest after the fact. The direction: **keel assigns the step, materialises exactly its declared inputs into a disposable working copy, runs the agent there, harvests only its declared outputs, and runs the step's bound gate itself. The agent's own verdict is never an output.** Processes are chains of typed steps with declared inputs and outputs; the frozen meta-process that defines processes lives inside the signed keystone and is authored by human-signed CR only, never by a step. Two step classes: **CONSTRAINED** (scrubbed environment, no credential the agent can read, egress only to declared providers) and **UNCONSTRAINED** (full tool set, whose outputs are inputs to a CONSTRAINED or deterministic step and never the model). The human's surface for the runner subsystem is the browser; the runner-facing surface is the existing versioned JSON CLI surface (D0271) plus one new verb. **It is built in three slices, each with a stated trigger for the next** (§3); slice 1 has no daemon, no lease server and no proxy.

#### 2. Why this direction — claims, restated after round 1

C0. **This direction exercises a clause an accepted Decision reserved, and must say so.** D0209 (accepted 2026-08-24, `.engine/decisions/0209-adhere-not-invert.sysml:17-19`) says "do NOT invert the topology" and defers "an opt-in `keel run <process>` orchestrator … to last … only with its own code inside the signed keystone", while its rationale says "the inversion remains available as clause 5 if they later want prospective admission for a specific expensive-to-unwind transition" and "the human may override any clause". Its three findings stand and shape this direction: (1) the achievable win is deterministic *acceptance*, so slice 1 is a harvest-and-gate, not a behaviour controller; (2) the turn-boundary gate stays for UNCONSTRAINED steps; (3) the monitor-modifiable-by-monitored gap (issue236) is closed only if the meta-process is keystone-signed and no step output can touch it — stated as invariant I-11. The 2026-08-24 panel's alternative (process cursor, `keel advance`, D0435/D0436) was built and, per the sprint record, cited in sprints 662–663 and then in none of 664–759 except 748: the cursor was reported, not required (D0436 "reported unenforced"). **The Decision this direction needs is a `#SupersedeClause` on D0209's "deferred to last / opt-in" clause, HELD for the human as a process change.** Nothing else in D0209 is touched.

C1. **The defect is structural and counted; the inversion moves it into code, it does not abolish it.** Round 1's value critic classed 292 of the ledger's 667 issue titles by hand (`scratchpad/issue_classes.txt`, panel-computed, not re-measured by the author): 68 in the class the harvest removes outright (the agent recorded its own result: 27 before D0425, 41 in the eleven days since), 27 where the monitored actor modified its monitor (issue236, 276, 334, 488, 496, 650), 21 hook misfires, 8 records eaten by the shell (255, 256, 315, 320, 322, 543, 546, 641) — 129, 19% of the ledger. The premise critic's rebuttal is accepted: D0425's verifier/recorder ceremony *is* the direction in miniature and its defect moved rather than vanished — to the owed count typed by the primary, to inputs transcribed by hand, to a receipt not bound to its run (34 recorder/verifier issues, 13 corrective Decisions). So the claim is: **a boundary held by a working copy the agent cannot leave and a harvest of declared paths cannot be talked past; a boundary held by a prompt can.** The three moved defects each get a control in §3: the owed output set is computed from the step's declaration (never typed), inputs are materialised by keel from git objects (never transcribed), and the receipt is written by keel from the run it observed (never by the agent) — generalising today's write-path refusal of an AI `method=test` result with no `// RAN:` receipt (`WriteError::ReceiptOwed`, raised at `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:618`, message at `:88`). Falsifier for C1: a done step with no keel-written gate receipt is a red guard; if the guard is green and the ledger's self-attestation class keeps growing, C1 is false.

C2. **The design sits in an empty quadrant — and there are two hypotheses why.** `docs/reviews/agent-harness-landscape-2026-09-20.md:21-30` (94 rows): every tool that computes done outside the agent owns no persistent work item; every tool that owns the work item lets the agent (or a returned function) say when a step is done; four partial precedents (Gas Town's Refinery, Copilot coding agent, Dagger `check`, Dagster asset checks) put something automated between claim and status flip and none owns the item end to end. Materialise-declared-inputs / harvest-declared-outputs is the build-system norm (Bazel, REAPI) and absent from every agent harness; Argo materialises inputs and copies artifacts out but has no gate and no work item (`:545`). *Hypothesis A:* nobody has combined them. *Hypothesis B* (the review's own, `:576`): typed declared-I/O steps "lost developer mindshare to 'just write async code'" — nobody wants the authoring friction (D0054: friction is the #1 risk). The fact that distinguishes them is measurable after slice 1: the share of sprints run through `keel run` versus interactively, and the count of steps whose declared I/O had to be edited mid-sprint. If B holds, slice 2 is not built.

C3. **The pieces exist and converge; nothing is invented except the combination — and one thing is.** Lease with computed liveness: keel's own `Claim` (`.engine/schema/core/work.sysml:55-66`: "NO `status`, NO `expiresAt`, NO `released` FLAG … a claim is live if it is the newest claim on its item and its age against git-derived time is under the expiry window"); worktree-per-run: `members/keel-guards/src/history.rs:11-12` ("Each commit is checked out into a throwaway worktree and the real `validate` + `guard` code runs against it"); a launched, gate-judged run: `members/keel-serve/src/launcher.rs:1-18` (D0181/D0182: "dirty-tree refusal and snapshot at prepare, and the post-run gate + records at finish"; "Worktree-per-run stays deferred behind its named `DoR` (P5.6)") — built, and `.keel/runs`/`.tracking/runs` do not exist: never used. Outside keel: egress proxy holding the credential (six sandbox vendors, GitHub, GitLab); in-toto MATCH; version-pinned in-flight runs (Restate, Vercel, DBOS, Camunda). The invention is **renewing the job credential's validity with the lease so the final report never outlives it** — no tool in 122 threads does it (`runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:106`), and round 1 showed the naive form (rotating tokens) races the harvest; §3 states the computed form.

C4. **A third of the field's runner complaints are one defect this engine already refuses to have — with a caveat the ledger forces.** 35 of 122 threads (29%) are a stored flag standing in for a live fact (`runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:66-78`). keel's tree-side invariant is code (`Claim` above; `judgedAgainst` re-derivation; `suspect`). The caveat: keel's *runtime* facts are stored files and have had exactly theme 1's failures — a receipt file with one slot and two writers, a stale verdict read as the current one, a PID stub as liveness (issues 399, 468, 565, 569; D0493). So §3 does not claim the lease is "computed by construction"; it specifies the lease as **server-held state with an expiry, one writer, one reader, a bound test, non-authoritative, and every durable consequence landed as text** (§3.0).

C5. **What the human called best-in-class is kept, and two of it are unique in the field.** st182's sentence is quoted whole above. Kept unchanged: computed state, typed edges, the guard set (count unchanged, `keel version`), Decisions, knowledge records, and acceptance by the human's verbatim words through the console's own gesture receipt (D0192 option B / D0411 — already browser-native). The 2026-09-20 review found two things keel has that nobody else does (`:55`): verbatim-words acceptance, and done-as-a-passing-test on a persistent item. Also kept and extended: `judgedAgainst` (`.engine/schema/core/verification.sysml:56`) — a harvested step's receipt names the tree it was judged against, so a later change to its inputs or its gate makes it SUSPECT and re-derivable on any clone (D0101/D0307). REAPI's receipt is "trust-the-scheduler" (`2026-09-20.md:706`); this one is re-judgeable. Gap this fills: `ProcessStep.producedArtifact : String` (`.engine/schema/core/process.sysml:28`) is prose; typed I/O is the change.

C6. **What this direction does not touch.** Panel-computed (`issue_classes.txt`): 69 adoption/init/migrate/release issues, 32 parser/model, 42 suite/build/CI, and roughly 80 view/console/brief titles — over 40% of the ledger is keel-the-product and is untouched by any runner protocol. If F3 = separate project, it stays behind.

#### 3. The design, in slices (proposed, for approval)

##### 3.0 Invariants the design must hold (implementation-free; each with its violation test)

| # | Invariant | Class table | Violation test |
|---|---|---|---|
| I-1 | No agent works a step keel did not assign; every write to the tree names the run that produced it | C holds, U holds | a write with no live run id lands |
| I-2 | **No step is done except by a gate result keel computed; a step's declared outputs never intersect the read set of its gate, and never include a `TestResult`, gate result, acceptance, or any path under `.tracking/`/`.engine/` that a verdict is read from** — declared at definition time, refused at definition time | C holds, U holds | a step declaring `.tracking/**` as output is accepted; a harvested pass file is written rather than quarantined |
| I-3 | An agent reads nothing not declared as an input | C holds, U relaxed (full checkout is the declared input) | working copy holds a path outside the input set |
| I-4 | Nothing an agent produced reaches the model except declared outputs, via harvest; an UNCONSTRAINED step's outputs are inputs to a CONSTRAINED or deterministic step, never the model | C holds, U holds (as I-4') | a write to `.tracking`/`.engine` from inside a running step succeeds |
| I-5 | No credential the agent can read outlives the step; CONSTRAINED reads none | C holds, U relaxed — **the residual loss class is stated in F1** | a run credential is accepted after its lease's computed expiry |
| I-6 | "Running" is never a stored fact in the tree; a step runs iff a live lease exists; at most one holder | both | kill the runner, pass the lease: step not ready again; two acquires both succeed |
| I-7 | No human-judgment record originates from a runner, run token or session token; human judgment enters only through the browser session's own identity (D0411 gesture receipt) | both | `accept` under a run token succeeds |
| I-8 | Every verdict is re-derivable from committed truth alone; the server store is ephemeral and non-authoritative | both | drop the server store; a step's done state cannot be recomputed from the tree |
| I-9 | No dead runner, proxy or heartbeat passes silently | both | an unclaimed ready step past its declared interval raises nothing |
| I-10 | keel's own assignment never grows the set of *unjudged proposals* past a declared bound — an AI-side WIP limit on the work, no metric of the human (D0204 cl.2) | both | bound reached, ready step still leasable |
| I-11 | Process definitions live inside the signed keystone; no step output and no runner write can add or alter a process; an unbound runnable step is refused at definition, not reported | both | a process file arrives by harvest; a runnable step with no `checkedBy` validates |

**Truth partition (I-8):** *committed as text* — the step definition, the claim, the run receipt (input digests, output digests, worker id, timestamps, model id, `judgedAgainst`), the gate result, every refusal (claim refused, write after expiry, harvest under a lapsed lease); *server-only, ephemeral* — the lease, the run credential's validity window, cadence, the runner roster. A server crash loses leases; the steps become ready again and every landed fact survives.

**Hazard analysis owed before the Decision:** the direction adds controllers (server/scheduler, runner, proxy) and control actions (`assign`, `acquire`, heartbeat reply incl. `cancel`/`terminate`, `harvest`, `retire`) that `.tracking/architecture/engine-control-structure.sysml` does not carry, and a loss no EL covers (an agent acts on the host or a credential outside the model). The `stpa-self` run over the new structure — EL5, hazards per control action, UCA candidates at least for `terminate` PROVIDED mid-write, `assign` PROVIDED with an input set that does not close, `harvest` PROVIDED from a superseded runner version — is the first deliverable of slice 1's CR, not this document.

##### 3.1 Slice 1 — `keel run <process>.<step>`: in-process, no daemon, no lease server, no proxy

The slice that pays first (round 1's value critic: 24 of 60 sprint-700-series retros record a receipt, ladder or dispatch failure; ladder re-runs of 866–1070 s each). Contents:

1. **Schema CR (the load-bearing change):** `ProcessStep` gains `inputs[*]` and `outputs[*]` (item names or repo paths) and a `runnable` marker that makes `checkedBy` mandatory; guard `runnable-step-has-check`; I-2's output exclusion checked at `validate`. Today 13 of 191 steps carry `checkedBy` (`grep -c` over `.engine/processes/`); "done when the gate is green" is unrepresentable for the other 178, daemon or no daemon.
2. **Materialise:** `keel claim` the step (existing, computed liveness); create a per-run working copy **by `git clone --shared` or `--separate-git-dir` into a path carrying the run id — never a linked worktree** (issue249: a linked worktree shares `.git/config` and `.git/hooks`; `git config core.hooksPath` inside one disarmed the parent's commit gate; issue491, issue577); check out from git objects with `core.autocrlf=false` so digests are over blob content (issue478, guard `working-tree-eol`); throwaway `HOME`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, no `gh` session, no `.env`. This is the launcher's deferred P5.6 finished.
3. **Run:** spawn the agent as a Windows Job Object (Linux: process group) — `serve.rs:1222-1245` already documents why `kill_on_drop` alone orphans `claude.exe`; wall-clock deadline declared on the step type; on deadline: kill the job, harvest as `failed` with outputs retained as evidence, then reap. A refusal returned to the agent never carries an instruction to write (issue578).
4. **Harvest:** `git diff --name-only` of the working copy against its base; **any changed path outside the declared outputs fails the step** (in-toto MATCH in both directions — unexpected products are a violation); declared outputs are read as blobs, never as host-written files; the receipt (input digests, output digests, exit, timestamps, provider-returned model id — answers open issue498 — `judgedAgainst`) is written by keel.
5. **Gate:** keel runs the step's `checkedBy` over the harvested outputs and writes the one `TestResult`; an AI-examined method lands `proposed` (D0312 B) and routes the step to `awaiting-human`, never to done.
6. **Parallel:** N concurrent `keel run` invocations, each its own clone and claim; per-actor write targets already make concurrent writes non-colliding (`srDcPerActorWriteTargets`). The single-writer pin in D0182 is lifted by the clone.
7. **Retirement table (what slice 1 makes redundant, per hook kind and ceremony):**

| Today | Under slice 1, CONSTRAINED steps | UNCONSTRAINED steps |
|---|---|---|
| `pre-bash` denies (heredoc-backslash, unfed `cat`) | moot — the agent's shell cannot reach a governance record | stays |
| `pre-write` fact-surface protection | moot — `.tracking`/`.engine` are not declared outputs | stays |
| `post-edit` fast tier, `stop` validate+guard | replaced by the gate keel runs over the harvest | stays |
| D0425 verifier/recorder ceremony (two agent types, two skills, `check_receipt.py`, `check_report.py`, `verify_receipt.py`; 13 corrective Decisions) | retired — keel writes the receipt and the results | retired — the harvest is the receipt |
| `keel advance` process cursor | becomes the assignment order | same |
| guards (77) | unchanged; run by the gate | unchanged |
| CLAUDE.md §4 prose | Writes/Subagents/Git blocks (~60 of 98 lines) become moot; the rest is a tracked conversion list | stays |

**Trigger to build slice 2:** a second machine or a second runner process is enrolled, or a step's declared inputs cannot be materialised on this host.

##### 3.2 Slice 2 — the runner protocol (daemons claim leased steps; st186 "(ii) where feasible")

Each clause is tagged **[server]** (enforced by the server) or **[runner]** (asked of the runner, with the server check that detects its violation).

1. **Identity.** [server] Enrolment takes an organisation API key or a federated identity; a personal subscription OAuth token is refused (the human's enterprise seat is eligible — the credential the human holds today is stated in the CR before this clause is approved). `enrol` negotiates protocol version first (`low/high`, structured refusal) and yields a per-process session token. [server] A run credential is **a fixed identifier minted at acquire; its validity is computed from the lease record** (last accepted `received_at` + `lease_seconds` + declared harvest grace) — never rotated, so there is no renewal-vs-harvest race, and the final report is valid exactly as long as the lease says. [server] No run, session or runner credential can author a human-judgment record (I-7).
2. **Claim.** [server] `reserve` → `acquire` is an atomic checkout with a coded conflict; readiness recomputed at acquire; every write carries the run id; every claim attempt and refusal is a committed record the human reads in the browser.
3. **Lease.** [server] The claim response carries `lease_seconds` and `heartbeat_seconds`, server-chosen, `heartbeat_seconds ≤ lease_seconds / 3` held as an invariant, revisable on any heartbeat reply **effective from the next accepted heartbeat, never retroactively**; measured on server monotonic time. [runner] The heartbeat runs in its own process, hits the cheapest endpoint, echoes `sent_at`/`received_at`, carries progress; its reply may carry `cancel`, `terminate`, `retire_at`, cadence. [runner] **The runner keeps a local deadline (last accepted `received_at` + `lease_seconds` − echoed skew) and treats an unanswered deadline, a rejected heartbeat, or a wake from standby as `expired`: kill the job, harvest nothing, reap** — the server-side check: any write or harvest after computed expiry is a *recorded refusal* (fencing), so the kill is a spend courtesy and the fence is the control. Maximum lease and wall-clock deadline are declared on the step type.
4. **Progress and states.** [server] Progress is runner-observed monotonic counters declared per step type (accepted provider calls, bytes written under declared output paths, child exits); `stalled` = none advanced for N intervals; `expired` and `stalled` are distinct. [server] **The token/cost ceiling is mandatory per step type** and is the control for the looping-under-a-live-heartbeat case (D19) — `stalled` is not. [server] `awaiting-human` releases the lease, supersedes the run credential (a state transition is a fencing event), reaps the sandbox; the human's answer is a declared input to the next run of the same step. [server] Poison-pill counting is by cause (`expired` / `startup-failed` / `failed`); startup failures never count against the step; N expiries by one runner in a window poison the runner. An unclaimed ready step past a declared interval is a fault.
5. **Harvest.** [runner] The runner uploads declared outputs by digest under the run credential, then sends one `complete`. [server] The server verifies digests against the declared output set, fences a lapsed or superseded credential, writes completion and receipt in one write; "pull" is the server reading its own store — no inbound connection to a runner. Reaping happens on `complete`-ack or lease expiry + harvest grace, whichever is later, so an at-deadline exit still has a harvest window.
6. **Retire.** [server] Per runner version: `current / ramping / draining / drained`, in-flight runs pinned to their definition version (K12); an idle runner is told why. [runner] No self-update — detected by version negotiation at enrol.
7. **Liveness of the subsystem itself.** [server] One process hosts server, lease and (slice 3) proxy, under a named supervisor on this host (Windows Service or Task Scheduler); the heartbeat carries the daemon's own resource facts; **surface availability and queue visibility are computed indicators with a fault** (issue096, 150, 152: three console outages the human noticed first). A DOM-rung test is bound before the browser is the only human surface.

**Trigger to build slice 3:** a CONSTRAINED step's provider credential must leave this host, or the human states a hostile-agent threat model.

##### 3.3 Slice 3 — the egress proxy

[server] The proxy holds the provider credential; per step type it enforces a **body policy** — allowed `model` refused pre-flight, provider server-side network tools (web fetch/search, code-execution containers with egress) denied by default — and the denial names the field as it names a host. It meters the provider's `usage` fields (input, output, cache read, cache write) against a pricing table on the step type; a stream aborted before `usage` arrives is charged its `max_tokens`; a request whose `max_tokens` would overrun the remaining budget is refused pre-flight; async/batch operations are outside the CONSTRAINED operation set unless declared with a metered retrieval. It checks the run credential's computed validity on every forwarded call, so a partitioned runner's agent is starved regardless of the runner's health. Stated plainly: input content reaching the *declared* provider is the intended flow; the provider's data terms are the trust boundary; the proxy prevents undeclared hosts and undeclared operations, not the declared provider seeing the prompt.

#### 4. Open forks (stated, not decided — the human owns them)

- **F0 — D0209.** `#SupersedeClause` on D0209's "deferred to last / opt-in" clause so slice 1 may be chartered under D0209's own conditions (own code inside the signed keystone; the acceptance audit and freeze stay). Author's recommendation: supersede the clause; the rest of D0209 stands and this direction is built on it. **Held as a process change.**
- **F1 — UNCONSTRAINED steps.** Slice 1 runs them in the same scrubbed clone with the full tool set, and the whole environment is diffed after exit. The **residual loss class stated:** an UNCONSTRAINED agent can spend, can call any host its tools reach, and can act on anything the scrubbed environment still exposes (the network; the provider's own tools). The human either accepts that class for this mode or restricts UNCONSTRAINED to slice 3's proxy. Recommendation: accept for slice 1, revisit at slice 3's trigger.
- **F2 — gate set.** Are static analysis and secret scanning bound `checkedBy` predicates in the frozen meta-process, or project habits? Recommendation: bound (D5: green review, green CI, attacker got Jira).
- **F3 — subsystem or separate project** (st185). Slice 1 is a subsystem by construction (it is `keel run` plus a schema CR in this repo). The question becomes live at slice 2. Not recommended either way yet; the cost stated: a separate project leaves the 40% of the ledger in C6 behind.

#### 5. Vulnerabilities the author concedes

V1. Under F1's recommendation an UNCONSTRAINED step can still spend and reach the network; "outputs are proposals" plus the full-environment diff protects the tree and the developer's global config, not the bill.
V2. I-10 is a WIP limit on unjudged proposals; nothing here shortens the human's queue, and nothing may (D0204).
V3. Gates are only the predicates someone bound (F2).
V4. CLAUDE.md §4: ~60 of 98 lines become moot under slice 1 for CONSTRAINED steps; the remainder is a tracked conversion list, and every UNCONSTRAINED step keeps all 98.
V5. Cost on this host: a per-step clone plus a `--touched` gate is minutes per step; the measured floor (one `keel run` end to end on this machine) is stated in the CR before slice 1 is approved; a shared build cache is a named component if the floor is over the human's cadence.
V6. The shell class splits: records eaten by the shell (8 issues) vanish under harvest because the agent never invokes `keel record`; shell-writes-code (5 issues) stays in UNCONSTRAINED steps.
V7. Hypothesis B in C2 (nobody wants the authoring friction) is live until slice 1 is measured.

| PESSIMIST-2 5 (wall-clock per step) | V5: measured floor before approval; `--touched` scoped by declared inputs |
| PESSIMIST-2 6 (tenth layer) | §3.1.7 retirement table |
| PESSIMIST-2 8 (enrolment credential) | §3.2.1: eligibility stated in the CR before approval |
| PEDANT-1 1–12 (citations) | all corrected in place: D14 → 2026-09-21 review; 77 guards; issues 084/085 for "ignored"; write.rs:618 for the refusal; §0 header and glob; st181 truncation marked, st188 double space, st182 whole; "94 rows"; 67/45/9 +1 conditional; "35 of 122 (29%)"; Buildkite moved to the lease list; 98 lines (83–180); C2's four partial precedents |

**Refuted, with evidence:** none. Every round-1 finding is resolved in substance above; where a panelist's number was not re-measured it is attributed, not adopted.

## Appendix D3 — direction artifact, revision 3, verbatim

### Direction under review: keel owns the AI task — revision 3 (2026-09-22, after panel round 2)

**Status: a direction, not a Decision.** Nothing is built; no spec file exists; the design is unapproved (brainstorm HARD-GATE). Repository for verification: `C:\Users\WilliamWeatherholtz\claude_code\sysmlv2-ai-toolkit`, HEAD `f365cb7a`. Round 1: 2 ACCEPT / 7 OBJECT (3 BLOCKER, 47 MAJOR, 33 MINOR). Round 2: 0 ACCEPT / 9 OBJECT (0 BLOCKER, 26 MAJOR, 31 MINOR) — every round-1 flip set was accepted as resolved or renamed; the round-2 objections converge on six defects revision 2 introduced, all in slice 1. §R2 is the map. Every panelist number the author did not re-measure is attributed to the panelist and its file.

#### 0. The human's direction, verbatim (st181–st183, st185, st186, st188, st189; `.tracking/intake/intake-2026-09-20.sysml`, `-21.sysml`, `-22.sysml`)

- st181: "Let's make a branch of keel where the tool owns AI tasks instead of the reverse. We mentioned this earlier, a-la Jira, Linear, etc. I want to use the great stuff we've made so far, just with absolute deterministic control as a starting point. And strong customizable processes with clear inputs, outputs, hooks, and definition\n\nLet's br" [record ends mid-word]
- st182: "C, but I'm interested in going fully browser UI, I think and effectively ditching the cli. It's brought more issues than solutions (how many EOL bugs again?).\n\nAnyway, we've developed some truly excellent ideas, some of which I feel are best-in-class for my work. And I don't want to lose that, but I do want a full client-server architecture here that gives AI agents much more defined and narrow scope which can run in parallel and have more definite inputs and outputs. And keel can still detect discrepancies and lapsed guards, etc.\n\nI envision it working thusly - a process is defined which develop processes. That process is to define inputs, outputs, and steps. At the start/end of a step, what goes into or out of keel? What goes into or out of an agent? Then chain together steps ad nauseum until the process is defined. And a process can be made to use other processes nested-like. Is that defensible? Is it free from critique?" — (EOL issues in the ledger: three of 667 — 076, 351, 478 — and one sprint, 560; the direction does not rest on them, see C1)
- st183: "Let's just do both. A constrained container option and and an unconstrained agent option. Or is that not a good idea?\n\nIf not, I do think we do it. Agents can help make and test viable tool chains without too much difficulty, I expect. Though the bash issue still seems insurmountable"
- st185: "Yes I meant a keel subsystem/mode, though I might make it a totally separate project because I feel like it's quite distinct architecturally."
- st186: "I think (ii) is right where feasible." — (ii) = runner daemons claim leased steps, versus (i) the server spawns agents (`docs/reviews/agent-harness-landscape-2026-09-20.md:124`).
- st188: "browser only for the user, runner-facing (i.e. CLI, yes?) protocol is fine too.  as far as what the runner daemon actually is, etc., do some more research for best practices with existing frameworks, lookup complaints from 50 threads in forums to see user-identified weaknesses of their approach, and let's set ourselves apart."
- st189: "critique this direction with a panel, make an artifact, and convince me this is the right direction."

#### 1. The direction in one paragraph

keel is a work-tracking engine whose truth is SysML v2 text in git and whose state is computed (CLAUDE.md §1). Today the agent that does the work also decides what to work on, edits the tree, and records that the work is done; prose rules (CLAUDE.md §4, 98 lines, 83–180 blanks included), eight hook kinds and 77 guards (`keel version`) keep it honest after the fact. The direction: **keel assigns the step, materialises it into a disposable working copy the agent cannot write past, runs the agent there, harvests only the step's declared outputs — replaying any governance output through the write API so every write-time refusal fires — and runs the step's bound gate itself in a checkout the agent never touched. The agent's own verdict is never an output.** Processes are chains of typed steps with declared inputs and outputs; the frozen meta-process that defines processes lives inside the signed keystone (the D0070 hard lock: a process-definition change needs a human-held marked Decision) and no step output can touch it. **Slice 1 has one step class — BOUNDED**: a full clone with no remote, a scrubbed user profile, a Windows Job Object, a wall-clock deadline, the harvest and the keel-run gate; the agent holds a run-scoped provider credential it can read, and egress is open. The CONSTRAINED class st183 asked for (container, no readable credential, egress only to declared providers through a proxy) arrives in slice 3, and UNCONSTRAINED is then the BOUNDED class by another name. The human's surface for the runner subsystem is the browser, with the D0192-A chat channel kept as the surface-down fallback; the runner-facing surface is the existing versioned JSON CLI surface (D0271) plus one verb. Built in three slices with observable triggers (§3); slice 1 has no daemon, no lease server and no proxy.

#### 2. Why this direction — claims, restated after two rounds

C0. **This direction exercises a clause an accepted Decision reserved, and three other accepted clauses stand in its way; all four are named and held for the human.** D0209 (accepted 2026-08-24 under standing consent, `.engine/decisions/0209-adhere-not-invert.sysml`; `decision` at `:19`, rationale `:20`) says "do NOT invert the topology" and, clause 5 whole: "An opt-in keel run <process> orchestrator is DEFERRED to last, adopted only for transitions where a landed-then-detected bad state is expensive to unwind, only alongside library mode, and only with its own code inside the signed keystone." Its rationale: "the inversion remains available as clause 5 if they later want prospective admission for a specific expensive-to-unwind transition" and "the human may override any clause". Its three findings shape this direction: deterministic *acceptance* is the achievable win (slice 1 is a harvest-and-gate, not a behaviour controller); the turn-boundary gate stays for interactive work; the monitor-modifiable-by-monitored gap (issue236) is closed by the keystone lock and I-11. The alternative the 2026-08-24 panel preferred — the process cursor, `keel advance` (D0435/D0436) — was built and is cited in sprints 661–663 and 687; sprint 748's mention is a file move; D0436's own words are "a process with no bound step is reported unenforceable-by-step, never refused". **Fork F0** is a `#SupersedeClause` on D0209 clause 5 *whole* (D0398: one clause, reversed whole), with this direction re-adopting one of its four conditions by its own choice — the keystone condition, as I-11 — and dropping "deferred to last", "only expensive-to-unwind transitions" and "alongside library mode" (a term defined nowhere in the tree outside D0209). Three more accepted clauses this direction reverses, each its own held Decision (D0306: one clause per Decision; D0303: layered with `#DependsOn`): **F0b** D0182 "launcher concurrency pins to 1 with single-writer-per-tree during a run" — lifted for the *run* by the clone, kept for the *gate* (§3.1.5); **F0c** D0204 clause 2 is *not* reversed — I-10 is redrawn so it needs no reversal (§3.0); **F0d** D0192 option A is *not* reversed — I-7 keeps the chat channel (§3.0). So the held set is F0 and F0b; F0c and F0d are stated so the panel can verify nothing else moved.

C1. **The defect is structural and counted; the inversion moves it into code, it does not abolish it — and the invoker is named.** Round 1's value critic classed 292 of the ledger's 667 issue titles (`scratchpad/issue_classes.txt`; panel-computed, re-summed by the author from that file): 68 where the agent recorded its own result (27 before D0425, 41 in the eleven days since), 27 where the monitored actor modified its monitor (236, 276, 334, 650 among them), 21 hook misfires, 8 records eaten by the shell (255, 256, 315, 320, 322, 543, 546, 641), 5 sandbox/scratch-environment leaks (477, 539, 570, 577, 598) — 129 of 667, 19%. D0425's verifier/recorder ceremony *is* the direction in miniature and its defect moved rather than vanished: to the owed count typed by the primary, to inputs transcribed by hand, to a receipt not bound to its run, and — round 2's addition — to the *dispatcher*, who remained a full-tool actor choosing whether to dispatch at all (34 recorder/verifier issues, 13 corrective Decisions). So the claim is: **a boundary held by a working copy the agent cannot write past and a harvest of declared paths cannot be talked past; a boundary held by a prompt can.** The four moved defects each get a control that is code: the owed output set is computed from the step's declaration; inputs are materialised by keel from git objects; the receipt is written by keel from the run it observed (generalising `WriteError::ReceiptOwed`, raised at `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:618`, message `:88`); and **the only path to done for a runnable step is `keel run`** — a `TestResult` against a runnable step's `checkedBy` is refused at the write unless stamped by a run id (§3.1.5), so whoever invokes `keel run` cannot substitute `keel record result`. Falsifier for C1: the count of runnable-step results not stamped by a run; if it grows, C1 is false.

C2. **The design sits in an empty quadrant — and there are two hypotheses why.** `docs/reviews/agent-harness-landscape-2026-09-20.md:21-30` (94 rows): every tool that computes done outside the agent owns no persistent work item; every tool that owns the item lets the agent (or a returned function) say when a step is done; four partial precedents (Gas Town's Refinery, Copilot coding agent, Dagger `check`, Dagster asset checks) put something automated between claim and status flip and "None of the four owns the work item end to end." Materialise-declared-inputs / harvest-declared-outputs is the build-system norm (Bazel, REAPI) and absent from every agent harness; Argo materialises inputs and copies artifacts out with no gate and no work item (`:545`). *Hypothesis A:* nobody has combined them. *Hypothesis B* (`:576`): typed declared-I/O steps "lost developer mindshare to 'just write async code'" — nobody wants the authoring friction (D0054). The launcher's history is evidence for B: a gate-judged run existed since 2026-08-21 (D0181/D0182) and `.keel/runs`/`.tracking/runs` never existed (`git log --all -- .tracking/runs` is empty). The fact that distinguishes A from B is measured after slice 1: the share of sprints run through `keel run` versus interactively, and the count of steps whose declared I/O had to be edited mid-sprint. If B holds, slice 2 is not built.

C3. **The pieces exist and converge; nothing is invented except the combination — and one thing is.** Computed-liveness claim: `Claim` (`.engine/schema/core/work.sysml:55-67`: "NO `status`, NO `expiresAt`, NO `released` FLAG … Liveness is COMPUTED"; the code's holder rule is earliest un-expired, `members/keel-guards/src/acceptance.rs:627`; window `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS: i64 = 2`, `members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:15`). Throwaway checkout for a real gate: `members/keel-guards/src/history.rs:11-12`. A launched, gate-judged run: `members/keel-serve/src/launcher.rs:1-18` (D0181/D0182; "Worktree-per-run stays deferred behind its named `DoR` (P5.6)"). Outside keel: egress proxy holding the credential (six sandbox vendors, GitHub, GitLab); in-toto MATCH; version-pinned in-flight runs (Restate, Vercel, DBOS, Camunda). The invention: **a run credential that is a fixed run id whose validity is computed from that run's lease record** — the final report never outlives its credential (no tool in 122 threads does it, `runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:106`) and, being computed not rotated, it cannot race the harvest.

C4. **A third of the field's runner complaints are one defect — with the caveat the ledger forces.** 35 of 122 threads (29%) are a stored flag standing in for a live fact (`runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:66-79`). keel's tree-side invariant is code. keel's *runtime* facts are stored files and have had exactly theme 1's failures (issues 399, 468, 565, 569; D0493), and revision 2 re-created one: a run leased by an unchanged `keel claim` would hold a dead step for two days. §3.1.2 gives the run claim a window computed from the step's deadline, and §3.0 specifies the slice-2 lease as ephemeral server state with fencing as the control.

C5. **What the human called best-in-class is kept, and two of it are unique in the field.** Kept unchanged: computed state, typed edges, the 77 guards, Decisions, knowledge records, and acceptance by the human's verbatim words — through the console's gesture receipt (D0411) *or* the chat channel the human chose (D0192: "option A is fine. this hasn't been a real issue to date"). The 2026-09-20 review found two things keel has that nobody else does (`:55`): verbatim-words acceptance and done-as-a-passing-test on a persistent item. Extended: `judgedAgainst` (`.engine/schema/core/verification.sysml:56`) on the run receipt makes a harvested step SUSPECT and re-derivable when its inputs or gate change (D0101/D0307); REAPI's receipt is "trust-the-scheduler" (`2026-09-20.md:706`). Gap filled: `ProcessStep.producedArtifact : String` (`process.sysml:28`) is prose; typed I/O is the change.

C6. **What this direction does not touch.** From the same classification: 69 adoption/init/migrate/release, 32 parser/model, 42 suite/build/CI issues, plus about 80 unclassed view/console/brief titles — 223 of 667, about a third, is keel-the-product and untouched by any runner protocol. If F3 = separate project, it stays behind.

#### 3. The design, in slices (proposed, for approval)

##### 3.0 Invariants (implementation-free; violation test; the slice that first binds each)

| # | Invariant | Slice 1 (BOUNDED) | Slice 3 (CONSTRAINED) | Violation test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I-1 | No agent works a step keel did not assign; every write to the tree names the run that produced it — including the invoker's | 1 | — | a `TestResult` against a runnable step's `checkedBy` lands with no run id |
| I-2 | **No step is done except by a gate keel computed in a checkout the agent never wrote.** Declared outputs are the gate's *subject*, never its *evidence*: they may not include a `TestResult`, gate result, acceptance, receipt, `.keel/**`, `target/**`, or any process definition. Governance outputs under `.tracking/` are typed proposals replayed through `keel record` at harvest, never blob-copied | 1 | — | a step declaring an evidence path validates; a harvested blob adding `: TestResult`, `outcome =`, an acceptance `Test` or a `DecisionStatus` change is written rather than quarantined; a gate reads a receipt the agent could write |
| I-3 | An agent reads nothing not declared as an input | relaxed (full clone) | 3 (`git archive` of declared paths) | working copy holds a path outside the input set |
| I-4 | Nothing an agent produced reaches the model except declared outputs, via harvest | 1 | — | a change outside the declared set is harvested rather than failing the step |
| I-5 | No credential the agent can read outlives the step | relaxed: run-scoped, readable, revoked at reap; spend uncontrolled | 3 (proxy holds it) | a run credential is accepted after its window |
| I-6 | "Running" is never a stored fact in the tree; a step runs iff a live run exists; at most one holder | 1 (run claim window = deadline + grace; dead-run reaper) | — | kill `keel run`, pass the window: step not ready again |
| I-7 | No human-judgment record originates from a runner, run id or session credential; human judgment enters by the human's own identity — console gesture receipt (D0411) or the D0192-A chat channel with its D0423 WARN semantics | 1 | — | `accept` under a run id succeeds |
| I-8 | Every verdict is re-derivable from committed truth alone; the server store is ephemeral | 1 (trivially: no server) | — | drop the store; a step's done state cannot be recomputed |
| I-9 | No dead run, runner, proxy or heartbeat passes silently | 1 (a run claim past its window with no receipt is a fault) | — | a dead run raises nothing |
| I-10 | keel's own concurrency is bounded — a declared maximum of concurrent live runs (a fact about the work); the depth of unjudged `proposed` results is a computed indicator with no threshold (D0088) | 1 | — | bound reached, a new run starts |
| I-11 | Process definitions live inside the keystone; no step output and no runner write can add or alter a process; `runnable` is *computed* (inputs, outputs and `checkedBy` all present) and `keel run` refuses to run a step that is not runnable — `validate` reports, never refuses (D0436, D0098) | 1 | — | a process file arrives by harvest; `keel run` runs a step with no `checkedBy` |

**Truth partition (I-8).** *Committed as text:* the step definition; the run claim; the run receipt (base tree id, input digests, output digests, worker/actor, timestamps, provider-returned model id, metered usage, `judgedAgainst`); the gate result; every refusal (claim refused, write after expiry, harvest under a lapsed window, quarantined harvest — the last as an Issue with the evidence); the runner's identity as an `Actor`. *Server-only, ephemeral (slice 2):* the lease, cadence, the runner roster, rollout state, poison counts — a crash forgets a `draining` runner and un-poisons one; accepted, and stated.

**Hazard analysis, per slice.** `stpa-currency` already asks that every computed control action be walked by a recorded `stpa-self` run (`.engine/docs/guards.md:113`, warning-tier). Slice 1 adds one controller (`keel run`, in the invoker's process) and the agent in the clone, with actions materialise / spawn / kill-on-deadline / harvest / replay / gate / quarantine, and a loss no EL covers (an agent acts on the host or a credential outside the model — EL5). The slice-1 run names UCA candidates at least for `kill-on-deadline` PROVIDED mid-write and `materialise` PROVIDED with an input set that does not close; it is a `verify`-linked DoD Test of the CR's sprint, after the schema CR that creates the structure. Slice 2's run (server, runner; `acquire`, heartbeat reply, `harvest` from a superseded runner version, `retire`) is bound the same way when slice 2 is chartered.

##### 3.1 Slice 1 — `keel run <process>.<step>`: in-process, one step class, no daemon, no lease server, no proxy

**Who invokes it.** `keel serve` (D0181: the console is the launch boundary) or the human at a terminal; never the agent harness's own shell tool (issue453: a ten-minute cap that kills the process tree). The run detaches from its invoker; the invoker's identity is stamped on the run.

1. **Schema CR (the load-bearing change).** `ProcessStep` gains `inputs[*]` and `outputs[*]` (item names or repo paths); `runnable` is computed; I-2's evidence exclusion is checked at `validate` (reports) and at `keel run` (refuses). Today 13 of 191 steps carry `checkedBy`; "done when the gate is green" is unrepresentable for the other 178.
2. **Claim.** `keel claim` the step; **the run claim's window is computed in `claims.rs` from the step type's declared wall-clock deadline plus harvest grace** (a computation change; no field on `Claim`, `schema/core` untouched), so a dead run frees its step in minutes, not `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS`. A run whose `keel run` pid is dead (pid + start time, the D0493 stub shape, *with* a bound test because C4 lists that shape among theme-1 failures) is reaped and its claim past its window with no receipt is a fault (I-9).
3. **Materialise.** A **full clone with `origin` removed** into a path carrying the run id — never a linked worktree (issue249: "A linked worktree SHARES the common .git/config … disarms the real commit gate"; issue491, issue577) and never `--shared` (its `alternates` exposes the parent's unreferenced objects, its `origin` is the parent path so `git push` writes into the developer's `.git`, and git-clone(1) warns the parent's own `git maintenance` can corrupt it; the whole store is 18 MiB packed + 60 MiB loose, so a full copy is seconds). Local config sets `core.autocrlf=false` (system scope carries `true`; local wins), so digests are over blob content (issue478, guard `working-tree-eol`). **The tracked hook configuration (`.claude/settings.json`, whose eight hooks fire in any clone) is removed from the clone**; the agent inside a run needs no turn gate — the harvest is the gate. **Profile scrub, Windows-correct:** `USERPROFILE`, `HOMEDRIVE`/`HOMEPATH`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `CARGO_HOME`, `RUSTUP_HOME`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` all point into a throwaway per-run profile (Node and keel resolve the home from `USERPROFILE`, `members/keel-process/src/library.rs:32`; a `HOME`-only scrub is a no-op on this host). The run's provider credential is placed in that profile, scoped to the run, revoked at reap (I-5 relaxed, stated). **Stated residual (F1):** the agent is the same OS user; the parent repository and every other repository on disk are writable by path whatever the environment says. The honest boundary is a separate low-privilege OS user with the parent read-only by ACL — offered as the slice-1 option the human may choose over the scrub (F1).
4. **Run.** Spawn the agent under a **Windows Job Object** (kill-on-close; bounds Windows-native processes only — `serve.rs:1223-1246` documents the `cmd → claude.exe` orphan it fixes). Linux toolchains the step needs run inside a container labelled `keel.run=<id>`, never bare `wsl.exe`; the kill path ends with `docker kill` by label, and leftover containers or volumes by label are part of the environment diff. Wall-clock deadline declared on the step type; at the deadline: kill, harvest as `failed` with outputs retained as evidence, reap. A refusal returned to the agent never carries an instruction to write (issue578).
5. **Harvest and gate.** `git diff --name-only` of the clone against its recorded base tree; **any changed path outside the declared outputs fails the step** (in-toto MATCH both directions). Declared non-governance outputs are read as blobs and committed by keel to `refs/keel/runs/<id>`; **declared governance outputs (`.tracking/`) are typed proposals in the write API's own input form and are replayed through `keel record` at harvest, so `ReceiptOwed`, provenance-never-defaulted and the AI-actor acceptance refusal all fire** (`validate` alone accepted six hand-written `TestResult`s with non-hex ids — `.tracking/issues-claudeOpus5.sysml:1837`). **The gate runs in a keel-owned checkout of that ref — never the agent's clone — with the run's `judgedAgainst` pinning the `checkedBy` version and `KEEL_NO_RECEIPT=1` so no cached green (D0371/D0369/D0474) is read.** Where the gate is the ladder (`suite --touched` scoped by declared inputs), a shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` is a named slice-1 component and gate parallelism is 1 (D0182's pin, kept for the gate — F0b lifts it for the run only). keel writes the one `TestResult`, stamped with the run id; an AI-examined method lands `proposed` (D0312 B) and the step goes to `awaiting-human`, never done; `keel judge-set` or an answer-as-input to a re-run leaves that state. Merge to trunk is by a gate-green step or the human. The receipt carries the provider-returned model id (open issue498). The out-of-tree profile diff is recorded as an indicator (D0088), not a gate.
6. **Parallel.** N concurrent `keel run` invocations, each its own clone and run claim; per-actor write targets keep concurrent writes non-colliding (`srDcPerActorWriteTargets`); I-10's concurrency bound is declared in `activation.toml`.
7. **Retirement table — for steps run through `keel run`; an interactive session keeps every row.** The share of sprints run through `keel run` is C2's measured fact.

| Today (eight hook kinds; ceremony) | Steps run through `keel run` (slice 1) |
|---|---|
| `pre-bash` denies, `pre-write` fact-surface protection, `post-edit` fast tier, `stop` and `subagent-stop` gates, `subagent-start`, `user-prompt`, `config-change` | not present in the clone — the harvest, the replay and the keel-run gate are the control |
| D0425 verifier/recorder ceremony (two agent types, two skills, `check_receipt.py`, `check_report.py`, `verify_receipt.py`; 13 corrective Decisions) | retired — keel writes the receipt and the result |
| `keel advance` process cursor | the assignment order |
| guards (77) | unchanged; run by the gate |
| CLAUDE.md §4 prose | Writes/Subagents/Git blocks (64 lines, 60 non-blank) become moot inside a run; the rest is a tracked conversion list |

**Measured before approval (V5):** one `keel run` end to end on this host — clone, spawn, harvest, gate — with the floor in seconds in the CR.

**Trigger to build slice 2 (observables):** a recorded Statement asking for a run on a host that does not hold the working tree, or for unattended launch with no human at the console; or a recorded `keel run` refusal because a declared input cannot be materialised on this host.

##### 3.2 Slice 2 — the runner protocol (daemons claim leased steps; st186 "(ii) where feasible")

Clauses tagged **[server]** (enforced by the server) or **[runner]** (asked of the runner, with the server check that detects its violation).

1. **Identity.** [server] Enrolment takes an organisation API key or a federated identity; a personal subscription OAuth token is refused (the credential the human holds today, and its eligibility, is stated in the CR before this clause is approved). `enrol` negotiates protocol version (`low/high`, structured refusal) and yields a per-process session credential; the runner's identity is a committed `Actor`. [server] **The run credential is the run id; its validity is computed from *that run's* lease record** (last accepted `received_at` + `lease_seconds` + declared harvest grace) — never rotated, never re-validated by a later run on the same step. [server] No run, session or runner credential can author a human-judgment record (I-7).
2. **Claim.** [server] `reserve` → `acquire` is an atomic checkout with a coded conflict; readiness recomputed at acquire; every write carries the run id; every claim attempt and refusal is a committed record. **Coupling to the tree:** a lease exists only under a live committed run claim by the same worker, and the run claim's window is the step type's maximum lease — one clock, the tree's.
3. **Lease.** [server] Claim response carries `lease_seconds` and `heartbeat_seconds`, server-chosen, `heartbeat_seconds ≤ lease_seconds / 3`, revisable on a heartbeat reply effective from the next accepted heartbeat, never retroactively; measured on server monotonic time. [runner] The heartbeat runs in its own process, hits the cheapest endpoint, echoes `sent_at`/`received_at` (diagnostic), carries progress; its reply may carry `cancel`, `terminate`, `retire_at`, cadence. [runner] **The process that owns the job handle keeps a local deadline = its own `sent_at` + `lease_seconds` on its monotonic clock, and treats an unanswered deadline, a heartbeat-child death, or a wake from standby as `expired`: kill the job, reap.** A heartbeat rejected *because the run is superseded* → kill, harvest nothing; rejected *because the lease is exhausted* → kill, harvest as `failed` within grace. The server-side check for all of it: any write or harvest after computed expiry is a *recorded refusal* — the kill is a spend courtesy, the fence is the control.
4. **Progress and states.** [server] Progress = runner-observed monotonic counters declared per step type (bytes written under declared output paths; child exits; from slice 3, accepted provider calls); `stalled` = none advanced for N intervals → `terminate`. **Interim spend control until slice 3:** wall-clock deadline plus the provider's organisation-level spend limit; D19 (looping under a live heartbeat) is OPEN until the proxy meters. [server] `awaiting-human` is entered only from `complete` or the post-complete gate — so the transition is race-free with an in-flight upload — and releases the lease, supersedes the run credential, reaps the sandbox; exits are the human's answer as a declared input to a re-run, or `keel judge-set`. [server] Poison counting by cause (`expired` / `startup-failed` / `failed`); startup failures never count against the step; N expiries by one runner in a window poison the runner. An unclaimed ready step past a declared interval is a fault.
5. **Harvest.** [runner] Upload declared outputs by digest under the run id, then one `complete`, **idempotent**: a retried `complete` returns the same result. [server] Verify digests against the declared output set, fence a lapsed or superseded run, write completion and receipt in one write; a lease expiring after N blobs and no `complete` lands as `expired` with the received digests; orphan blobs are GC'd by run id; a restarted runner never resumes an on-disk run. Reaping on `complete`-ack or expiry + grace, whichever is later.
6. **Retire.** [server] Per runner version: `current / ramping / draining / drained`, in-flight runs pinned (K12); an idle runner is told why. [runner] No self-update — detected at enrol.
7. **Liveness of the subsystem.** [server] One process hosts server, lease and (slice 3) proxy under one named supervisor on this host (the CR picks Windows Service or Task Scheduler); the heartbeat carries the daemon's own resource facts; **surface availability and queue visibility are computed indicators with a fault** (issue150 and 152 were outages the human noticed first; issue096 a missing surface). A DOM-rung test is bound before the browser is the only human surface; the chat channel stays as the fallback (I-7).

**Trigger to build slice 3:** a recorded Statement asking for the first CONSTRAINED step (a container, no readable credential), or naming a hostile-agent threat model.

##### 3.3 Slice 3 — CONSTRAINED steps: the container and the egress proxy

Materialisation is `git archive` of the declared input paths at the base tree into a fresh `git init` with no remote (I-3 holds; base tree id in the receipt). The proxy holds the provider credential; per step type it enforces a **body policy** — allowed `model` refused pre-flight, provider server-side network tools (web fetch/search, code-execution containers with egress) denied by default — and the denial names the field as it names a host. It meters the provider's `usage` fields (input, output, cache read, cache write) against a pricing table on the step type; a stream aborted before `usage` arrives is charged its `max_tokens`; a request whose `max_tokens` would overrun the remaining budget is refused pre-flight; async/batch operations are outside the CONSTRAINED operation set unless declared with a metered retrieval. It checks the run credential's computed validity on every forwarded call, so a partitioned runner's agent is starved regardless of the runner's health. Stated plainly: input content reaching the *declared* provider is the intended flow; the provider's data terms are the trust boundary.

#### 4. Open forks (the human owns them)

- **F0 — D0209 clause 5, superseded whole**, this direction re-adopting the keystone condition as I-11. Recommendation: supersede. Held as a process change. **F0b — D0182's single-writer clause**, lifted for the run, kept for the gate. Recommendation: supersede. Held.
- **F1 — the slice-1 boundary.** (a) profile scrub on the same OS user, residual class stated: the whole user profile and every repository on disk are reachable by path; or (b) a separate low-privilege OS user with the parent repository read-only by ACL and the run profile as the diff baseline. Recommendation: (b) if the CR's measured floor allows it; (a) otherwise, with the residual accepted in the human's words.
- **F2 — gate set.** Static analysis and secret scanning as bound `checkedBy` predicates in the frozen meta-process, or project habits. Recommendation: bound.
- **F3 — subsystem or separate project** (st185). Slice 1 is a subsystem by construction (a schema CR on `schema/core` plus one `CliCommand`); separation later is a `project-migration` (D0275). About a third of the ledger (C6) is keel-the-product either way. Not recommended either way yet.

#### 5. Vulnerabilities the author concedes

V1. Slice 1's agent can spend without a ceiling and reach the network; under F1(a) it can also reach every repository on disk. The harvest protects the model, not the host.
V2. Nothing here shortens the human's queue, and nothing may (D0204); I-10 bounds keel's concurrency, not the human's pace.
V3. Gates are only the predicates someone bound (F2).
V4. CLAUDE.md §4: 64 of 98 lines become moot inside a run; the remainder is a tracked conversion list; interactive sessions keep all 98.
V5. Cost on this host: the measured floor is owed before approval; the ladder as gate is minutes per step with parallelism 1.
V6. Shell class splits: the 8 records-eaten-by-the-shell issues vanish under replay; the 5 shell-writes-code issues stay inside a run.
V7. Hypothesis B (nobody wants the authoring friction) is live until slice 1 is measured; the unused launcher is evidence for it.
V8. Until slice 3 the D19 loop is bounded only by wall-clock and the provider's spend limit.
