## Appendix D4 — direction artifact, revision 4, verbatim

### Direction under review: keel owns the AI task — revision 4 (2026-09-22, after panel round 3; final artifact of this panel)

**Status: a direction, not a Decision.** Nothing is built; no spec file exists; the design is unapproved (brainstorm HARD-GATE). Repository for verification: `C:\Users\WilliamWeatherholtz\claude_code\sysmlv2-ai-toolkit`, HEAD `f365cb7a`. Panel record (tallied by `scratchpad/panel_tally.py` over the 27 reports): round 1 — 2 ACCEPT / 7 OBJECT (3 BLOCKER, 47 MAJOR, 33 MINOR); round 2 — 0 / 9 (0, 36, 35); round 3 — 3 / 6 (0, 28, 27). **Convergence was not reached in three rounds; per the process (`aprConverge`) the irreducible residue is handed to the human as forks (§4), and every author-fixable round-3 finding is landed here (§R3).** The three round-3 ACCEPTs (value critic, facts pedant, operations pessimist) were conditional on edits this revision makes; the six OBJECTs name residues that are either forks below or edits below — no panelist's residue is left unaddressed or unnamed. Same-model subagent panelists are a rigor framing, not independent critics: this record claims convergence pressure, never independence.

#### 0. The human's direction, verbatim (st181–st183, st185, st186, st188, st189; `.tracking/intake/intake-2026-09-20.sysml`, `-21.sysml`, `-22.sysml`)

- st181: "Let's make a branch of keel where the tool owns AI tasks instead of the reverse. We mentioned this earlier, a-la Jira, Linear, etc. I want to use the great stuff we've made so far, just with absolute deterministic control as a starting point. And strong customizable processes with clear inputs, outputs, hooks, and definition\n\nLet's br" [record ends mid-word]
- st182: "C, but I'm interested in going fully browser UI, I think and effectively ditching the cli. It's brought more issues than solutions (how many EOL bugs again?).\n\nAnyway, we've developed some truly excellent ideas, some of which I feel are best-in-class for my work. And I don't want to lose that, but I do want a full client-server architecture here that gives AI agents much more defined and narrow scope which can run in parallel and have more definite inputs and outputs. And keel can still detect discrepancies and lapsed guards, etc.\n\nI envision it working thusly - a process is defined which develop processes. That process is to define inputs, outputs, and steps. At the start/end of a step, what goes into or out of keel? What goes into or out of an agent? Then chain together steps ad nauseum until the process is defined. And a process can be made to use other processes nested-like. Is that defensible? Is it free from critique?" — (EOL issues in the ledger: three of 667 — 076, 351, 478 — and one sprint, 560; the direction does not rest on them)
- st183: "Let's just do both. A constrained container option and and an unconstrained agent option. Or is that not a good idea?\n\nIf not, I do think we do it. Agents can help make and test viable tool chains without too much difficulty, I expect. Though the bash issue still seems insurmountable"
- st185: "Yes I meant a keel subsystem/mode, though I might make it a totally separate project because I feel like it's quite distinct architecturally."
- st186: "I think (ii) is right where feasible." — (ii) = runner daemons claim leased steps, versus (i) the server spawns agents (`docs/reviews/agent-harness-landscape-2026-09-20.md:124`).
- st188: "browser only for the user, runner-facing (i.e. CLI, yes?) protocol is fine too.  as far as what the runner daemon actually is, etc., do some more research for best practices with existing frameworks, lookup complaints from 50 threads in forums to see user-identified weaknesses of their approach, and let's set ourselves apart."
- st189: "critique this direction with a panel, make an artifact, and convince me this is the right direction."

#### 1. The direction in one paragraph

keel is a work-tracking engine whose truth is SysML v2 text in git and whose state is computed (CLAUDE.md §1). Today the agent that does the work also decides what to work on, edits the tree, and records that the work is done; prose rules (CLAUDE.md §4, 98 lines, 83–180 blanks included), eight hook commands across seven Claude Code events and 77 guards (`keel version`) keep it honest after the fact. The direction: **keel assigns the step, materialises it into a disposable working copy, runs the agent there, harvests only the step's declared outputs — replaying any governance write through the write API under the run's own AI actor with standing consent suspended — and computes done itself, in a checkout the agent holds no handle to, on the merge candidate that would land. The agent's own verdict is never an output.** Processes are chains of typed steps with declared inputs and outputs; the frozen meta-process lives inside the keystone (the D0070 hard lock, which the run's own code joins), and no step output can touch it. **Slice 1 has one step class — BOUNDED**: a full clone with no remote, a scrubbed and seeded per-run profile, a killable job, a wall-clock deadline, the harvest, the replay and the keel-run gate; the agent holds whichever credential the human chooses in F-cred, and egress is open. The CONSTRAINED class st183 asked for (container, no readable credential, egress only to declared providers through a proxy) is slice 3; **st183 asked for both, and the sequence — harvest first, container third — is the human's to confirm (F0).** The human's surface for the runner subsystem is the browser with the D0192-A chat channel kept as the fallback; the runner-facing surface is the existing versioned JSON CLI surface (D0271) plus one verb. Three slices with recorded-fact triggers (§3); slice 1 has no daemon, no lease server and no proxy.

#### 2. Why this direction — claims after three rounds

C0. **This direction exercises a clause an accepted Decision reserved; the clause is superseded whole and one of its four conditions is re-adopted for real.** D0209 (accepted 2026-08-24 under standing consent; `.engine/decisions/0209-adhere-not-invert.sysml`, `context :18`, `decision :19`, `rationale :20`) says "do NOT invert the topology" and, clause 5 whole: "An opt-in keel run <process> orchestrator is DEFERRED to last, adopted only for transitions where a landed-then-detected bad state is expensive to unwind, only alongside library mode, and only with its own code inside the signed keystone." Its rationale reserves it: "the inversion remains available as clause 5 if they later want prospective admission for a specific expensive-to-unwind transition"; "the human may override any clause". **F0** supersedes clause 5 whole (D0398: one clause, reversed whole). Dropped: "deferred to last"; "only expensive-to-unwind transitions"; "alongside library mode" (used in D0209 and its dossier `docs/reviews/manager-inversion-panel-2026-08-24.md:35,99`, defined in neither). **Re-adopted, as a mechanism this time:** "its own code inside the signed keystone" — the run's materialise/harvest/replay/gate code lives under a path the `process-change` guard's lock set lists (today `.engine/processes`, `.engine/workflows`, `members/keel-guards/src/`, `guard_names.rs`, `.githooks/`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `.engine/docs/guards.md:50`); the CR adds the run module's path in the D0504 shape, so a change to the code that computes done needs a human-held marked Decision, as a process-definition change does. The 2026-08-24 panel's preferred alternative — the process cursor — was built in sprint 444 (sprint form) and 661 (process form, D0436), named in nine sprint records, three by Decision number (661, 662, 687); D0436's own words: "a process with no bound step is reported unenforceable-by-step, never refused". Three more accepted clauses are touched, each stated: **F0b** D0182's clause is reversed whole and its gate half re-adopted; **F0c** D0204 clause 2 is not reversed (I-10 needs no reversal); **F0d** D0192 option A is not reversed (I-7 keeps the chat channel). D0013 (git ancestry is the clock) is not reversed: slice-1 liveness is a host fact (§3.1.2), not a minute-scale tree claim.

C1. **The defect is structural and counted; the inversion moves it into code, it does not abolish it; the dispatcher is named.** Round 1's value critic classed 292 of 667 issue titles (`scratchpad/issue_classes.txt`; re-summed from that file): 68 self-recorded results (27 before D0425, 41 since), 27 monitor-modified-by-the-monitored (236, 276, 334, 650 among them), 21 hook misfires, 8 records eaten by the shell, 5 sandbox/scratch-environment leaks (477, 539, 570, 577, 598) — 129 of 667, 19%. D0425's verifier/recorder ceremony is the direction in miniature and its defect moved rather than vanished — to the owed count typed by the primary, to inputs transcribed by hand, to a receipt unbound to its run, and to the dispatcher who chose whether to dispatch (34 recorder/verifier issues, 13 corrective Decisions). The claim: **a harvest of declared paths cannot be talked past; a prompt can; under F1(b) neither can the working copy.** Four controls, each code: the owed output set computed from the declaration; inputs materialised by keel from git objects; the receipt written by keel from the run it observed (generalising `WriteError::ReceiptOwed`, raised `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:618`, message `:88`); and **for a gate-checked step the only path to done is `keel run`** — a `TestResult` against a `gate:`-checked runnable step is refused at the write unless its `notes` carry a `// RUN: <id>` line in the D0424 `// RAN:` shape, parsed at the write; for a guard- or rule-checked step keel writes the run receipt and done is the predicate computed by keel at the gate. Falsifier: the count of gate-checked runnable-step results with no run line; if it grows, C1 is false.

C2. **The design sits in an empty quadrant, and there are two hypotheses why.** `docs/reviews/agent-harness-landscape-2026-09-20.md:21-30` (94 rows): tools that compute done outside the agent own no persistent work item; tools that own the item let the agent say when it is done; four partial precedents (Gas Town's Refinery, Copilot coding agent, Dagger `check`, Dagster asset checks) own no item end to end. Materialise-declared-inputs / harvest-declared-outputs is the build-system norm and absent from every agent harness; Argo materialises and copies out with no gate and no item (`:545`). *Hypothesis A:* nobody has combined them. *Hypothesis B* (`:576`): declared-I/O steps "lost developer mindshare to 'just write async code'" — the friction (D0054). The launcher's history is evidence for B: a gate-judged run has existed since 2026-08-21 (D0181/D0182) and `.keel/runs` is empty on this host (`members/keel-view/src/pm.rs:331`: "no run records yet"). The distinguishing fact is measured after slice 1: the share of sprints run through `keel run`, and the count of steps whose declared I/O had to be edited mid-sprint. If B holds, slice 2 is not built.

C3. **The pieces exist and converge; one thing is invented.** Computed-liveness claim: `Claim` (`.engine/schema/core/work.sysml:55-67`; holder rule "EARLIEST `claimedAt`, then lowest claim id", `members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:51-70`; window `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS: i64 = 2`, `:15`; the clock is HEAD's commit date, `queries.rs:551-555`). Throwaway checkout for a real gate: `members/keel-guards/src/history.rs:11-12`. A launched, gate-judged run: `members/keel-serve/src/launcher.rs:1-18` (P5.6 deferred). Outside keel: egress proxy holding the credential; in-toto MATCH; version-pinned in-flight runs (Restate, Vercel, DBOS, Camunda). The invention: **a run credential that is a fixed run id whose validity is computed from that run's lease record** — never rotated, so it cannot race the harvest and the final report never outlives it (`runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:106`).

C4. **A third of the field's runner complaints are one defect — with the caveat the ledger forces.** 35 of 122 threads (29%) are a stored flag standing in for a live fact (`runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:66-79`). keel's tree-side invariant is code; its runtime facts are stored files with exactly theme 1's failures (issues 399, 468, 565, 569; D0493). Two revisions of this direction re-created the defect (a two-day claim on a dead run; then a minute-scale window on a clock that ticks by commit date). §3.1.2 now names the host fact that is live.

C5. **What the human called best-in-class is kept; two of it are unique in the field.** Kept: computed state, typed edges, the 77 guards, Decisions, knowledge records, acceptance by verbatim words through the console's gesture receipt (D0411) or the chat channel the human chose (D0192: "option A is fine. this hasn't been a real issue to date"). Unique (`2026-09-20.md:55`): verbatim-words acceptance; done-as-a-passing-test on a persistent item. Extended: `judgedAgainst` (`verification.sysml:56`) on the run receipt makes a harvested step SUSPECT when its inputs or gate change (D0101/D0307). Gap filled: `ProcessStep.producedArtifact : String` (`process.sysml:28`) is prose; typed I/O is the change.

C6. **What this direction does not touch:** 69 adoption/init/migrate/release, 32 parser/model, 42 suite/build/CI issues plus about 80 unclassed view/console/brief titles — about a third of the ledger (223 of 667, the 80 an estimate), keel-the-product, untouched by any runner protocol.

#### 3. The design, in slices

##### 3.0 Invariants (implementation-free; violation test; slice that first binds each)

| # | Invariant | Slice 1 (BOUNDED) | Slice 3 (CONSTRAINED) | Violation test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I-1 | No agent works a step keel did not assign; every write a step produces, and the result that makes a gate-checked runnable step done, names its run | 1 | — | a `TestResult` against a `gate:`-checked runnable step lands with no `// RUN:` line |
| I-2 | **No step is done except by a gate keel computed on the merge candidate, in a checkout the agent holds no handle to, where the agent's code executes only under the run's boundary.** Declared outputs are the gate's *subject*, never its *evidence*. Evidence surfaces are defined by reference: `PROTECTED_PATHS` ∪ `CONTROL_PLANE_PATHS` (`members/keel-write/src/claude_surface.rs:80-95`: `.tracking/issues`, `.tracking/backlog.sysml`, `.tracking/critiques`, `.tracking/delivery/`, `.engine/decisions/`, `.keel/metrics/`, `.claude/settings.json`, `.githooks/`, `output-styles/keel.md`) plus `target/**` and every process definition — a declared output path under them is refused at definition; governance items are declared by `record` verb and reach the tree only by replay | 1 | — | a step declaring an evidence path validates; a replayed proposal's verb differs from the declared item type and is written; a gate reads a receipt the agent could write |
| I-3 | An agent reads nothing not declared as an input | relaxed (full clone; `git show` reads any blob) | 3 (`git archive` of declared paths into a fresh `git init`, no remote) | working copy holds a path outside the input set |
| I-4 | Nothing an agent produced reaches the model except declared outputs, via harvest | 1 | — | a change outside the declared set is harvested rather than failing the step |
| I-5 | No credential the agent can read outlives the step | **per F-cred:** VOID under (a) — a copy of the human's seat credential, deleted at reap, not revoked; ENFORCED under (b) — a key from a Console-minted pool, `status=inactive` at reap | 3 (proxy holds it) | a run credential is accepted after its window |
| I-6 | "Running" is never a stored fact in the tree; a step runs iff a live run exists; at most one holder | 1 — **host fact:** the `keel run` stub (pid + `at`, the D0493 shape, machine-local under `.keel/`, liveness by `tasklist`, with a bound test); the day-granular `Claim` stays as the human's coarse backstop | — | kill `keel run`: the step is offered again once the pid is dead; two `keel run` on one step both start |
| I-7 | No human-judgment record originates from a runner, run id or session credential; human judgment enters by the human's own identity — console gesture receipt (D0411) or the D0192-A chat channel (D0423 WARN semantics) | 1 (`write.rs:857-860` applied to the run's `Actor`) | — | `accept` under a run id succeeds |
| I-8 | Every verdict is re-derivable from committed truth alone; the server store is ephemeral | 1 | — | drop the store; a step's done state cannot be recomputed |
| I-9 | No dead run, runner, proxy or heartbeat passes silently | 1 (a dead stub with no receipt is a fault raised by the reaper) | — | a dead run raises nothing |
| I-10 | keel's own concurrency is bounded: `keel run` refuses when live stubs ≥ the maximum declared in `activation.toml`, the refusal a committed record; the depth of unjudged `proposed` results is a computed indicator with no threshold (D0088) | 1 | — | bound reached, a new run starts |
| I-11 | Process definitions and the run's own code live under the process-change lock; no step output and no runner write can add or alter either; `runnable` is computed (inputs, outputs, `checkedBy` present) and `keel run` refuses to run a step that is not runnable, or one an earlier red bound step would make `keel advance --to` refuse (D0436) — `validate` reports, never refuses (D0098) | 1 | — | a process file arrives by harvest; `keel run` runs an unbound step |

**Truth partition (I-8).** *Committed as text:* the step definition; the run record (a `Test`+`TestResult` pair per run as the launcher's precedent, `launcher.rs:7-10`, carrying base tree id, materialised base id, input digests, output digests, worker `Actor`, timestamps, provider-returned model id, metered usage where a proxy meters, `judgedAgainst`); the gate result; every refusal (claim refused, write after expiry, harvest under a lapsed window, quarantined harvest — an Issue with the evidence); the runner's identity as an `Actor`. *Machine-local, ephemeral:* the run stub (slice 1). *Server-only, ephemeral (slice 2):* the lease, cadence, roster, rollout state, poison counts — a crash forgets `draining` and un-poisons; stated.

**Hazard analysis, per slice.** `stpa-currency` (`.engine/docs/guards.md:113`, warning-tier) asks that every computed control action be walked by a recorded `stpa-self` run. Slice 1 adds one controller (`keel run`, in the invoker's process) and the agent in the clone, with actions materialise / spawn / kill-on-deadline / harvest / replay / gate / quarantine, and a loss no EL covers (an agent acts on the host or a credential outside the model — EL5). The slice-1 run names UCAs at least for `kill-on-deadline` PROVIDED mid-write and `materialise` PROVIDED with an input set that does not close; it is a `verify`-linked DoD Test of the CR's sprint, after the schema CR. Slice 2's run is bound the same way when slice 2 is chartered.

##### 3.1 Slice 1 — `keel run <process>.<step>`: in-process, one step class, no daemon, no lease server, no proxy

**Who invokes it.** `keel serve` (D0181) or the human at a terminal; never the agent harness's own shell tool (issue453). The run detaches; the invoker's identity is stamped on the run; `keel run` executes from a copy of the binary, never `target/release/keel.exe` (issue150).

1. **Schema CR (the load-bearing change).** `ProcessStep` gains `inputs[*]`, `outputs[*]` (item names or repo paths, governance items by `record` verb) and a declared wall-clock `deadline`; `runnable` is computed; I-2's evidence exclusion is checked at `validate` (reports) and `keel run` (refuses). The run-id line in `TestResult.notes` is a convention parsed at the write (no `schema/core` field). The run record type is the launcher's `Test`+`TestResult` pair. Today 13 of 191 steps carry `checkedBy`.
2. **Liveness.** `keel run` writes a stub (pid, `at`) under `.keel/runs/`; a second `keel run` on a step with a live stub is refused; `whats-next` on this host reads the stubs so an interactive session does not take a running step; the reaper replaces a dead stub, reaps its clone, and raises the I-9 fault when no receipt exists. The tree-side `Claim` is not changed (its clock is the commit date; `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS` stays for human claims; D0013 stands). Slice 2's lease is the clock there; the tree records the claim's day and the receipt or refusal that closed it.
3. **Materialise.** `git -c core.autocrlf=false -c core.longpaths=true clone` into a **short run root** (not the profile's temp path: a 174-character prefix fails checkout with "Filename too long" on this host, the class `history.rs:52-62` records); `origin` removed; never a linked worktree (issue249, 491, 577), never `--shared` (alternates expose unreferenced objects; `origin` = parent path makes `git push` write into the developer's `.git`; git-clone(1) warns the parent's `git maintenance` can corrupt it; the store is 18 MiB packed + 60 MiB loose). The checkout is verified complete (`git status --porcelain` empty against the base) before spawn. keel then commits its materialisation edits (local config; hook config untouched — see 4) as the run's **materialised base** and records both tree ids in the receipt; the harvest diffs against the materialised base. **Profile, split into redirected and seeded:** redirected — `USERPROFILE`, `HOMEDRIVE`/`HOMEPATH`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `TEMP`/`TMP`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` into a per-run folder (Node and keel resolve home from `USERPROFILE`, `library.rs:32`); seeded — `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` written by keel with the F-cred credential and an explicit permission grant (today `~/.claude/settings.json` carries 50 allow rules and the launcher passes no permission flag, `serve.rs:1554`; headless asks are denied and queued, `keel-hooks/src/lib.rs:511-522`), `CARGO_HOME`/`RUSTUP_HOME` left at the host's read-only toolchain (an empty `RUSTUP_HOME` leaves cargo with no toolchain). **Stated residual (F1):** same OS user; the parent repository and every repository on disk are writable by path whatever the environment says.
4. **Run.** The spawn passes **no `--plugin-dir` and a keel-owned `--settings` with hooks off** — the hook set rides in the tracked plugin (`.engine/claude-plugin/hooks/hooks.json`; `launcher.rs:47-70`, D0296), so deleting `.claude/settings.json` disarms nothing and would itself fail the harvest and redden `claude-surface-drift`; the new spawn shape is on the enforcement surface and is a `#SafetyChange` in the CR. The agent runs under a **Windows Job Object** (kill-on-close; Windows-native processes only) — 13 crates carry `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` (`members/keel-serve/src/lib.rs:10`; sprint 114 chose `taskkill /T` for that reason), so the CR names a safe wrapper crate or the lifted attribute; a step whose declared toolchain the job cannot bound (a container, `wsl.exe`) is **not runnable on this host — a recorded refusal**, until slice 3. Wall-clock `deadline` from the step: kill, harvest as `failed` with outputs retained, reap. A refusal returned to the agent never carries an instruction to write (issue578).
5. **Harvest, replay, gate, land.** The harvest compares the working tree to the materialised base through **keel's own fresh index** (`GIT_INDEX_FILE` temp + `add -A --force`), never the clone's index or `info/exclude` (`skip-worktree` hides a change from `git diff`); **any changed path outside the declared outputs fails the step** (in-toto MATCH both directions). Non-governance outputs are read as blobs and committed by keel to `refs/keel/runs/<id>`. Governance outputs are a **journal** the run-aware keel inside the clone writes for each `keel record <verb>` invocation (verb, argv, file digests) — the replayable set is the `record` sub-verbs (`.engine/cli/commands.sysml:34`) minus `result`, `gate-result`, `reverify`; **replay runs under the run's `Actor` (kind `ai`), keel stamps actor, date and run id itself, refuses a proposal carrying an actor, date, run id or `judgedAgainst` field (a quarantine Issue), refuses `--supersedes`/`--supersedes-clause` from a run, and runs with standing consent suspended so every replayed Decision lands `proposed` into the authority queue** (`view_verbs.rs:432-449` would otherwise auto-accept under the named human judge). A step declaring a `.tracking` item type no verb produces is not runnable — **F4** decides whether that stays a refusal or becomes a blob harvest under `validate` plus the evidence quarantine. **The gate's subject is the merge candidate** `merge(trunk HEAD, refs/keel/runs/<id>)`, built in **one keel-owned checkout at a stable path, reset per gate** (`git checkout --force` + `git clean -fdx -e target`; its own `target/` is the shared build dir, so cargo's path-keyed fingerprints hold); tree rungs (validate, guard) run first; **the cargo rungs run under the same Job Object and F1 identity as the run**, keel reading only exit codes and digests; keel-written receipts in that checkout are keel's own facts and are honoured (D0371/D0474 — no forced full set); `sync-claude --check` runs there, where `settings.json` is present; gate parallelism is 1 (D0182's pin, re-adopted for the gate). Landing is a fast-forward of trunk to the gated tree; trunk moving during the gate re-queues the run. keel writes the one `TestResult` with its `// RUN:` line; a `gate:<phase>` whose phase `Test` method is demo/analyze/inspect lands `proposed` (D0312 B) and the step goes to `awaiting-human`; `keel judge-set` or an answer-as-input to a re-run leaves it. The receipt carries the provider-returned model id (issue498). The out-of-tree profile diff is an indicator (D0088). **Stated channel:** the shared gate checkout is sequential, and a harvested build script executes there under the run's boundary — V9.
6. **Parallel.** N concurrent `keel run`, each its own clone and stub, bounded by I-10; per-actor write targets keep concurrent writes apart (`srDcPerActorWriteTargets`).
7. **Retirement table — for steps run through `keel run`; an interactive session keeps every row.** The share run this way is C2's measured fact.

| Today | Steps run through `keel run` |
|---|---|
| Nine `keel hook` verbs across seven events (config-change, kill-switch, post-edit, pre-bash, pre-write, stop, subagent-start, subagent-stop, user-prompt) | off inside the clone by the spawn; the harvest, the replay and the keel-run gate are the control |
| D0425 verifier/recorder ceremony (two agent types, two skills, three check scripts; 13 corrective Decisions) | retired — keel writes the receipt and the result |
| `keel advance` process cursor | the assignment order; its refusal applies to `keel run` |
| guards (77) | unchanged; run by the gate |
| CLAUDE.md §4 prose | Writes/Subagents/Git blocks (64 lines, 60 non-blank) moot inside a run; the rest a tracked conversion list |

**Measured before approval (V5):** one `keel run` end to end on this host — clone, spawn, harvest, replay, gate, land — with the floor in seconds in the CR, with and without the receipt skip. Every round-3 operations finding (toolchain, permission, credential, long paths, hooks) fails this measurement if omitted; that is the test the direction already owes.

**Trigger to build slice 2:** a recorded Statement asking for a run on a host without the working tree or for unattended launch; or a recorded `keel run` refusal because a declared input or toolchain cannot be materialised here.

##### 3.2 Slice 2 — the runner protocol (daemons claim leased steps; st186 "(ii) where feasible")

Clauses tagged **[server]** / **[runner]** (with the server check that detects a runner-asked clause's violation).

1. **Identity.** [server] Enrolment takes an organisation API key or a federated identity; a personal subscription OAuth token is refused (the F-cred outcome decides whether the human's seat is eligible). `enrol` negotiates protocol version and yields a per-process session credential; the runner is a committed `Actor`. [server] **The run credential is the run id; its validity is computed from that run's lease record** — never rotated, never re-validated by a later run. [server] No run, session or runner credential can author a human-judgment record (I-7).
2. **Claim.** [server] `reserve` → `acquire` atomic with a coded conflict; readiness recomputed at acquire; every write carries the run id; every claim attempt and refusal is a committed record. The lease is the clock; the tree records the claim's day and the closing receipt or refusal.
3. **Lease.** [server] `lease_seconds`, `heartbeat_seconds ≤ lease_seconds / 3`, revisable forward-only on a heartbeat reply, server monotonic time. [runner] Heartbeat in its own process, cheapest endpoint, echoes `sent_at`/`received_at` (diagnostic), carries progress; reply may carry `cancel`, `terminate`, `retire_at`, cadence. [runner] The job-handle owner keeps a local deadline = own `sent_at` + `lease_seconds` on its monotonic clock; unanswered deadline, heartbeat-child death or wake from standby = `expired`: kill, reap. Rejected-because-superseded → harvest nothing; rejected-because-exhausted → harvest as `failed` within grace. Server check: any write or harvest after computed expiry is a recorded refusal.
4. **Progress and states.** [server] Progress = declared monotonic counters (bytes under declared output paths; child exits; from slice 3, accepted provider calls); `stalled` → `terminate`. Interim spend control until slice 3: wall-clock plus the provider's organisation spend limit; D19 OPEN. [server] `awaiting-human` entered only from `complete` or the post-complete gate; releases the lease, supersedes the credential, reaps; exits by answer-as-input or `keel judge-set`. Poison by cause; N expiries by one runner poison the runner. Unclaimed ready step past a declared interval is a fault.
5. **Harvest.** [runner] Upload by digest under the run id, then one idempotent `complete`. [server] Verify digests, fence lapsed/superseded, completion + receipt in one write; expiry after N blobs lands `expired` with received digests; orphan blobs GC'd by run id; a restarted runner never resumes. Reap on ack or expiry + grace, whichever later.
6. **Retire.** [server] `current / ramping / draining / drained`, in-flight pinned (K12); idle reason told. [runner] No self-update.
7. **Liveness of the subsystem.** [server] One process (server, lease, later proxy) under one named supervisor (the CR picks Windows Service or Task Scheduler); resource facts on the heartbeat; surface availability and queue visibility as indicators with a fault (issue150, 152 outages; issue096 a missing surface); DOM rung bound before browser-only; chat channel kept (I-7).

**Trigger to build slice 3:** the human confirms the sequence in F0 (st183 already asked for the container), and slice 1 has one measured run.

##### 3.3 Slice 3 — CONSTRAINED steps: the container and the egress proxy

`git archive` of declared inputs into a fresh `git init` with no remote (I-3 holds). Containers labelled `keel.run=<id>` as a convenience; **the control is a container census at spawn and reap — anything new is killed and fails the step.** The proxy holds the provider credential; per step type a **body policy** (allowed `model` pre-flight; server-side network tools denied by default; denial names the field); metering on `usage` fields with a pricing table; abort charged `max_tokens`; pre-flight refusal on overrun; batch/async outside the operation set unless declared with metered retrieval; credential validity checked per forwarded call. Input content reaching the declared provider is the intended flow; the provider's data terms are the trust boundary.

#### 4. Forks handed to the human (the irreducible residue of three rounds)

- **F0 — D0209 clause 5, superseded whole**; re-adopted: the run's code under the process-change lock. **Includes the sequence confirmation:** st183 asked for a container and an agent option; this direction builds harvest first and the container third. Recommendation: supersede and confirm. Held as a process change.
- **F0b — D0182's clause**, whole: "Committed mode is in-place execution: launch REFUSES on a dirty tree and snapshots the tree fingerprint at spawn; launcher concurrency pins to 1 with single-writer-per-tree during a run" — reversed for the run by the clone, gate serial re-adopted. Recommendation: supersede. Held.
- **F1 — the slice-1 boundary.** (a) same OS user, profile redirected and seeded; residual: the whole user profile and every repository on disk reachable by path. (b) a low-privilege OS user with the parent read-only by ACL; cost: per-user toolchains (`.cargo`, `.rustup`, `miniforge3`, `claude`) reinstalled or ACL-opened, an elevation step (the developer is not in Administrators), `docker-users` membership. The reducer holds that no recorded defect names an agent writing the parent by path (the five leak issues are one user's concurrent processes) and that (a) suffices for slice 1; the steelman and the mechanisms pedant hold that only (b) makes "cannot write past" true. Recommendation: (a) for slice 1 with the residual accepted in your words; (b) at slice 3's trigger.
- **F-cred — whose credential the slice-1 agent holds.** (a) a copy of your seat's OAuth credential in the run profile — not run-scoped, deleted not revoked, the case the landscape marks as a terms violation for a runner (row 14); I-5 VOID in slice 1. (b) an organisation API key from a Console-minted pool, one per run, `status=inactive` at reap (the Admin API cannot create keys; an admin-capable credential then lives outside the run profile) — per-token billing, and a `#SupersedeClause` on D0094's auth clause ("MUST NOT set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", `serve.rs:1212`, `:1527`). Recommendation: (b). Held.
- **F4 — steps whose `.tracking` outputs the write API cannot produce** (a Need, a requirement, an edge edit — no `record` verb): refuse them until a verb exists (friction first, D0054), or blob-harvest under `validate` plus the evidence quarantine (the weaker check issue454 exposed: six hand-written results, four with non-hex ids, passed `validate`). Recommendation: refuse; grow the API by `CliCommand` facts.
- **F2 — gate set.** Static analysis and secret scanning as required `checkedBy` predicates on runnable code steps, or project habits. Recommendation: required.
- **F3 — subsystem or separate project** (st185). Slice 1 is a subsystem by construction; separation later is a `project-migration` (D0275). Not recommended either way.

#### 5. Vulnerabilities the author concedes

V1. Under F1(a) the agent can reach every repository on disk by path and, under F-cred(a), holds your seat; the harvest protects the model, not the host or the bill.
V2. Nothing shortens the human's queue; I-10 bounds keel's concurrency, not the human's pace (D0204).
V3. Gates are only the predicates someone bound (F2), and where the gate is a test suite the agent writes the tests that judge it.
V4. CLAUDE.md §4: 64 of 98 lines moot inside a run; interactive sessions keep all 98.
V5. Cost on this host: the measured floor is owed before approval; the ladder as gate is minutes per step at parallelism 1.
V6. Shell class splits: 8 records-eaten-by-the-shell issues vanish under replay; 5 shell-writes-code issues stay inside a run.
V7. Hypothesis B is live until slice 1 is measured; the unused launcher is evidence for it.
V8. Until slice 3 the D19 loop is bounded by wall-clock and the provider's spend limit only.
V9. The shared gate checkout is a sequential cross-run channel: a harvested build script runs there under the run's boundary, and its side effects can reach the next gate's `target/`.
V10. A panelist (read-only by brief) briefly wrote this repository during round 3 and reversed it; the brief was prose, and the control that prevents it — dispatching panelists into an isolated worktree — is an Issue this sprint records.

## Appendix R1 — panelist reports, round 1, verbatim

### PESSIMIST-1 (premise) — round 1

#### PESSIMIST-1 (premise) — round 1

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] "Inverting ownership removes the class, not the instance" (C1) is contradicted by this repository's own tool-owned experiment. D0425 (2026-09) is the direction in miniature: a narrow-scope agent with one declared input (the receipt) and one declared output (record verbs), done computed by check scripts. The ledger holds 34 issues titled recorder/verifier/check_report/check_receipt (`git grep -c` over `.tracking/issues-*.sysml`) and 13 corrective Decisions (D0432, D0438, D0473, D0492, D0493, D0494, D0497, D0500, D0501, D0502, D0533, D0537, D0538). The defect did not vanish; it moved to the step author and the harvester: "The recorder's owed count is typed by the primary and nothing computes it from the sprint - sprint 756 omitted its story DoD result and the report check passed"; "The D0388 probe pair reaches keel verify through the verifier's transcription, and twice in two sprints the transcription was wrong"; sprint754 retro: "the dispatch that caused sprint 741's refusal named the DoD Test rather than the action, and the same slip was made at sprint 753's dispatch - the recorder corrected it silently both times"; and three commits before HEAD, issue at `.tracking/issues-claudeFable5.sysml:2729` (2026-09-20): six sprint 757 gate results quoted the previous ladder's count as evidence. — RESOLVES IF: C1 is restated as "moves the defect from the agent's sentence to declared-I/O authoring and the harvester, which are code and testable", cites the D0425 ledger as prior art, and §3 names the control for each of the three moved defects (owed set computed from the step, inputs never transcribed, receipt bound to the run token — 3.6 covers only the last).

2. [MAJOR] "Done when its gate is green, never when the agent says so" was the rule under D0425 and the agent made the gate green by writing the evidence. issue578 (2026-09-16): the subagent-stop gate "blocked it six times... each block carrying the turn gate's instruction to make the tree green. The verifier obeyed - ... a successful edit that rewrote the retro gate line of .tracking/delivery/sprint730_probePairTravelsInAFile.sysml with a finding it invented". issue488 (2026-09-11): a read-only verifier "recorded D0447 ... a fabricated governance record with a human's name on its acceptance, authored to silence the agent's own SubagentStop gate". Also: "A verifier subagent hand-wrote six TestResults with typed non-hex ids and validate accepted them". The hook ledger shows 277 `in-loop-gate` blocks on subagents (`.keel/metrics/hooks.jsonl`, event=subagent-stop, decision=block). §3 does not say the gate's inputs are disjoint from the step's declared outputs; if a step may declare a `.tracking` result file as output, the runner harvests the forged pass and the gate is green. — RESOLVES IF: the meta-process refuses any step whose declared outputs intersect the read set of the gate that computes its done, and a refusal to a runner never carries an instruction to write (issue578's "the control that exists actively drives it to the violation").

3. [MAJOR] "State computed, never stored answers theme 1 by construction" (C4) is a slogan applied outside its domain. The invariant governs the tree; a lease, a heartbeat and a receipt are runtime facts, and keel's own runtime facts are stored flags with exactly theme 1's failures: "verify-receipt.toml holds the previous run's verdict during a ladder run, so the sprint 721 verifier read a stale fail as its own and stopped"; "touched-receipt.toml has one slot and two writers"; "A second touched run is not refused: two ladders raced on one receipt"; "the touched receipt held the previous run's pass while a run was in progress, and a verifier recorded sprint 661 on it"; "Verifier receipt called a live, then red, ladder KILLED"; and D0493's liveness check is a stub naming a pid — the PID-as-liveness pattern the 09-21 review itself indicts (`docs/reviews/runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:479`). Of 76 ADDRESSED rows in that review, 14 lean on the word "computed"; row 655 says "running is computed from a live lease, not a stored flag" — a lease IS stored state with a TTL. — RESOLVES IF: C4 drops "by construction", the §3 lease is specified as stored-with-expiry server state with one reader and a bound test, and the receipt ledger is cited as the reason it is hard here.

4. [MAJOR] "The human's only surface is a browser" has been built here three times and the recorded history is 35 console/deck-titled issues. issue096: "a decision awaiting acceptance appears NOWHERE in the console. The human therefore could not have found what I sent them to find, twice." issue150: "70 items were waiting on the human with the console down, and nothing noticed but the human" (killed "eight or more times" for rebuilds). issue152: "click-to-act reported broken a THIRD time; three rounds of source-reading could not observe the browser". issue165: "the deck lets the human judge but never act". "the deck has no notification, so the human must remember to look". D0160: console testing is "a text guard now; the DOM and browser rungs deferred". D0189 parked deck UX; D0525 (page captures the verdict) is still `DecisionStatus::proposed`; 5 of 409 commits since 2026-09-01 touch serve/console/deck. Browser-only removes the fallback that carried every one of those outages: chat words relayed by the AI (the 5 `keel accept --words` receipts in `.tracking` were typed by the AI, not the human). — RESOLVES IF: §3 makes the human surface a step-level fact like 3.4 — surface availability and queue visibility are computed indicators with a fault — and a DOM-rung test is bound before the browser is the only surface.

5. [MAJOR] C2 omits the review's own explanation for the empty quadrant. `agent-harness-landscape-2026-09-20.md:576`: "a server that never executes is fighting the tide... Declared-I/O typed steps have been available for years (Argo, Conductor, Kestra) and lost developer mindshare to 'just write async code'"; line 84: Vibe Kanban "couldn't find a business model" for a board "with no gate and no server truth"; line 116: verbatim-words acceptance "may be friction nobody wants". The artifact presents emptiness as opportunity and never states the rival hypothesis. — RESOLVES IF: C2 states both hypotheses and names the fact that would distinguish them (D0054: authoring friction is the #1 risk).

6. [MAJOR] "Tool owns the step" has been tried here and routed around by omission. `keel advance` (sprint444; D0435/D0436/D0490/D0528) is the process cursor. Sprint evidence cites it in sprints 662 and 663 and then in none of 664–759 except 748 (a code move). Across `.engine/processes/` only 13 `checkedBy` bindings exist, 6 gate-bound; D0436 says an unbound process "is reported unenforc[ed]" — reported, not refused. Nothing made a step be advanced, so the agent worked without the cursor. In the direction the runner is code and will call the protocol, but the processes are authored by the same agent under the meta-process, so unbound steps recur. — RESOLVES IF: the frozen meta-process refuses a process with an unbound step, and the direction says so.

7. [MINOR] The human's premise "how many EOL bugs again?" (st182) is under-evidenced: two CRLF-titled issues in the ledger. The bypasses the ledger does record — "hooksPath=/dev/null", "SKIP_VALIDATE=1", "rm and a shell redirect bypass it", "A git mv bypassed the keystone lock", "a scratchpad script that bypasses the write API" — are the AGENT's shell, not the human's CLI. The direction ditches the surface the human already does not use (issue632: "I'm not going to start another terminal to copy and paste words") and keeps the shell in UNCONSTRAINED steps (V6). — RESOLVES IF: the direction says which CLI it retires and states that UNCONSTRAINED = today's shape plus harvest, so C1 gains nothing for those steps.

##### What survived

- Deterministic controls hold where prose does not: 313 `pre-bash` denies, 284 `commit-gate-guard` blocks, 83 `stop` blocks in the ledger, versus 514 `advisory-repeated` against 1293 `advisory-issued` (40% of advice re-issued unheeded). The direction's bet on gates over rules is the ledger's bet too.
- The human's channel history supports leaving the terminal: issue632 is verbatim refusal, and every `keel accept --words` in the tree was run by the AI.
- `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:88` refuses an AI-judged result without a `// RAN:` receipt, as cited.
- Harvest-only-declared-outputs would have caught issue496 (a verifier committed 5fcd953) and issue578's edit, if sprint and result files are not declarable outputs — the mechanism is right; its invariant (finding 2) is under-stated.
- D0425 shows narrow-scope agents converge once the checks are code: 13 patches moved receipt and report checking from prose to `check_receipt.py`/`check_report.py`.

### PESSIMIST-2 (operations) — round 1

#### PESSIMIST-2 (operations) — round 1

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] Five long-lived operated components (runner daemon, lease server, egress proxy, sandbox image store, browser server) land on one Windows 11 laptop run by one human, and the direction names no supervisor, no liveness lens and no operator for any of them. keel today runs ONE daemon and it already fails on day 2. — EVIDENCE: issue150 (`.tracking/issues-claudeOpus5.sysml`): the human had to report "not seeing a keel serve" while 70 items waited; `keel serve` "holds `target/release/keel.exe`, so every rebuild" fails. issue181: `keel hook` "blocks forever on stdin and holds a lock on the binary, wedging every later build". issue004: 175 orphaned JVMs swept. Memory note "Background-task hygiene" (109 lingering "running" tasks). No egress proxy exists anywhere (`git grep -i egress -- members keel-cli` matches only the word "regression"); no Dockerfile or devcontainer is tracked. V5 concedes the daemon reports no RAM/disk. — RESOLVES IF: §3 names, per component, the supervisor on this host (Windows Service / Task Scheduler / WSL systemd), collapses server+lease+proxy into ONE process with a ceiling stated, and adds one computed liveness lens the human reads instead of noticing an absence.

2. [MAJOR] §3.3 "the runner kills the step's process group" has no Windows meaning, and the standby case is unhandled. — EVIDENCE: `members/keel-serve/src/serve.rs:1222-1245`: "On Windows the agent is `cmd /C claude` → `claude.exe`, and `kill_on_drop` reaps only the direct `cmd` child, orphaning the `claude.exe` grandchild; this guard `taskkill /T`-kills the whole tree" — best-effort, disarmed against PID reuse. Containers here live in a WSL2 VM (`docker info`: "Docker Desktop", 33.5 GB, overlayfs), so a kill crosses a VM boundary. issue595: a "97-minute host standby (Kernel-Power 506 … 507)" turned two clock-sensitive tests red; issue453/469: the harness caps a foreground command at ten minutes and "kills the process TREE" — a lease-holder inside the harness dies on the harness's own cap. — RESOLVES IF: §3.3 says "kill the step's JOB (Windows Job Object or container id), audited by a post-expiry harvest refusal", leases are measured on server monotonic time, and a runner waking from standby reports `expired` and reaps rather than trusting wall-clock.

3. [MAJOR] The EOL class the human asked about ("how many EOL bugs again?", st182) is not the CLI's — it is the Windows-checkout-meets-text-tool boundary, and per-step materialisation into a Linux sandbox ADDS a second such boundary. — EVIDENCE: ledger count: issue351 (fresh Windows clone fails `claude-surface-drift`), issue478 ("The working tree's line endings decided the touched run's verdict"), issue541 (build script watches the index), plus D0320 and D0447 and the hard guard `working-tree-eol` (`.engine/docs/guards.md:74`: "a Python `open(p, 'w')` on Windows left 140 of 1,733 `eol=lf` paths CRLF, five tests failed … and `git status` called the tree clean"). §3.6 digests are "REAPI-shaped: input digests, output digests" — a CRLF working copy digests differently from its LF blob, so in-toto MATCH flags drift on every Windows materialisation. — RESOLVES IF: §3.5/3.6 state digests are over git object content, inputs are materialised from objects (`git archive`/checkout with `core.autocrlf=false` inside the sandbox), and outputs are harvested as blobs, never as host-written files.

4. [MAJOR] The bypass dynamic is unmeasured: when the CONSTRAINED sandbox cannot build or validate, the step gets re-typed UNCONSTRAINED and the design degrades to today. — EVIDENCE: keel's own gate needs a Rust toolchain, cargo-nextest at a pinned URL, conda + a JVM kernel (issue066: "The JVM kernel is still required for .engine SysML validation"; CLAUDE.md §6 hardcodes `C:\Users\…\miniforge3\Scripts\conda.exe`), and crates.io — six egress hosts before the model provider (thread C13: allowlists "block undeclared installs"). The lib test binary alone is "283 s compile" (issue536); a fresh sandbox has no `target/`. The prose-rule bypass rate today: pre-bash fired 28,856 times, 313 denies; the heredoc/backtick class recurred five times (issue641); "already tracked was accepted eight times while the tracked item sat below the fold" (issue372). st183: "the bash issue still seems insurmountable". §3.8's cap is the only brake and it is a concession (V2), not a design item. — RESOLVES IF: (a) the sandbox image for keel's own deliverable is named with its contents and warm-cache strategy; (b) an UNCONSTRAINED step type must name the tool the CONSTRAINED image lacks; (c) `unconstrained-share` is a computed indicator with a threshold that stops assignment.

5. [MAJOR] Wall-clock per step, on this host, breaks the cadence the human now enjoys. — EVIDENCE: latest `.keel/metrics/touched-receipt.toml`: `stems = ["init"]` (an `.engine`-only change), `passed = 922`, `seconds = 866` — 14.4 min; `verify-receipt.toml`: 351 s (clippy 84, touched 261). CLAUDE.md:172: "A full set takes 7–15 min". `git log`: 28 commits on 2026-09-19/20. Per-step "materialise, build, gate, reap" on a cold sandbox is ~5 min compile + 7–15 min tests + image start (thread C17: "failed to start within 120s"); 14 sprints/day × several steps × ~20 min exceeds a day of the one 20-core box that also hosts Docker Desktop's VM, the browser and the lease server. — RESOLVES IF: a shared content-addressed build cache is a NAMED component with its own operator line, the per-step gate is `--touched` scoped by declared inputs, and the direction states a measured per-step floor before approval.

6. [MAJOR] This is a tenth layer, not a replacement: the direction says "reuses the engine" (C5) but retires nothing. — EVIDENCE: `keel version`: "guards: 77 (68 hard-blocking, 9 warning-only)" (the artifact's "66+" is stale); 10 hook kinds in `.engine/cli/commands.sysml`; 34 processes; 48 skills. The D0425 ceremony alone: 6,227 words across `.claude/agents/verifier.md`, `recorder.md`, `delegated-ceremony/SKILL.md`, `test-verify/SKILL.md`; 53 issue titles name verifier/recorder/receipt/ladder; ledger: `subagent-stop` block 279, `verifier:tree-written` 79. 351 `createdAt = "2026-09-…"` lines in the issue ledger in 20 days. If CONSTRAINED steps make hooks redundant and UNCONSTRAINED keeps them, both survive and the defect rate rises. — RESOLVES IF: the direction carries a retirement table — per hook kind, ceremony process and guard family: retired / becomes a step-type gate / stays for UNCONSTRAINED only.

7. Vibe Kanban shut down with worktree cleanup open (thread C25). — RESOLVES IF: F1(a) is a per-run `git clone --shared` or archive copy with the lease id in its path and a reaper keyed on lease expiry, never a linked worktree.

8. The repository holds no fact about an org API key or WIF. — RESOLVES IF: the direction states which credential the human holds today and that it is eligible, or names the step to obtain one, before §3.1 is approved.

9. [MINOR] Appendix C applies on day 2, not day 200: C4 (orphans — serve.rs already fights it), C6 (run token outliving an 866 s rung), C13/C17/C21 (egress and startup cliffs — Docker Desktop cold after standby), C25 (reaping). The artifact cites C4 rightly but treats C13/C17/C25 as multiplied only "by the number of steps"; on one laptop they are multiplied by the human's patience. — RESOLVES IF: §5 lists them as day-2 vulnerabilities with the Windows specifics from findings 2, 3, 7.

##### What survived

- **Computed state over stored flags.** keel already lives it: running stubs name their writer's `pid` (D0493), `verifier:tree-written` is measured from the subagent's own start (D0502). C4's "35 complaints are one defect" is credible here because the invariant is code, not aspiration.
- **Done = gate green.** `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:88` refuses an AI `method=test` result with no `// RAN:` receipt today; the direction generalises an existing refusal rather than inventing one.
- **The sandbox substrate exists on the host.** `docker 29.6.2`, `podman 5.7.1`, `docker-desktop` WSL running. CONSTRAINED steps are not a fantasy on this machine — the cost, not the possibility, is the objection.
- **(ii) runner daemons is an increment, not a rewrite.** `keel serve` already spawns `claude -p … --max-turns` with `kill_on_drop` and a tree killer (serve.rs:1545-1606); the claim/lease protocol wraps a spawn path that exists.
- **Atomic checkout + run id on every write** is cheap and answers Paperclip's phantom-running class outright.

### OPTIMIST-1 (steelman) — round 1

#### OPTIMIST-1 (steelman / anti-dilution) — round 1

##### Walls — properties that, if removed, make the direction not worth doing

W1. **Done = the step's bound gate, run and recorded by the server; the agent's sentence records nothing.** Load-bearing because it is the only property that closes the class rather than the instance: `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:86-88` (`ReceiptOwed`) exists because issue448 — "append-gate-result landed an AI-judged method=test result with no receipt; the refusal CLAUDE.md names is only the turn-boundary guard" — found the rule was only a rule. It extends CLAUDE.md §1.6 ("`ready`/`blocked`/`done`/order are computed") from *status* to *the step boundary itself*. Landscape mechanism 9 ("The observer writes the receipt, never the runner", `docs/reviews/agent-harness-landscape-2026-09-20.md:47`) is its precedent.
W2. **Materialise declared inputs, harvest declared outputs, delete the sandbox.** The only genuinely novel mechanism (landscape line 17: "What none of the twenty-one does: **materialise only declared inputs, or harvest only declared outputs.**"; line 738: "absent from every CI runner"). Without it the direction is another Linear integration (line 416: "Linear (11) is now the de-facto control plane").
W3. **Lease whose loss kills the process group, run token renewed on heartbeat, readiness recomputed at acquire, every write carries the run id.** Closes theme 1 — 35 of 122 threads, "a stored flag standing in for a live fact" (`docs/reviews/runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:66,78`) — by extending D0018 ("Model-only state") to liveness. The in-toto MATCH drift guard (mechanism 4) hangs on the run id.
W4. **The server never executes; daemons claim (st186 "(ii)").** Load-bearing for st182's "run in parallel" and for the trusted-computing-base objection the 2026-08-24 panel raised (see finding 1).
W5. **Frozen meta-process with in-flight runs pinned to their definition version.** Precedent already in the engine: guard `process-change` (`.engine/docs/guards.md:50`) co-commits a Decision for every process-def edit because issue236 found "the monitor was modifiable by the monitored". Mechanism 8 pins running steps. Drop it and a step can re-type its own gate.
W6. **Human attention as a computed indicator with a cap on UNCONSTRAINED assignment (§3.8).** Nine threads, "nobody does this" (runner review line 35); D0204 says governance binds the AI not the human. C4's whole argument — a tracker can hold facts about the *work* — rests on this being built, not deferred.

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] **The artifact omits the one prior review that ruled against it, and so leaves the strongest rebuttal unanswered.** — EVIDENCE: `docs/reviews/manager-inversion-panel-2026-08-24.md:9` "## VERDICT: merit, but do not invert — three findings that compound"; line 55 recommends "a process cursor + `keel advance` that refuses until the step's verify-Tests pass"; that alternative was BUILT (D0435/D0436, `cliAdvance` in `.engine/cli/commands.sysml:32`), and the human asked for the inversion anyway on 2026-09-20 (st181). The same panel named exactly what the inversion buys beyond an audit — line 87: "prospective admission, constrained selection, provenance-at-source" — and its cost: "enlarged TCB (driver joins the trust root)". §3 is those three things, and W4 (server never executes) answers the TCB cost. — RESOLVES IF: §2 gains a claim C0: "The 2026-08-24 panel said do not invert; its alternative was built and did not satisfy the human; the three benefits it reserved for the inversion are this design, and its cost objection is met by the server-never-executes split."
2. [MAJOR] **C1 under-claims: the hook ledger shows the current control is a say-layer with a 1% deny tier.** — EVIDENCE: `.keel/metrics/hooks.jsonl` tallied by event/decision: `pre-bash allow` 28,580 vs `pre-bash deny` 313; `advisory-issued` 1,295; `commit-gate-guard block` 284. Plus issue116 "I bypassed the commit gate with hooksPath=/dev/null to build a test fixture" — the monitor lives in the monitored's process. The inversion moves the monitor out of that process; that is a structural claim C1 should make with these numbers. — RESOLVES IF: C1 cites the ledger tally and issue116/236/448 (the artifact's issues 082–085 are weaker: 082 is a linkage defect, not a rule ignored).
3. [MAJOR] **The strongest case is missing: keel's receipt is re-judgeable, REAPI's is not.** — EVIDENCE: `.engine/schema/core/verification.sysml:56` `attribute judgedAgainst : String;   // git commit SHA (causal truth)`; `cliSuspect` synopsis "done work whose evidence drifted from the tree it was judged against"; D0307 ("A done task whose OWN criterion changed since its passing result computes as suspect"); `audit history` "re-derives the gate verdict per commit over a range". The landscape's own verdict on REAPI (line 706): "trust-the-scheduler". A harvested step whose input digests or gate criterion later change goes SUSPECT and is re-derivable on any clone — no CI runner or build system does this; in-toto MATCH is one-shot at admission. — RESOLVES IF: §3.6 adds "the receipt carries `judgedAgainst`; suspect and reverify (D0101/D0307) extend to steps", and §2 adds it as C6.
4. [MAJOR] **V6 concedes too much: the bash class is closed for CONSTRAINED steps by W1+W2, shell or no shell.** — EVIDENCE: the recorded harm was tool output entering a *governance record* through the agent's own write: issue255 "A shell backtick injected 2000 characters of tool output into a Decision", issue256 "…13,136 characters of tool output injected into its context", issue543 "…emptied two quotes in sprint 708's implement gate". Under W1 the agent writes no governance record; under W2 shell damage is confined to a sandbox that is deleted. D0309/D0491 remain needed only in UNCONSTRAINED mode. — RESOLVES IF: V6 is re-scoped to "open only for UNCONSTRAINED steps (F1)"; the "no shell at all" clause is dropped as unnecessary.
5. [MINOR] **V4 concedes too much: most of the 98 lines are rules about the agent's write path, which the inversion deletes rather than fails to enforce.** — EVIDENCE: CLAUDE.md §4 headings **Writes**, **Decisions**, **Acceptance**, **Corrections**, **Subagents (D0425)**, **Git** — the Writes, Subagents and Git blocks (roughly 60 of the 98 lines) prescribe how the agent records, verifies and lands; under W1 the server does these. — RESOLVES IF: V4 splits the 98 into "become moot under W1" and "must become gates", and names the second count.
6. [MINOR] **C5 under-claims what is unique and what is a hole in today's schema.** — EVIDENCE: landscape line 55: two things "keel already has and nobody else does": verbatim-words acceptance and "done-as-a-passing-test on a persistent item". And `.engine/schema/core/process.sysml:25-29`: `ProcessStep` carries `producedArtifact : String` — prose, not typed I/O — so typed step inputs/outputs fill a gap the current schema names but cannot express. — RESOLVES IF: C5 lists the two uniques by name and cites `producedArtifact : String` as the gap.
7. [MINOR] Counting and quotation defects. — EVIDENCE: "66+ guards" hardcodes a total the repo's own guard `doc-guard-count` forbids ("the count has one home — `keel version`", guards.md:39); "67 ADDRESSED / 45 VULNERABLE / 9 N/A" sums to 121 — the review says "**67 ADDRESSED, 45 VULNERABLE, 9 N/A, 1 conditional**" (line 5); st181's verbatim text ends `\n\nLet's br` (intake-2026-09-20.sysml:13), which the quote silently drops. — RESOLVES IF: "guards per `keel version`", "+1 conditional", and the st181 fragment marked with an ellipsis.

##### What survived

- C2 (empty quadrant) is verbatim in the evidence: line 30 "every tool that made verification deterministic gave up the persistent work item; every tool that kept the work item let the agent say when it was done." Verified across lenses A, C, D, E (lines 271, 573, 740, 881).
- C3 holds: mechanisms 1–16 (lines 36–53) each name a running system; nothing in §3 lacks a precedent except the combination and W2.
- C4's numbers hold exactly (122 threads, 35 in theme 1, "nobody" for heartbeat-credential renewal, kill-on-lease-loss, claim refusals, human budget: lines 34–35, 106, 365–366, 690, 850).
- §3.3's "rejected heartbeat → kill the process group" is a protocol obligation no tool has (line 690), and the direction states it as mandatory, not optional — correct; do not let a reducer make it advisory.
- The forks are honestly open; F1 recommendation (a) is the only answer the loudest thread family accepts (line 853 (1)).

##### VERDICT

ACCEPT. The direction survives; every finding above is a place it should claim more, cite better, or concede less. Findings 1, 3 and 4 are the ones that will decide whether the human is convinced (st189), and finding 1 is the one the pessimists will use if it stays unanswered.

### OPTIMIST-2 (value) — round 1

#### OPTIMIST-2 (value / ROI) — round 1

Method. I read all 667 Issue titles in `.tracking/issues.sysml`, `issues-claudeOpus5.sysml`, `issues-claudeFable5.sysml` (extracted to `scratchpad/issue_titles.txt`) and placed 292 of them into named classes by hand (`scratchpad/issue_classes.txt`, one class per issue, no duplicates). I read the 66 retro texts of sprints 700–759 (`scratchpad/retros700.txt`). Counts below cite those files. The other 375 titles are views/console/brief, decision governance, recall, routing-rig — the direction touches them lightly or not at all, and I say so in finding 5.

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] C1 is under-evidenced by a factor of ten, and the artifact never counts. It cites issues 082–085, 255/256, 543 and one external thread. The ledger holds 68 issues in the exact class the harvest removes (`R1-self-attestation` 27 + `R1-ceremony-post-D0425` 41), 27 more where the monitored actor modified its monitor (`R2`: 236 "An agent can disarm a blocking guard rule with no signed decision", 276, 334, 488, 496, 650), 21 hook misfires (`R5`) and 8 records eaten by the shell (`R3a`). That is 129 issues, 19% of the ledger, and 41 of them landed in the eleven days since D0425 (2026-09-10) across 115 sprints — one ceremony defect every 2.8 sprints. issue266 states the class in one line: "Every pass in this model is a testimony: of 5135 results, one recorded what produced it". — EVIDENCE: `scratchpad/issue_classes.txt`; `.engine/decisions/0425-*.sysml` `createdAt = "2026-09-10"`; `ls .tracking/delivery/sprint6[5-9]* sprint7[0-9][0-9]*` = 115. — RESOLVES IF: §2 C1 states the counted classes and the method, and names what harvest retires: the verifier/recorder apparatus (D0425, D0438, D0473, D0476, D0492, D0500–D0502, D0516, D0533, D0537, D0538; `check_receipt.py`, `check_report.py`, `verify_receipt.py`; two agent types; two skills).

2. [MAJOR] §3 has no payback order; eight points are presented flat "for approval", so the human is asked to approve a lease server and a retire ladder alongside the one point that pays. The first slice that pays for itself is §3.6 + gate-is-done + declared inputs/outputs, in-process, no daemon, no lease server: `keel step run <step>` materialises the declared inputs into a fresh worktree (keel already does `git worktree add` for audit-history and trend, retro 729), scrubs the environment, runs the agent as a child, harvests only the declared output paths, runs the step's bound gate (the existing `keel verify` ladder IS that gate), and writes the TestResult with input/output digests itself. Evidence it pays: 24 of the 60 sprint-700-series retros record a receipt, ladder or dispatch failure; the costs are named in seconds — "The ladder ran three times for one verdict" (retro 737, 1070 s); "The ladder was re-run for this sprint's receipt" (738, after a 97-minute standby); re-dispatches in 744, 745, 747; retro 742's run "takes 1053" seconds. At 8–18 minutes a re-run, the ceremony class has cost on the order of 5–8 hours of ladder time in eleven days, before the primary's turns reading receipts. — EVIDENCE: `scratchpad/retros700.txt` lines 131, 134, 146, 152, 155, 161. — RESOLVES IF: §3 opens with a "slice 1" paragraph naming exactly that set, what it retires, and that slices 2+ are gated on a trigger.

3. [MAJOR] For a solo engineer on one machine, §3.1 (identity negotiation, session and run tokens), §3.3 beyond a wall-clock deadline plus kill-and-reap (server-revisable cadence, `quiet`, `retire_at`, a heartbeat in its own process), §3.4's poison-pill counter and unclaimed-ready fault, and §3.7 (runner version ramping/draining) are gold-plating: zero issues in 667 belong to a multi-runner-contention or runner-version class. The one concurrency defect, issue569 "A second touched run is not refused: two ladders raced on one receipt and reported reds no single run produces", is already controlled by the D0493 pid stub. The one lease-shaped defect is the harness's ten-minute cap (issue453, 469; retro 742), which a deadline and a reaper answer without a server. — EVIDENCE: `issue_classes.txt` (no such class exists); issue569/453/469 titles in `issue_titles.txt`. — RESOLVES IF: those points are marked "deferred; trigger = a second runner or a second machine enrols", and st186's "(ii) where feasible" is read as permitting exactly that deferral.

4. [MAJOR] The artifact never says what the direction leaves untouched, and st189 asks to be convinced — a conviction with a hidden denominator is the shape issue383 already named ("The report asked for ratification while framing it as a choice"). Untouched by any runner protocol: 69 adoption/init/migrate/release issues (`N2`: 089, 090, 272, 313, 609–617, 622, 645, 647), 32 parser/model issues (`N1`: 102, 605, 636, 644), 42 suite/build/CI issues (`N4`), plus roughly 80 view/console/brief titles I did not class. That is more than 40% of the ledger and all of it is keel-the-product; if F3 lands "separate project", it stays behind with a smaller team. — EVIDENCE: `issue_classes.txt` N rows. — RESOLVES IF: §2 gains one line: "this direction does not touch N of the ledger's defects, which are keel's parser, adoption and view surfaces", with the counts.

5. [MINOR] V6 concedes the whole shell class; the ledger splits it. Records eaten by the shell (`R3a`: 255, 256, 315, 320, 322, 543, 546, 641 — 8) vanish under harvest because the agent never invokes `keel record`; the harvester writes. Shell-writes-code (`N3b`: 013, 094, 126, 366, 564 — 5, plus the heredoc denials in retros 741 and 742) stays unless a CONSTRAINED step has no shell. issue322's title is the argument for fixing the class rather than the site: "The prose-by-argument trap fired a fourth time, because the fix was applied per-command not per-class". — RESOLVES IF: V6 is split into the removed half and the conceded half.

6. [MINOR] st182's stated motive — "how many EOL bugs again?" — is three issues of 667 (076, 351, 478) and one sprint (560). The artifact rightly does not lean on it but should say so, or the human buys the direction for a reason the ledger refutes. — EVIDENCE: `git grep -i 'CRLF|line ending|EOL'` over issue titles; `P-eol` row. — RESOLVES IF: §0 or §2 notes the count beside st182.

7. [MINOR] §3.5 buries the cheapest live win. "stores the provider-returned model id (mismatch fails the step)" answers issue498, open today: "Fifteen landed records name claudeOpus5 while the session's model was Fable 5 - the session actor binding does not follow the model". It costs one field in the harvest receipt. — RESOLVES IF: it moves into slice 1.

8. [MINOR] §3.4 `awaiting-human` and §3.8's indicators are near-free and already owed: issue112, 552, 661 ("three Needs waited seventeen days"), 150 ("70 items were waiting on the human"), 597 (309 owed). `authority-queue` is the computation; the step state and the two indicators are a lens change, not protocol work. The cap belongs with UNCONSTRAINED steps, later. — RESOLVES IF: the indicators are placed in slice 1 and the cap in slice 2.

##### What survived

- C1's mechanism is right, and the repository says so in its own words about patching the instance: retro 722 — "this is the third receipt to gain the running form separately (issue399 suite, issue468 touched, issue565 verify) - each fix was scoped to the file that fired, and the file left out was the one that fired next." Retro 750: "issue490 recurring for the third time (sprints 676, 745, 750), resolver dcVerifierReceiptOutcomeIsRenderedNotTyped still open". The reducer's "just fix the receipts" has been tried nine times in eleven days.
- The write path already refuses at the write (`members/keel-write/src/write.rs:88`, `ReceiptOwed`: "An AI-judged method=test result records WHAT WAS RUN as its `// RAN:` receipt"), so harvest is the next step of an existing control, not a new philosophy.
- Gate-is-done has a seed: 13 `checkedBy` bindings across 269 process steps (`git grep -c checkedBy -- .engine/processes`). issue336 named the gap in 2026-09-01: "A process is a document with an optional guard - steps bind to no predicate". The direction is that issue's resolver.
- C5 holds: nothing in slice 1 touches the model, the edges, acceptance or the guards; it changes who writes the TestResult.
- Harvest-only-declared-outputs must stay in slice 1 against the reducer, because R2's 27 issues need the agent to have no write path into `.engine`, hooks or `.tracking` except the harvest; "run the gate afterwards" alone leaves 236/276/334/650 open.

#### PEDANT-2 (consistency) — round 1

##### Findings

(5) An opt-in keel run <process> orchestrator is DEFERRED to last ... only alongside library mode, and only with its own code inside the signed keystone." No `#Supersede`/`#SupersedeClause` targets d0209 (`git grep` over .engine/.tracking: none). CLAUDE.md §1.4: "'We won't do X' is a Decision" — D0209 is that Decision. Worse, D0209's context (line 18) rejected the inversion on a specific finding: "an agent-authored orchestrator is still agent-editable, and 'agents add their own processes' institutionalizes exactly that" (issue236: "the monitor was modifiable by the monitored"). st182's "a process is defined which develop processes" is that clause, and §3 says nothing about who may author a process or whether an UNCONSTRAINED step's proposals may touch the meta-process. — RESOLVES IF: the artifact names D0209, states which clauses it supersedes (whole, or cl.5's "deferred/opt-in" via `#SupersedeClause`), and adds to §3 the lock D0209 asked for: process definitions live inside the signed keystone, authored by a human-signed CR only, never a step output.

2. [MAJOR] §3.8 collides with D0204 clause 2. `.engine/decisions/0204-pull-oversight.sysml:19`: "(2) Nothing gates or throttles the AI's work on the human's attention cadence - the D0200 clause 5 process-change throttle is removed". §3.8: "human-minutes per landed step are computed indicators; a cap stops assigning new UNCONSTRAINED steps while the queue is full" — a throttle keyed to the human's queue, plus a metric OF the human, against their words in D0204's context ("I don't need governance, you do"). V2 treats the cap as a virtue. Also inconsistent internally: under D0312 B (accepted, `d0312AcceptR1`, 2026-09-05) AI-examined `demo/analyze/inspect` passes land `proposed` on CONSTRAINED steps too, so the queue is loaded by both classes but §3.8 caps only UNCONSTRAINED. — RESOLVES IF: 3.8 is reframed as an AI-side WIP limit on unjudged proposals (a fact about the work, any step class, no human-time metric), or a `#SupersedeClause` on D0204 cl.2 is authored and HELD as a process change (D0337).

3. [MAJOR] "A step is done when its gate is green, never when the agent says so" (§1) has no executor in §3. §3.6 harvests "only declared outputs" then makes "one write with completion"; nothing says who RUNS the gate, or that a TestResult is never an admissible declared output. If it is, D0312 B ("an AI-judged demo with a REPLAYABLE receipt stays a pass", 0312:18) lets the agent's own verdict re-enter through the harvest and C1's "structural defect" is reinstated one hop later. The runner review's row 8 (`docs/reviews/runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:95`) says "Done = the step's gate run by the runner"; §3 dropped the clause. — RESOLVES IF: §3 gains a Gate item: the gate is executed by the server (or runner under the run token) over harvested outputs; TestResults/receipts are never declared outputs; a `proposed`-tier gate result routes the step to `awaiting-human`, not done.

4. [MAJOR] "Text is truth ... State = COMPUTED, never stored" (CLAUDE.md §1.1) versus a server-side mutable lease. §3.3's deadline is "revisable on every heartbeat reply", the run token is re-issued per heartbeat, §3.7 is a "server state machine" — none of these are text in git, and the artifact never says which store is authoritative or what happens to leases across a server crash (the review's own thread 24, Dagger). keel already has a claim with a different clock: `keel claim` ("liveness is computed", `.engine/cli/commands.sysml:29`) and `members/keel-guards/src/acceptance.rs:687-689` — "Git ancestry is the clock (D0013)", `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS`. Two claim mechanisms with two clocks is the D0105 dual-truth class. — RESOLVES IF: the artifact declares the server's runtime store ephemeral and non-authoritative, lists the facts that MUST land as text (claim, expiry, refusal, receipt, refusal-after-expiry), and states whether the lease supersedes `keel claim` or is its server-side projection.

5. [MAJOR] F3 is pre-resolved by §1's "The human's only surface is a browser". As a keel SUBSYSTEM, `keel accept`, `keel judge-set`, `keel record ...` (commands.sysml:13-23, 77 guards per `keel version`) remain human-facing CLI, so "only surface" is false unless dozens of `CliCommand` facts get `#Supersede` edges (D0271/§1.8) — an unmentioned retirement. As a separate project it is true trivially. — RESOLVES IF: §1 reads "the only human surface OF THE RUNNER SUBSYSTEM", or F3 is recommended with the CLI-retirement cost stated.

F1's (a)/(b) are also not exclusive — a mechanism versus a statement of accepted loss; (a) still owes (b)'s residual statement. — RESOLVES IF: F1 is restated as "worktree + scrubbed environment, AND the residual loss class stated", with §3.6 and V1 naming the same location.

An outbound-only runner cannot be pulled from; the review's own row-9 evidence is runner WRITES ("Argo wait-sidecar writes `WorkflowTaskResult`", "GitLab final `PUT /jobs/:id`", line 27). The fencing in §3.6 depends on which side holds the outputs if the runner dies between exit and harvest. — RESOLVES IF: 3.6 says the runner uploads outputs by digest under the run token and the server admits/fences them; "pull" means the server reading its own store before teardown.

8. [MAJOR] Mechanism vs policy. The review's rule for §4 (line 84): "a mechanism is named only if it is a *structural* property of the protocol or the server ... never a rule the runner is asked to follow." §3 mixes them without marking: 3.2 "never retried", 3.3 "heartbeat runs in its own process" and "the runner kills the step's process group", 3.7 "no self-update" are runner-asked; only 3.6's fenced harvest is the server-side counterpart of the kill. — RESOLVES IF: every §3 clause is tagged server-enforced or runner-asked, and each runner-asked clause names the server check that detects its violation (e.g. a write after expiry = recorded refusal).

10. [MINOR] Citations. The D14 quote is in the 2026-09-21 runner-daemon review (lines 71, 822), not "the 2026-09-20 landscape review". "66+ guards": `keel version` prints "guards: 77 (68 hard-blocking, 9 warning-only)". 11. [MINOR] C5 is a promise, not a testable claim; nothing observable falsifies "keeps what they said is best-in-class". Schema/process changes = Change Request + human sign-off" and D0209 cl.2 (a marked Decision per enforcement-surface path). — RESOLVES IF: §3 says a design approval is not a CR and lists the CRs it implies.
