### PEDANT-3 (mechanisms) — round 1

#### PEDANT-3 (mechanisms) — round 1

Method: each §3 point walked as inputs/state → wrong outcome, checked against `docs/reviews/runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md` Appendix E (lines 901–1064) for whether the cited protocol does what §3 says it copies. Repository at HEAD f365cb7a; nothing written under it.

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] Run-token rotation on heartbeat races the harvest — EVIDENCE: §3.3 "The run token is renewed on each accepted heartbeat"; §3.6 "fenced against expired or superseded tokens". Scenario: heartbeat N is accepted and its reply carries token T(N+1); the reply is lost or arrives after the runner has already begun harvest under T(N). Wrong outcome: the harvest is refused as superseded, the lease expires, the step is re-leased and a completed run is executed twice. No cited protocol rotates the job credential: GitHub's job OAuth "lives for the run or the job timeout (6h) plus 10 minutes" (App. E ¶3), Buildkite's per-job `Token` "expires at job finish" (App. E row 1); the review's own §1 row 4 says "nobody renews the runner credential on heartbeat" — §3 copies an invention, not a practice — RESOLVES IF: the run token is a fixed identifier minted at acquire and its validity is COMPUTED from the lease record (last accepted `received_at` + `lease_seconds` + a declared harvest grace). That is keel's own invariant (state computed, never stored) applied to the credential, and it closes E9 without a rotation race.

2. [MAJOR] "Harvest is a server pull after exit" is copied from nothing and needs an inbound channel the field forbids — EVIDENCE: §1 practice row 9 cites Argo ("wait-sidecar writes `WorkflowTaskResult`, controller reads"), SQS `DeleteMessage`, GitLab `PUT /jobs/:id` — all three are client WRITES the server then reads; "server pull" appears only as the lens's suggestion (App. E "What argues against our design (a)"). Consensus 1 of the same review: "Pull, not push. Every modern daemon long-polls or streams outbound" — a server cannot open a connection to a runner behind NAT. Second scenario: §3.6 "Sandboxes and worktrees are reaped on lease expiry" plus a step that exits at its deadline → the harvest window is zero → Argo #4565 ("podGC deleted the pod before the controller read completion", E22) reproduced by contract — RESOLVES IF: harvest is a runner-initiated digest upload (REAPI `FindMissingBlobs`/`BatchUpdateBlobs` shape) under the run token, followed by ONE `complete`; the server's "pull" is semantic (verify digests against the declared output set), not transport; reaping happens on `complete`-ack or lease expiry + declared harvest grace, whichever is later; the runner daemon (long-lived, §3.7) serves the upload — the run token dies with the job, the daemon does not, and the text should say which "dies".

3. [MAJOR] Unanswered is not rejected: the actual Nomad #2185 partition case has no runner-side rule, and the proxy's location is undefined — EVIDENCE: §3.3 "A rejected heartbeat → the runner kills the step's process group". Scenario: network partition. The server expires the lease and re-leases to runner B; runner A's heartbeats get no reply at all, so nothing is "rejected", nothing is killed, and A's sandbox keeps calling the egress proxy. If the proxy is runner-side holding the key (§3.5 does not say), A spends against a budget the partitioned proxy cannot check; E16's disposition demands "keel must fence expired run tokens at the harvest, not trust the daemon to stop" — harvest fencing saves the tree but not the bill. Variant: the heartbeat process (its "own process") dies while the step lives — the same hole — RESOLVES IF: (a) the runner keeps a local lease deadline from the last accepted `received_at` + `lease_seconds` minus the echoed skew, and treats an unanswered deadline as `expired` (kill); (b) the proxy checks the run token's lease on every forwarded call, so a dead lease starves the agent inside CONSTRAINED regardless of the runner's health. Two independent enforcement points, one of them server-side.

4. [MAJOR] `stalled` has no progress unit; for an LLM step the case it must catch is the one it cannot — EVIDENCE: §3.3 "carries progress", §3.4 "`stalled` (heartbeats without progress)"; nowhere is "progress" defined. Scenario A (wedged): a child blocked on stdin — D0491's own hang class — emits nothing; `stalled` catches it only if the runner observes something concrete. Scenario B (D19, 96M tokens): the looping agent makes a proxy call every few seconds — by any plausible observable that IS progress, so `stalled` never fires and the run is stopped only by the ceiling, after the spend. Wrong outcome: the review's row 3 claims D19 is "answered by construction"; it is answered by the ceiling, and §3.5 does not say the ceiling is mandatory — RESOLVES IF: progress is defined as runner-observed monotonic counters (accepted proxy calls, bytes written under declared output paths, child exits), declared per step type; `stalled` = none advanced for N intervals; the ceiling is mandatory per step type and named as the D19 control.

5. [MAJOR] Cost metering at the proxy undercounts in three named cases — EVIDENCE: §3.5 "meters tokens and cost against the step's ceiling — cost is a second lease bound". Scenario 1: a streaming response is cut at the ceiling; the provider bills generated tokens, the `usage` block arrives only in the final stream event → never counted. Scenario 2: a batch/async operation — one submission call, consumption via a later retrieval the proxy may never see. Scenario 3: cached-token pricing differs from the proxy's flat count. Wrong outcome: the "lease bound" is exceeded by up to one maximal call per concurrent stream, and the receipt's cost is wrong — RESOLVES IF: metering reads the provider's `usage` fields (input, output, cache-read, cache-write) with a pricing table on the step type; a stream aborted before `usage` is charged its request's `max_tokens`; a request whose `max_tokens` would overrun the remaining budget is refused pre-flight; async/batch operations are outside the CONSTRAINED operation set unless declared with a metered retrieval.

6. [MAJOR] The request body is the egress; provider-side tools are arbitrary egress through the declared host — EVIDENCE: §3.5 "enforces per-step declared providers and operations, names the blocked host" — host-and-operation granularity. App. D row 3 disposition, verbatim: "VULNERABLE: the proxy's one key is itself an ambient credential - the agent can use the declared provider for anything that key allows, so key scope per step matters more than the proxy." Scenario: a CONSTRAINED step declares its LLM provider; the agent puts a server-side web-fetch/web-search tool in the `tools` array and composes a URL whose query string carries input content; the provider fetches it from its own infrastructure. The proxy saw one allowed host. Second scenario: `model` enforcement is post-hoc ("stores the provider-returned model id (mismatch fails the step)") — the money is spent before the failure — RESOLVES IF: the proxy enforces a body policy per step type — allowed `model` (refused pre-flight), allowed tool types with server-side network tools denied by default, no provider code-execution containers with egress — and the denial names the field as it names a host. Concede in text that input content reaching the DECLARED provider is the intended flow; the provider's data terms are the trust boundary, and the design should say so rather than imply the proxy prevents it.

7. [MAJOR] F1(a) "worktree snapshotted before start" is not isolation, and "harvests only declared outputs" filters what the server SEES, not what the agent DID — EVIDENCE: §4 F1(a), §1 "lets the server harvest only declared outputs". Scenario: an UNCONSTRAINED agent in a git worktree runs `git config core.hooksPath` or writes `.git/hooks/post-checkout` — a worktree shares `.git/config` and `.git/hooks` with the developer's main checkout; the proposal diff is clean; the hook fires on the developer's next checkout. Or it writes `~/.gitconfig` `[credential]`, `~/.ssh/config`, `~/.claude/settings.json`. No deterministic step in the design inspects anything outside the declared outputs, so the outcome is invisible by construction — RESOLVES IF: F1(a) becomes a clone (or `--separate-git-dir`) with a throwaway `HOME`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`; the whole scrubbed environment is snapshotted and diffed after exit; any change outside the declared output set FAILS the step (in-toto MATCH applied in both directions — unexpected products are a violation, not noise).

8. [MAJOR] `awaiting-human` does not say what happens to the lease, the sandbox, or the run token — EVIDENCE: §3.4 "`awaiting-human` (not leasable)"; review row 16 "copied from Cursor's fix as a state". Scenario: the agent asks a question mid-step. If the lease persists, the heartbeat reply vocabulary (`cancel`, `quiet`, `terminate`, `retire_at`, cadence) has no `answer`, so the sandbox idles at the reviewer's pace — D18's shape. If the lease ends, the answer must become an input to a re-run — deterministic, but unstated. Race: the human answers in the browser while a stale runner still holds the old run token; nothing says the transition superseded it, so an old harvest can land after the answer — RESOLVES IF: entering `awaiting-human` releases the lease and supersedes the run token (a state transition is a fencing event), the sandbox is reaped, and the human's answer is a declared input to the next run of the same step.

9. [MINOR] The poison-pill counter attributes every expiry to the step — EVIDENCE: §3.4 "after N re-leases raises an Issue". Scenario: one runner host at 70 GB RAM (D21) or a sandbox that fails to start (C17) expires three different steps; each step is poisoned, the runner is not. The flaky-provider case the dispatch asked about is `failed` with harvested error output, not a re-lease, so the counter as written does not hit it — say so — RESOLVES IF: the counter is by cause (`expired` / `startup-failed` / `failed`), startup failures never count against the step, and N expiries by one runner in a window poison the runner.

10. [MINOR] A downward `lease_seconds` revision with an unchanged cadence expires an honest runner — EVIDENCE: §3.3 "both server-chosen and revisable on every heartbeat reply"; App. E thread 13 (Temporal, heartbeat at 5 min against a 5-min timeout) and its disposition "server-dictated at <= 1/3 of the lease" — RESOLVES IF: the server holds `heartbeat_seconds ≤ lease_seconds / 3` as an invariant and a revision takes effect from the next accepted heartbeat, never retroactively.

11. [MINOR] Hitting the step type's maximum lease destroys the evidence — EVIDENCE: §3.3 "Maximum lease ... declared on the step type"; §3.6 "reaped on lease expiry"; App. E thread 27's disposition "dies at hour 12 with no recovery path" is answered only by making the cap declarable, not recoverable — RESOLVES IF: at the cap the runner kills, then harvests as `failed` (outputs retained as the Issue's evidence), then reaps.

##### What survived

- **Claim (§3.2)**: atomic `reserve`→`acquire`, coded conflict, readiness recomputed at acquire — matches GitLab 201/204/409 and Paperclip's "never retry a 409" (App. E row 2, review §1 row 3), and it is keel's computed-state invariant applied to dispatch.
- **Cadence and skew (§3.3)**: server-chosen `lease_seconds`/`heartbeat_seconds` revisable per reply, `sent_at`/`received_at` echo — verbatim Buildkite (`ping_interval`, `heartbeat_interval`, `received_at`) and Nomad `HeartbeatTTL` (App. E ¶1, ¶5). "Heartbeat in its own process" matches Buildkite's agent-process/job-child split.
- **Rejected heartbeat is fatal** — Hatchet #4824 (lens B row 14) supports it exactly; the gap is the unanswered case (finding 3), not the rejected one.
- **Retire (§3.7)**: Current/Ramping/Draining/Drained, in-flight pinned, no self-update — Temporal Worker Versioning and Restate as cited; GitHub #246 is the counter-example it avoids.
- **Startup as a timed phase, denial names the host, human-attention indicators** — each is a "nobody does" row the review earned, and the indicator shape fits D0088 (monitored, computed).

##### VERDICT

OBJECT (minimal flip set: 1, 2, 3, 6, 7)

### REDUCER — round 1

#### REDUCER — round 1

Test applied to every element: does the human's stated goal (st181/st182: tool owns the task; deterministic control; narrow, parallel agents with definite inputs/outputs; keel still detects discrepancies and lapsed guards) fail without it? Checked against HEAD f365cb7a, not the artifact.

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] The direction omits that keel already built the tool-owns-the-run inversion in single-process form, and that it was never used — EVIDENCE: `members/keel-serve/src/launcher.rs:3-5` "WHAT A RUN IS: a console-launched, human-approved process instance executing in fresh bounded context (`claude -p`) ... the dirty-tree refusal and snapshot at prepare, and the post-run gate + records at finish." (D0181 "the console is the launch boundary for routine EXECUTE and RECORD work", D0182 run records + post-run gate + `judgedAgainst` + diff routed to human "UNCONDITIONALLY, green or red"). `ls .tracking/runs | wc -l` = 0; `ls .keel/runs | wc -l` = 0; `grep -c '"launch' .keel/metrics/hooks.jsonl` = 0. Launcher.rs:18: "Worktree-per-run stays deferred behind its named `DoR` (P5.6)." — RESOLVES IF: §1/§2 cite D0181/D0182, state why a browser-launched, gate-judged run existed for months and was never taken, and name what a daemon adds that finishing P5.6 (worktree-per-run) does not. Without this the panel cannot tell a new direction from an unfinished one.

2. [MAJOR] §3.2–3.3 (claim, lease, heartbeat) re-invent `keel claim`, whose design already answers C4's "stored flag" defect by construction — EVIDENCE: `.engine/schema/core/work.sysml:55-57` "NO `status`, NO `expiresAt`, NO `released` FLAG, and that absence is the design. Liveness is COMPUTED: a claim is live if it is the newest claim on its item and its age against git-derived time is under the expiry window." `members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:15` `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS: i64 = 2`; holder = earliest `claimedAt`, tie-broken by UUID, so "every clone agrees without talking" (work.sysml:66). `commands.sysml:29` `keel claim <item> | --list | --mine` "liveness is computed". What §3.3 adds is a shorter window and a kill-on-loss obligation. — RESOLVES IF: the lease is written as a delta to `Claim` (a second, shorter expiry for a run claim; heartbeat = re-claim against the same `claimedAgainst`; kill on stale), not as a new token protocol with `reserve/acquire`, signed run tokens and server-chosen cadence.

3. [MAJOR] Sandbox materialisation is a solved local problem; no daemon is needed to get it — EVIDENCE: `members/keel-guards/src/history.rs:11-12` "Each commit is checked out into a throwaway worktree and the real `validate` + `guard` code runs against it. Nothing is trusted"; `verdict_for` does `git worktree add --detach` and checks `.engine`/`.tracking` exist before any verdict (history.rs:48-56). The Claude Code Agent tool this repo already runs under offers `isolation: "worktree"` per subagent. — RESOLVES IF: §3.5–3.6 "materialise declared inputs / harvest declared outputs" is stated as: worktree at HEAD (history.rs pattern), scrubbed environment, `git diff --name-only` after exit filtered to the step's declared outputs, any undeclared path in the diff = the run is refused. That is the whole harvest; REAPI-shaped digests are a later hardening.

4. [MAJOR] The real gap the goal fails without is not ownership but that steps have no typed I/O and almost no bound check — EVIDENCE: `.engine/schema/core/process.sysml:26-34`: `triggerCondition : String`, `actionText : String`, `producedArtifact : String`, `checkedBy : String [0..1]` "Optional: a step with none is judgment". `grep -c ": ProcessStep" .engine/processes/*.sysml` = 191; `grep -c checkedBy` = 13. "A step is done when its gate is green" is unrepresentable for 178 of 191 steps today; a daemon leasing them cannot compute done either. st182's "what goes into or out of keel? ... out of an agent?" is a schema CR (typed `inputs`/`outputs` on `ProcessStep`, `checkedBy` mandatory for a runnable step, one guard) and it is the load-bearing change. — RESOLVES IF: §1 names this schema CR as the first and central deliverable, ahead of any §3 item.

5. [MAJOR] The egress proxy (§3.5, C3) serves a threat the human never stated, and the direction's own evidence concedes it does not close the hole — EVIDENCE: none of st181–st189 mentions credentials or exfiltration; `docs/reviews/runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md` row 5: "the proxy's one key is itself an ambient credential" (their own lens C). A CONSTRAINED step still needs a model endpoint, so a key exists somewhere reachable from the run. — RESOLVES IF: the proxy is moved out of the direction into a named later hardening, and the direction relies on env scrubbing + harvest-by-declared-output for the deterministic-control goal.

6. [MAJOR] "Run in parallel" is delivered by worktree-per-run plus mechanisms keel already has, and the daemon adds nothing to it — EVIDENCE: `srDcPerActorWriteTargets` (`.tracking/requirements/distributed-collaboration-requirements.sysml:73`, satisfying `nConcurrentAuthoringSafe`): per-actor files so concurrent writes never collide; claims computed without coordination (finding 2). The one thing that blocks parallelism today is D0182: "launcher concurrency pins to 1 with single-writer-per-tree during a run". Lifting that = one worktree per run with a per-run actor id. — RESOLVES IF: §1 states that parallelism comes from worktree-per-run, and lists the daemon only for remote/unattended runners.

7. [MINOR] C1's issue citations are half wrong — EVIDENCE: `.tracking/issues.sysml`: issue082 "`work done` and `requirement verified` are DISCONNECTED"; issue083 "An acceptance event with an EMPTY procedureText passes every guard". Neither is "CLAUDE.md rules ignored"; 084 (D0052 ignored) and 085 (findings escaped to CLAUDE.md prose) are. — RESOLVES IF: C1 cites 084/085/255/256/543 only.

8. [MINOR] The "versioned JSON protocol with thin CLI clients" already exists under another name — EVIDENCE: CLAUDE.md §1.8 "CLI surface is an authored fact (D0271). Every command/lens is a `CliCommand`"; 92 `: CliCommand` parts in `.engine/cli/commands.sysml`; §1.9 "CLI/JSON is authority". `keel show status` reports the engine pin. — RESOLVES IF: §1 says the runner protocol IS the existing JSON CLI surface, versioned by the engine pin, and the only new verb is `run`.

9. [MINOR] Nesting, retire states (§3.7), poison-pill counter (§3.4) and human-budget cap (§3.8) do not bear on the goal — EVIDENCE: landscape 2026-09-20 §3: "Nesting has no prior art ... Prototype nesting last." Poison-pill and retire states presuppose re-leasing and multiple runner versions, which exist only if a daemon exists. Human budget: `keel show authority-queue` and `indicators` already exist; a depth indicator is one TOML view (D0088: "no defensible boundary → stays indicator"), not a protocol clause. — RESOLVES IF: these are moved to "after the minimal plan proves out".

##### The minimal plan (what the reducer would build instead)

One in-repo change, three parts, no daemon, no lease protocol, no proxy:
(a) Schema CR: `ProcessStep` gains typed `inputs[*]`/`outputs[*]` (item names or paths) and a `runnable` marker that makes `checkedBy` mandatory; guard `runnable-step-has-check`.
(b) `keel run <process>.<step>`: `keel claim` the step (existing); `git worktree add --detach` at HEAD (history.rs pattern); spawn `claude -p` with the launcher's existing hook pin and a prompt built only from the step's declared inputs, with a wall-clock timeout (launcher already records `timedOut`); on exit, diff the worktree, refuse the run if any path outside declared `outputs` changed, apply the declared outputs, run `checkedBy`, write one Test+TestResult with verdict `proposed` (D0312, `element.sysml:27`) under the run's actor; reap the worktree.
(c) The console's existing approve queue shows the run; `keel judge-set` is the human's verdict. Parallel = N concurrent `keel run` invocations, each its own worktree and claim.

What the direction loses by it, honestly: (i) remote runners on other machines (st186 chose (ii) "where feasible") — the minimal plan is local; feasibility is exactly what it would establish first; (ii) credential isolation stronger than env scrubbing — a proxy is better against a hostile agent, but no human statement asked for it; (iii) unattended operation — someone or a cron invokes `keel run`; (iv) browser-only for the human is partial — launch and approve are already in `keel serve`, but `accept --words` and `judge-set` remain CLI verbs; st188 accepts a runner-facing CLI, so the loss is scope, not principle; (v) mid-run cancellation is timeout-only.

##### What survived

- C1's core: the self-reporting defect is structural. `delegated-ceremony.sysml` shows twelve Decisions (D0425, D0438, D0473, D0476, D0492, D0500–D0502, D0516, D0533, D0537, D0538) each patching a prompt-drawn role boundary that broke in a named sprint (647, 703, 723, 735, 741, 743). Boundaries held by prose fail; boundaries held by a worktree and a declared-output harvest cannot be talked past. The direction's instinct is right.
- "Done = the step's gate green, never the agent's sentence" survives; it is already the write-path rule (`write.rs` `ReceiptOwed`, `proposed` verdicts) and the minimal plan keeps it.
- C5 survives: everything the human called best-in-class is reused unchanged by both plans.
- Fork F1 dissolves in the minimal plan's favour of (a): a run in a scrubbed worktree with the full tool set IS the "unconstrained" class; V1 stops being a vulnerability.
- The human-attention indicator (3.8) is a good idea at the right altitude — as an indicator, not a protocol clause.

##### VERDICT

OBJECT (minimal flip set: 1, 2, 4, 5 — name the existing launcher/claim/worktree mechanisms and state the direction as their delta; make the ProcessStep I/O + mandatory-check schema CR the first deliverable; defer the proxy)

### SE (kernel) — round 1

#### SE (systems engineer, kernel) — round 1

##### Kernel invariants the direction must hold (derived from st181–st188 + the artifact)

| # | Invariant (implementation-free) | In artifact? | Form | Violation test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I-1 | No agent works a step keel did not assign it; every write to the tree names the run that produced it | §3.2 "every write carries the run id" | mechanism | a write with no live run id, or an expired one, lands |
| I-2 | No step is done except by a gate result computed by something other than the step's agent; an agent's own pass is testimony | §1 "done when its gate is green, never when the agent says so" | invariant (good) | a harvested output containing a `TestResult` pass makes the step done |
| I-3 | An agent reads nothing not declared as an input to its step | §1 "materialises exactly the declared inputs" | mechanism | sandbox holds a file outside the input digest set (in-toto MATCH is the test the artifact already names) |
| I-4 | Nothing an agent produced reaches the model except declared outputs, via harvest | §1, §3.6 | mechanism | a write to the model from inside a running step succeeds |
| I-5 | No credential the agent can read outlives the step (CONSTRAINED: it reads none) | §3.1/3.3/3.5 tokens, proxy | mechanism | any token minted for a run is accepted on any endpoint after expiry |
| I-6 | "Running" is never a stored fact: a step runs iff a live lease exists; at most one holder | §3.3 lease/heartbeat seconds | mechanism (the invariant is CLAUDE.md §1.1) | kill the runner, pass the lease: step not ready again; two `acquire` both succeed |
| I-7 | No human-judgment record originates from a runner or agent (K6/K6a) | not for the new topology | absent | `accept` posted under a run or session token succeeds |
| I-8 | Every verdict is re-derivable from committed truth alone (K15) | §3.6 REAPI receipt | mechanism | delete the server database; a step's done state cannot be recomputed from the tree |
| I-9 | No dead runner, proxy, or heartbeat passes silently (K2) | §3.4 unclaimed-step fault, §3.7 idle reason | mechanism, adequate | an unclaimed ready step past its interval raises nothing |
| I-10 | keel's own assignment never grows the human's queue past a declared bound | §3.8 | mechanism | queue at cap, ready UNCONSTRAINED step still leasable |

##### Findings

1. [BLOCKER] I-2 has no mechanism: nothing in §3 says a **declared output may never be a verdict record**. If `.tracking/` (or any TestResult/gate-result form) is a legal declared output, the agent harvests its own pass and C1's class survives intact under new plumbing. — EVIDENCE: §3.6 harvests "only declared outputs" with no exclusion; today's refusal is `WriteError::ReceiptOwed` at `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:87-89` and the D0425 verifier/recorder split (`.engine/processes/delegated-ceremony.sysml`), both of which the artifact says the direction replaces. — RESOLVES IF: §3.6 states I-2 as an invariant ("a harvest never writes a `TestResult`, gate result or acceptance; the step's gate is run by the server after harvest and is the only completion write") with test T2: a step whose declared outputs include a pass result is refused at declaration time, and a harvested pass file is quarantined, not written.

2. [BLOCKER] The direction introduces three controllers (server/scheduler, runner daemon, egress proxy) and at least five control actions (`assign`, `reserve/acquire`, `heartbeat` reply incl. `cancel`/`terminate`, `harvest`, `retire`) with no hazard analysis, and the loss it concedes (V1: host, `.env`, `gh` session) has no Loss or Hazard to sit under. — EVIDENCE: `.tracking/architecture/engine-control-structure.sysml:16-50` authors exactly seven controllers (Human, Agent, Hooks, CommitGate, CI, Remote, Console); `engine-safety.sysml` EL1–EL4 are all losses of *recorded truth*; EHZ1–EHZ9 contain no hazard for "an agent acts on the host outside the model" or "a credential is exfiltrated from a step". The `stpa-currency` guard (`.engine/docs/guards.md:113`) walks computed control actions — none of these exist yet to walk. — RESOLVES IF: the direction adds EL5 (host or credential loss through an agent's execution) and hazards for each new control action before any Decision, and names UCA candidates at least for: `terminate` PROVIDED mid-write (what is harvested after a cancel is undefined in §3), `assign` PROVIDED with an input set that does not close (I-3), `harvest` PROVIDED from a superseded runner version (§3.7).

3. [MAJOR] Dual truth: the artifact never says which facts live in git and which in the server. Leases and tokens are rightly ephemeral, but §3.6 receipts, §3.4 poison-pill counts, and claim *refusals* "a human can read" are durable facts with no stated home. — EVIDENCE: CLAUDE.md §1.1 "Text is truth"; D0105 one home per fact; K15 (`production-readiness-requirements.sysml:132`) "re-derivable from committed truth alone, independent of any hook having run". §3 mentions git zero times. — RESOLVES IF: restate as I-8 with test T8 (drop the server store; recompute every step's state from the tree + receipts committed with the harvest) and a one-line partition: "committed: receipt, verdict, refusal record; server-only: lease, token, cadence".

4. [MAJOR] I-7 is missing for the new topology. Today K6 rests on the write layer refusing AI-kind actors and a TTY channel check (`srK06Corr1`, `.tracking/critiques.sysml:3176`). With "thin CLI clients" on a "versioned JSON protocol", what stops a runner client presenting a human `judged_by`? issue204 already names this as name-spoofable; a network protocol widens it. — EVIDENCE: K6a statement at `production-readiness-requirements.sysml:243`; §3.1 defines runner identity only. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1 adds "a run or session token can never author a human-judgment record; human judgments enter only through the browser session's identity" with test T7 (accept under a run token → refused, ledgered).

5. [MAJOR] §3 defines a parallel step model while C5 claims engine reuse. keel already has `ProcessStep` with `checkedBy` — a step done iff a named guard/rule predicate holds — and guard `step-check-resolves` fails a dead check (`.engine/schema/core/process.sysml:25-34`). "Done when its gate is green" is not new; typed inputs/outputs ARE new and `schema/core` is frozen (CLAUDE.md §1.5). — EVIDENCE: as cited; the artifact names neither `ProcessStep` nor a CR. — RESOLVES IF: §3 binds to `EngineProcess::ProcessStep` (+`checkedBy` as the gate) and states the schema CR for `declaredInput`/`declaredOutput`, or states why a second step model is not dual truth.

6. [MAJOR] UNCONSTRAINED steps violate I-3, I-4 and I-5 by construction, and the artifact does not say which invariants that class relaxes. F1(a) (worktree snapshot) addresses I-3 only; V1 concedes I-5 is broken. — EVIDENCE: §1 "full-tool agent in a real checkout"; §5 V1. — RESOLVES IF: the class table states per invariant HOLDS / RELAXED, and the one invariant UNCONSTRAINED must keep is stated: "its outputs are inputs to a CONSTRAINED step and never the model" (the artifact already implies it — write it as I-4').

7. [MINOR] Mechanisms standing in for requirements — restate each: §3.3 `lease_seconds`/`heartbeat_seconds`/`sent_at`/`received_at`/own-process heartbeat → I-6 (the clock-skew echo serves theme 7 diagnostics only); §3.3 "runner kills the process group" → the server cannot enforce it; the enforceable invariant is "no output of a lapsed lease is harvested" (§3.6 fencing already does this — the kill is a spend courtesy, say so); §3.5 stored model id → an unstated I-11 "a receipt names the model that ran; a step type may pin one"; §3.1 three token tiers → I-5. Orphan candidates (serve no invariant about the work, belong in an implementation spec): §3.7 `current/ramping/draining/drained` (keep only "in-flight runs pinned, no self-update" → K12 definition-version), §3.3 `quiet`/`retire_at`, §3.1 `low/high` negotiation.

8. [MINOR] Citations: the D14 quote ("It governed nothing") is in the 2026-09-21 runner review (`docs/reviews/runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:71,822`), not the 09-20 harness review; issues 082/083 (`.tracking/issues.sysml:964,976`) are "work done / requirement verified disconnected" and "empty procedureText passes", not "CLAUDE.md rules ignored" (084/085 are); "66+ guards" is 77 catalogue rows (`.engine/docs/guards.md`). — RESOLVES IF: corrected.

##### What survived

- **C1's diagnosis holds as a class statement**, with one correction: the repo already has a structural (not prose) mitigation in D0425 verifier/recorder — but the *primary* dispatches it, so the actor that does the work still chooses whether the check runs. Moving dispatch to the server is a real removal of the class, iff finding 1 lands.
- **C4's four "nobody does" claims match their source** (`runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:34-35,89,99`); the source is the AI's own spike, so this is consistency, not independent confirmation.
- **I-6 is keel's founding invariant applied to liveness** — the lease design is a correct instantiation of "state computed, never stored"; only the wording is mechanistic.
- **I-9 is covered**: unclaimed-step fault, poison-pill Issue, idle-reason all give fail-loud shape consistent with K2.
- **I-10 is the one genuinely new invariant**, and it is consistent with D0204 (it binds keel's assignment, not the human).
- The `// RAN:` refusal exists where cited (`write.rs:87-89`); st181–st188 are quoted verbatim against `.tracking/intake/intake-2026-09-20.sysml`, `-21.sysml`.

##### VERDICT

OBJECT (minimal flip set: 1, 2, 3)

## Appendix R2 — panelist reports, round 2, verbatim

### PESSIMIST-1 (premise) — round 2

#### PESSIMIST-1 (premise) — round 2

##### Flip-set verification (round-1 findings 1–4)

**1 (D0425 = the direction in miniature) — RESOLVED in substance.** C1 now says "the inversion moves it into code, it does not abolish it", accepts the rebuttal by name, and names one control per moved defect (owed set computed from the declaration; inputs materialised from git objects; receipt written by keel). Citations hold: `WriteError::ReceiptOwed` is raised at `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:618` and its message at `:88` ("refusing to write: {what} is AI-judged (`{judge}`) and carries no --evidence"). D0425 `createdAt = "2026-09-10"`, so "eleven days since" is right to a day. One MINOR misquote: D0436 says "reported unenforceable-by-step, never refused", not "reported unenforced".

**2 (agent made the gate green by writing the evidence) — RENAMED, not resolved as written.** I-2's first clause is unsatisfiable against the design's own §3.1.5. I-2: "a step's declared outputs never intersect the read set of its gate". §3.1.5: "keel runs the step's `checkedBy` over the harvested outputs". A gate that judges the outputs reads them; the read set intersects by definition, so the violation test cannot be written. The satisfiable clause is the second one (no `TestResult`, gate result, acceptance, or verdict-read path under `.tracking/`/`.engine/` is declarable) — and that clause I accept: it would have quarantined issue578's edit and issue488's D0447. What remains, one level down: when the gate is `cargo test`/a guard and the step's declared outputs include the tests, the agent still writes its own gate's evidence. Second gap in the same finding: "a refusal returned to the agent never carries an instruction to write" covers keel's refusal, but the instruction that drove issue578 comes from `.claude/settings.json`, which is TRACKED (`git ls-files .claude` lists it; `"Stop"` at line 57, `"SubagentStop"` at line 83, each running `hook stop`/`hook subagent-stop`). A `git clone` carries it into the working copy; `.keel/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:74`) so the hook falls through to PATH — and if a keel is on PATH the same six-block loop fires inside the clone. The harvest then fails the step (undeclared path), so the tree is safe; but the run is burnt and the retirement row "`stop` … replaced by the gate keel runs" is false for that working copy. RESOLVES IF: I-2 clause 1 is reworded to the verdict-read set; §3.1.2 strips or replaces the harness hook config in the materialised copy; §5 concedes test-authoring-as-own-evidence as a vulnerability or splits the step.

**3 ("computed by construction" outside the tree) — RESOLVED.** C4 drops the phrase, cites four real receipt-ledger issues (399 "a failed instrument leaves its previous artefact in place, reading as the current answer"; 468; 565; 569 "A second touched run is not refused: two ladders raced on one receipt"), and §3.0/§3.2.3 specify the lease as ephemeral server state with fencing as the control and the kill as "a spend courtesy". The `Claim` quote is verbatim (`work.sysml:55-57`); `cliClaim` synopsis reads "take or inspect a work claim; liveness is computed".

**4 (browser-only, three outages) — RESOLVED in mechanism, then UNDONE by I-7.** §3.2.7 has the computed indicator with a fault and the three issues are correctly titled (096 "The console has no surface for decisions AWAITING human acceptance"; 150 "70 items were waiting on the human with the console down, and nothing noticed but the human"; 152 "click-to-act reported broken a THIRD time"); a DOM rung is bound before browser-only (D0160 deferred rungs 2/3). But the fallback that carried every one of those outages is the chat channel, and I-7 removes it — see N1.

##### Rebuttals of other panelists' resolutions

**R1 — PEDANT-2 finding 1 (BLOCKER: D0209). F0 is under-scoped and should not flip PEDANT-2.** D0209 clause 5, verbatim: "An opt-in keel run <process> orchestrator is DEFERRED to last, adopted only for transitions where a landed-then-detected bad state is expensive to unwind, only alongside library mode, and only with its own code inside the signed keystone." Three conditions. F0 supersedes "deferred to last / opt-in" and keeps one condition ("own code inside the signed keystone"). Slice 1 is `keel run <process>.<step>` for any runnable step — not "only for transitions where a landed-then-detected bad state is expensive to unwind". "Library mode" appears in the repository only in D0209, D0053 and the 08-24 dossier (`manager-inversion-panel-2026-08-24.md:35, :99` — "alongside library mode, never replacing it"); it was never defined or built. Either the `#SupersedeClause` names all three conditions, or slice 1 violates two of them on the day it is chartered. MAJOR.

**R2 — PESSIMIST-2 finding 6 (tenth layer, nothing retired). The retirement table's CONSTRAINED column is empty in slice 1.** `serve.rs:1527`: "CLI uses the user's own subscription/enterprise auth (we never set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)"; `:1212`: "server NEVER sets ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (that would force pricier API-rate billing, D0094)". The agent's credential is the human's login under HOME. §3.1.2 gives the run a "throwaway `HOME`", which leaves `claude -p` with no auth at all; the two ways to make it run in slice 1 are the real HOME (the agent reads the human's OAuth credential and global config — the very things the full-environment diff claims to protect) or a key in the environment (the agent reads it; D0094's billing objection). I-5 "CONSTRAINED reads none" therefore cannot hold before slice 3, and slice 3's trigger is "a CONSTRAINED step's provider credential must leave this host" — a step that cannot exist until the proxy does. Circular. Slice 1 has one class, UNCONSTRAINED, whose retired row is the D0425 ceremony alone; `pre-bash`, `pre-write`, `post-edit`, `stop` and all 98 CLAUDE.md §4 lines "stay" (V4 says so). PESSIMIST-2's finding is largely unresolved. MAJOR.

**R3 — REDUCER 1/2/4, PEDANT-1: verified, no rebuttal.** `launcher.rs:1-18` quotes are verbatim; `.keel/runs` and `.tracking/runs` do not exist; 13 `checkedBy` against 191 `: ProcessStep` in `.engine/processes/`; CLAUDE.md `## 4` at line 82 and `## 5` at 181 make §4 = 83–180 = 98 lines.

##### New findings (introduced by revision 2)

**N1. [MAJOR — a BLOCKER by PEDANT-2's own round-1 standard] I-7 reverses an accepted human choice without supersession, and C5 mis-cites it.** C5: "acceptance by the human's verbatim words through the console's own gesture receipt (D0192 option B / D0411)". D0192's acceptance record: "OPTION A chosen (amend the requirement). Their words, verbatim: 'option A is fine. this hasn't been a real issue to date'". Option B — "acceptances become human-gesture-only … the accept-by-chat workflow they use today stops working … their 'accepted all' would become fifteen deck taps" — is the option the human declined. I-7 ("human judgment enters only through the browser session's own identity") is option B renamed; CLAUDE.md §4 still runs on A ("Chat acceptance: `keel accept <d> --words`"). Combined with I-10, the chain is: browser outage (three so far, noticed by the human first) → no acceptance can land → unjudged proposals hit the bound → keel assigns nothing. Revision 2 turned my finding 4's outage from a visibility fault into a work stoppage. RESOLVES IF: I-7 admits the D0192-A chat channel with its existing WARN semantics (D0423) as the surface-down fallback, or F0-style names a `#SupersedeClause` on D0192 held for the human; C5 cites option A.

**N2. [MAJOR] Slice 1's CONSTRAINED class is vacuous and its trigger to slice 3 is circular** — R2 above, stated as a finding against §3.1/§3.2/§3.3. RESOLVES IF: §3.1 says slice 1 runs UNCONSTRAINED steps only and states which credential the agent holds; slice 3's trigger is restated so it can fire (e.g. "the first step the human wants CONSTRAINED"); the retirement table drops its CONSTRAINED column until then.

**N3. [MAJOR] I-2 clause 1 unsatisfiable; tracked harness hooks travel with the clone** — finding 2 above.

**N4. [MINOR] §R claims a resolution the body lacks.** The PESSIMIST-1 4 row says "slice 1 keeps the console fallback"; §3.1 names no human surface at all, and §1 says "The human's surface for the runner subsystem is the browser". The map must not carry facts the design does not.

**N5. [MINOR] Two quotes.** D0436 (above). F0's elided quote of clause 5 drops the two conditions R1 turns on; quote the clause whole.

- Clone-not-worktree is correctly grounded: issue249 verbatim — "A linked worktree SHARES the common .git/config, so `git -C <worktree> config core.hooksPath nul` … rewrites the parent repository's config and disarms the real commit gate."
- Harvest-by-declared-outputs still would have caught issue496's commit and issue578's edit — with I-2's second clause, which stands.

### PESSIMIST-2 (operations) — round 2

#### PESSIMIST-2 (operations) — round 2

Method: every claim in §3.1 (slice 1) checked against this Windows host at HEAD f365cb7a, because slice 1 is now where the direction says it pays first. Commands run are read-only; nothing written under the repository.

##### Findings

###### A. Verification of my flip set {1, 4, 5, 6}

1. **[Finding 1 — five daemons, no supervisor] RESOLVED for slice 2, one gap in slice 1 (MINOR).** §3.2.7 does what I asked: one process, a supervisor on this host, liveness as computed indicators with a fault, and slice 1 adds no daemon. Two residuals. (a) "Windows Service or Task Scheduler" is a fork, not a name; pick one in the CR. (b) Slice 1's `keel run` is itself a 20-plus-minute process and §3.1 never says who launches it. The three launchers this host has all have a recorded failure: the agent's Bash tool (issue453, `.tracking/issues-claudeFable5.sysml:1829`: "the agent harness's shell tool caps a foreground command at ten minutes and, on the cap, kills the process TREE ... Nothing recorded that a land had started and not finished"), a detached launch (the orphan class: right now this host holds 16 `claude` processes, nine of them started 2026-09-14 09:02, three `java` since 2026-09-11, and nothing reports them), or the human's terminal (which makes the human the supervisor, D0204). The Job Object answers what happens to the AGENT when `keel run` dies; nothing answers what happens to the STEP — see the REDUCER-2 rebuttal below (a 48-hour claim). — RESOLVES IF: §3.1 names the launcher of `keel run` on this host and the run-claim's expiry.

2. **[Finding 4 — bypass to UNCONSTRAINED] UNRESOLVED: revision 2 made the bypass the default rather than measuring it (MAJOR).** The resolution ("F1 restated; the share of UNCONSTRAINED steps is a computed indicator") presumes slice 1 has two classes. It has one. CONSTRAINED is defined (§1) as "scrubbed environment, no credential the agent can read, egress only to declared providers". In slice 1 there is no proxy (slice 3) and no container, so: egress is unenforced; the model credential MUST be in the agent's environment for `claude` to run at all (I-5 "CONSTRAINED reads none" is false); and the scrub is a POSIX assumption on this host. Everything the scrub targets lives under `%USERPROFILE%`: `.cargo`, `.rustup`, `miniforge3`, `.claude\.credentials.json`, `AppData\Roaming\GitHub CLI` (all `Test-Path` true), and `CARGO_HOME`/`RUSTUP_HOME`/`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`HOME` are all unset. Node resolves the home from `USERPROFILE`, and so does keel: `members/keel-process/src/library.rs:32` `std::env::var_os("USERPROFILE").or_else(|| std::env::var_os("HOME"))`. So a "throwaway `HOME`" scrubs git and nothing else; redirecting `USERPROFILE` instead removes cargo, rustup, conda and the agent's login in one move — my round-1 toolchain gap, now on the host instead of in a container. The class table's "C holds" for I-3 and I-5 is therefore false for every slice-1 step, and the promised indicator reads 100% UNCONSTRAINED by construction. — RESOLVES IF: the class table gains a slice column; slice 1 declares its one class honestly ("scrubbed-UNCONSTRAINED": clone, Job Object, harvest, gate; credential readable; egress open); §3.1.2 names the Windows knobs it sets (`USERPROFILE` or not, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `KEEL_BIN` injected) and lists what the agent can still read.

3. **[Finding 5 — wall-clock per step] PARTIAL: the floor is owed to the CR, but the choice that decides the floor is unstated (MAJOR).** V5 promises "the measured floor ... in the CR before slice 1 is approved" — accepted as the form I asked for. But §3.1.5 "keel runs the step's `checkedBy`" cannot be the whole gate for keel's own deliverable: `checkedBy` is "a GUARD_NAMES entry or declared rule" (`.engine/schema/core/process.sysml:30-34`), a five-second predicate (`verify-receipt.toml`: guard rung 5 s), while the work the retired D0425 ceremony did is the ladder: clippy 84 s, touched 261-866 s (`touched-receipt.toml`: `passed = 922`, `seconds = 866`). Where does the ladder run under `keel run`? In the clone: no `target/` exists there — the main tree's is 77.7 GB, 12.8 GB of release deps, 1,890 built test binaries — so every code step cold-builds the workspace (issue536: the lib test binary alone is 283 s to compile). In the main tree: the harvested outputs must be applied to the main working copy BEFORE the gate, so the tree is dirty with unjudged proposals, `suite --touched` refuses a second launch while a pid lives (D0493), and D0182's single-writer pin (`launcher concurrency pins to 1 with single-writer-per-tree during a run`) returns at the gate. "The single-writer pin in D0182 is lifted by the clone" (§3.1.6) is true of the run and false of the gate. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.5 states where the ladder runs, and either names a shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` as a slice-1 component (not "if the floor is over the cadence") or states gate parallelism = 1.

4. **[Finding 6 — tenth layer] PARTIAL: the table exists; three rows are wrong for this host (MAJOR).** (a) Coverage: the table names four hook kinds; `.claude/settings.json` wires eight (`config-change`, `post-edit`, `pre-bash`, `pre-write`, `stop`, `subagent-start`, `subagent-stop`, `user-prompt`). `subagent-stop` — 279 blocks in my round-1 ledger count and the hook whose block text drove issue578's fabricated edit — is not in the table. (b) The CONSTRAINED column applies to zero slice-1 steps (finding 2 above), so under slice 1 every hook row reads "stays". (c) The ceremony row says "retired" in both columns, but C2's own distinguishing fact is "the share of sprints run through `keel run` versus interactively": interactive sprints keep the D0425 ceremony, so two ceremony paths coexist for as long as hypothesis B is live — that IS the tenth-layer shape. (d) Hooks inside the clone, unstated and decisive: `.claude/settings.json` is tracked (`git ls-files`), so all eight hooks FIRE inside every clone. Their probe is `KEEL_BIN` → `.keel/bin/keel(.exe)` → pin cache under `.keel/bin/<pin>/` → PATH; `.keel/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:74`), and `keel` resolves on no PATH here (`command -v keel` = none; `Get-Command keel` = none). So without injection the `stop` hook returns `{"decision":"block","reason":"[keel] The turn gate could not run: the keel binary is NOT FOUND ..."}` on every turn and the agent can never finish; WITH injection (D0182's "absolute KEEL_BIN injection") the agent holds a path to the main tree's binary, and every keel verb takes a `ROOT`: `keel record issue C:\...\sysmlv2-ai-toolkit --description-from F` lands a fact in the REAL tree past the harvest. I-1's violation test ("a write with no live run id lands") is red on day one, because the write API has no run id and adding one is a write-path change absent from slice 1's seven contents. — RESOLVES IF: the table lists all eight hook kinds; says what `keel run` does to the tracked hooks inside the clone (strip `settings.json`, or inject a `KEEL_BIN` that refuses any `ROOT` outside the clone); scopes the ceremony row to `keel run` work; and puts the write-API run-id refusal in slice 1's contents.

###### B. Rebuttals of other panelists' resolutions

5. **REDUCER 2 / SE 5 — "`keel claim` is the slice-1 lease" adopts a 48-hour lease with no kill-on-loss (MAJOR).** `members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:15` `pub const CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS: i64 = 2;` — constant, not per step. A `keel run` killed by the harness cap or a standby (issue595, `:1949`: a 97-minute Modern Standby mid-run) leaves its step claimed for two days; I-6's test "kill the runner ... step not ready again" is met only by being that slow, and §3.1 has no "step ready again" path at all. — RESOLVES IF: the run claim's expiry is the step type's wall-clock deadline plus harvest grace, stated in §3.1.2.

6. **SE 1 / PESSIMIST-1 2 — I-2's "never intersect the read set of its gate" forbids every tracked output for this project (MAJOR, operations).** The gate's read set here is the whole tracked tree: `working-tree-eol` runs "ONE `git ls-files --eol -z` over the tracked tree per run" (`.engine/docs/guards.md:74`), and `gate validate` reads all of `.tracking`. Read literally, no RECORD-route step (a Decision, an Issue, a backlog item — the 19% class C1 wants to move) can declare any output. A rule that forbids everything is re-typed around on day one, which is finding 2's dynamic under another name. The disjointness that does the work is against THIS step's verdict surfaces and the process definitions, not against the gate's reads. — RESOLVES IF: I-2 is narrowed to that.

7. **PEDANT-3 7 / my round-1 7 — "throwaway HOME" is a no-op for the two programs that matter on Windows (MINOR, folded into finding 2).** Evidence above (`library.rs:32`; Node uses `USERPROFILE`). The full-environment diff must name `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`, `.gitconfig`, `AppData\Roaming\GitHub CLI`, or redirect `USERPROFILE` and accept finding 2's consequence.

8. **My round-1 2 (Windows kill) — ACCEPTED.** A Job Object with kill-on-close reaps the `cmd /C claude` → `node` chain that `serve.rs:1222-1245` documents as orphaning; breakaway requires the parent's consent. Standby-as-expired is a slice-2 runner rule and correctly placed there.

###### C. New in revision 2

Nothing beyond what A and B already carry; findings 2-4 ARE the revision-2 introductions (slice-1 scoping put CONSTRAINED, the clone and the hooks on this host without a mechanism for any of the three).

##### What survived

- **Slicing with triggers.** Deferring daemon, lease server and proxy behind named triggers is the right operational order for one laptop; my round-1 finding 1 is answered in substance for the slice that would have carried it.
- **Clone, not worktree; digests over blobs; `autocrlf=false`.** Verified against issue249 (`:403`, "A linked worktree SHARES the common .git/config") and `.gitattributes` (`*.sysml text eol=lf`). Findings 3 and 7 of round 1 are closed.
- **Job Object.** Correct primitive for this host; the tree-kill in `serve.rs` is the best-effort version of it.
- **Harvest fails on any undeclared change.** in-toto MATCH both ways is the one control in §3.1 that needs no toolchain, no credential and no daemon, and it is the one that would have caught issue496 and issue578.
- **Retirement table as a form.** It is the right artifact; its rows need this host's facts.

##### VERDICT

OBJECT (minimal flip set: 2, 3, 4 — state slice 1's one honest class and its Windows knobs; say where the ladder runs and what that costs; say what the tracked hooks do inside the clone and put the write-API run-id refusal in slice 1. Findings 5 and 6 are one line each and I would not hold the verdict on them alone.)

### OPTIMIST-1 (steelman) — round 2

#### OPTIMIST-1 (steelman / anti-dilution) — round 2

##### Walls, checked against revision 2

- **W1 (done = the gate keel runs; the agent's sentence records nothing) — STANDS, strengthened.** §3.1.5 names the executor (keel runs `checkedBy`, writes the one `TestResult`, `proposed` routes to `awaiting-human`); I-11 refuses an unbound runnable step at definition. One wording defect in I-2 would make it unimplementable (finding 1).
- **W2 (materialise declared inputs, harvest declared outputs, delete the sandbox) — DILUTED in slice 1, half of it silently.** Outputs: intact, and stronger (in-toto MATCH both directions, §3.1.4). Inputs: `git clone --shared` (§3.1.2) materialises the whole tree; I-3 says "C holds" and nothing in slice 1 holds it. Sandbox: a directory on the same account (finding 2).
- **W3 (lease kills on loss, credential renewed with the lease) — honestly deferred to slice 2.** In slice 1 the Job Object makes liveness equal to `keel run`'s own life, which is stronger than any heartbeat. But the *claim record* under it is a two-day stored flag (finding 3).
- **W4 (server never executes) — inverted in slice 1, and D0209 cl.5 licenses exactly that** ("An opt-in keel run <process> orchestrator ... only with its own code inside the signed keystone", `0209:19`). The TCB cost the 2026-08-24 panel named ("driver joins the trust root", `manager-inversion-panel-2026-08-24.md:87`) is real in slice 1: the agent is a child of keel, on keel's account. Accepted as the clause's own shape; the boundary is finding 2.
- **W5 (frozen meta-process, in-flight runs pinned) — STANDS, strengthened** (I-11, §1 "human-signed CR only, never by a step"). Slice 1 should say the gate keel runs is the `checkedBy` at the run's `judgedAgainst`, not at parent HEAD at harvest time (MINOR, finding 6).
- **W6 (human attention as a computed fact) — the fact survives as I-10; the cap does not survive D0204 cl.2** (finding 4). I concede the cap was mine and was wrong as an invariant.

##### My round-1 findings, verified

1 (C0): resolved; every D0209 quote is verbatim (`0209:17-19`). The sprint claim is off (finding 5). 2 (C1 numbers): resolved on a stronger base than I asked for (the value critic's 129-issue class); the hook-ledger tally is not adopted and need not be. 3 (`judgedAgainst`/suspect): resolved, C5, `verification.sysml:56` verbatim. 4 (V6 rescope): resolved as text; its "moot" column is unearned until finding 2 is fixed. 5 (V4 split): resolved; the second count is arithmetic. 6 (C5 uniques, `producedArtifact : String`): resolved, `process.sysml:28` verbatim. 7 (counts): 77 per `keel version`, st181 marked, the 67/45/9 tally no longer restated in the body.

##### Findings (all introduced by revision 2)

1. [MAJOR] **I-2 as worded forbids every real gate.** "a step's declared outputs never intersect the read set of its gate" — a code step's gate is `cargo test`/`keel verify`, whose read set IS the outputs; for this repository the ladder reads the whole tree, so no step could declare any output. — EVIDENCE: the phrase comes from PESSIMIST-1 finding 2, whose cases were the gate reading the *verdict surface* the agent wrote: issue578 "rewrote the retro gate line of .tracking/delivery/sprint730_probePairTravelsInAFile.sysml with a finding it invented"; issue488 "recorded D0447 ... authored to silence the agent's own SubagentStop gate". SE finding 1 stated the correct narrower form ("a harvest never writes a `TestResult`, gate result or acceptance"). — RESOLVES IF: I-2 reads "declared outputs never include a `TestResult`, gate result, acceptance, receipt, or any path the gate reads a *prior verdict* from; the gate's subject is the outputs by definition".

2. [MAJOR] **Slice 1's CONSTRAINED class is a label, not a boundary, and the class table and retirement table are written as if it were.** I-3/I-4/I-5 are marked "C holds"; §3.1.7 retires `pre-write` and `pre-bash` for CONSTRAINED steps as "moot — the agent's shell cannot reach a governance record". In slice 1 the agent is `claude -p` spawned on the human's account with `.current_dir(&root)` (serve.rs:1555) and no filesystem, user or network boundary: it can `cd` to the parent repository and edit `.tracking` directly (I-4's violation test *passes the write*), can `git push origin HEAD:refs/heads/x` into the parent's `.git` (the clone's `origin` is the parent path), and reads the whole tree (I-3). I-5 "CONSTRAINED reads none": the spawned CLI "uses the user's own subscription/enterprise auth (we never set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)" (serve.rs:1527), so a throwaway `HOME` either breaks authentication or carries the credential into the agent's reach — without slice 3 there is no third option. The human's own word for the class is a container: st183 "A constrained container option and and an unconstrained agent option"; D0540's context glosses it "constrained sandbox". Landscape mechanism 16 states the posture: "Refuse to run rather than run unconfined" (`2026-09-20.md:53`). This is the boundary C1 rests on ("a working copy the agent cannot leave") and it is the one thing slice 1 does not build. — RESOLVES IF: either (a) slice 1 CONSTRAINED runs in a container or under a separate low-privilege account with the parent path denied and the clone's remote removed, the image a declared input (mechanism 7), and a step whose sandbox cannot be established is *refused, not re-typed*; or (b) slice 1 is stated as UNCONSTRAINED-only — I-3/I-4/I-5 marked RELAXED for slice 1, the CONSTRAINED column of §3.1.7 deleted until the container CR, and the payback claim re-scoped to the UNCONSTRAINED column (D0425 ceremony retired, keel writes the receipt), which is where the 129 issues live anyway. Sparse-checkout of declared inputs (`git sparse-checkout set`) is near-free and should be in slice 1 under either option so I-3 has a mechanism.

3. [MAJOR] **"`keel claim` is the slice-1 lease" (§R, REDUCER 2) adopts the claim and drops the reducer's own condition.** REDUCER 2 asked for "a second, shorter expiry for a run claim". `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS: i64 = 2` (`members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:15`) is a constant; the holder is the earliest un-expired claim; and `Claim` has "NO `released` FLAG, and that absence is the design" (`work.sysml:55`). So a `keel run` that dies (power loss, `taskkill` of keel itself — the Job Object kills the agent, not the claim) leaves the step held for two days with no release path, and under §3.1.6's per-run actor ids no other run can take it. That is theme 1 — a stored flag standing in for a live fact — re-created inside the slice that cites C4. — RESOLVES IF: a run claim carries its own expiry computed from the step type's wall-clock deadline plus harvest grace (a `Claim` subtype or a `runDeadline` attribute, one schema line in the same CR), and I-6's test is run against a killed `keel run`, not a killed agent.

4. [MAJOR] **Rebuttal — PEDANT-2 finding 2's resolution (I-10) changed the metric, not the throttle.** D0204 cl.2, verbatim: "Nothing gates or throttles the AI's work on the human's attention cadence". I-10's violation test is "bound reached, ready step still leasable" — i.e. assignment halts when unjudged proposals exceed N. Unjudged proposals grow at exactly (AI production rate − human judging rate); a bound on them is a throttle on the human's cadence with the human's name removed. The metric is now honest (a fact about the work); the gate still contradicts an accepted clause with no supersession. — RESOLVES IF: I-10 becomes a computed indicator with a fault (D0088: "no defensible boundary → stays indicator"; the reducer's finding 9 said the same), or the bound is on *concurrent runs* (a WIP fact independent of the human), or a `#SupersedeClause` on D0204 cl.2 is authored and HELD beside F0. I would accept any of the three; the panel should not accept the invariant as written.

5. [MINOR] **C0's sprint history is under-counted.** "cited in sprints 662–663 and then in none of 664–759 except 748": `git grep -l` over `.tracking/delivery` for `keel advance|process cursor|cliAdvance` returns sprints 441, 444, 655, 656, 657, 661, 662, 663, 687, 748, 756. Sprint 687 is substantive ("gate:<phase> refused by keel advance --to while red (D0435/D0436)"); 748/756 are module moves. The substance holds — D0436's title is "a process with no bound step is reported unenforceable-by-step, never refused", and no sprint record shows a transition the cursor refused — but C0 paraphrases it as "reported unenforced". — RESOLVES IF: the list is corrected and the D0436 phrase quoted.

6. [MINOR] **Slice 1 pins the run to HEAD but not the gate.** §3.1.4 records `judgedAgainst`; §3.1.5 runs "the step's `checkedBy`" without saying which version. If the process file in the parent changed during the run, keel gates against a definition the step was not assigned under (W5's in-flight pin, slice 2 K12, has no slice-1 form). — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.5 reads "the `checkedBy` bound at the run's `judgedAgainst`".

As worded, slice 1 has no runnable step, and the steps that would pay first (ceremony writes; code steps whose outputs `suite --touched` must read) are the first refused. 4. [MAJOR — new in revision 2] I-5 "C holds" is false in slice 1. There is no proxy until slice 3, so a CONSTRAINED step's agent must hold a readable provider credential to call the model at all; §1 defines CONSTRAINED by "no credential the agent can read, egress only to declared providers" and slice 1 controls neither (no egress control exists before §3.3). Either slice 1 has zero CONSTRAINED steps — and the retirement table's left column is slice-3 value, not slice-1 — or the slice-1 class is defined by the harvest alone. ROI consequence: the rows that pay (pre-bash/pre-write moot, stop/post-edit replaced by the gate, D0425 ceremony retired) hold on the harvest and full-environment diff regardless of the credential; the class table's honesty does not. — EVIDENCE: direction_r2.md §1, I-5, §3.1.2 (throwaway `HOME`, no `gh` session — nothing about the provider key), §3.3 trigger. — RESOLVES IF: I-5 and the egress half of I-4 gain a slice column (slice 1: relaxed for both classes — credential in a run-scoped throwaway `HOME`, spend uncontrolled, V1 applies to C as well as U; holds from slice 3), and slice 1's CONSTRAINED is stated as "harvest-bounded, full-environment-diffed", not "no readable credential".

A `keel run` killed with its host (retro 738's 97-minute standby; issue453/469's ten-minute harness cap) leaves the step held for two days, and a parallel `keel run` (§3.1.6) cannot take it. Two-day dead-run friction is precisely the hypothesis-B bypass path (C2, D0054). — RESOLVES IF: the slice-1 schema CR gives a run claim its own expiry (the step's declared wall-clock deadline + harvest grace) so a dead run's step is ready again within the hour.

### PEDANT-2 (consistency) — round 2

#### PEDANT-2 (consistency) — round 2

Verified against HEAD f365cb7a. Every quote below is verbatim from the file named.

##### Findings

###### Flip set from round 1 — verification

1. [MAJOR] **R1-1 (D0209): resolved in instrument, wrong in scope.** The instrument is right: `#SupersedeClause`, not `#Supersede` (D0398: "reverses one clause of Y named in X's text and leaves Y in force"); never both; HELD under D0337 (a `#ProspectiveChange` target is the enforcement surface) and D0439 would hold it on text alone. D0209's own rationale frames clause 5 as the inversion's carve-out ("the inversion remains available as clause 5"), so exercising it does not collide with the preamble "do NOT invert". But F0 names the wrong text. Clause 5 reads, whole: "An opt-in keel run <process> orchestrator is DEFERRED to last, adopted only for transitions where a landed-then-detected bad state is expensive to unwind, only alongside library mode, and only with its own code inside the signed keystone" (`.engine/decisions/0209-adhere-not-invert.sysml:19`). F0 reverses "deferred to last / opt-in" and says "Nothing else in D0209 is touched" (C0). Yet §3.1 runs every sprint step, not "only ... expensive-to-unwind" transitions, and "library mode" exists nowhere in the tree outside D0209's own sentence (`git grep -i 'library[- ]mode' -- .engine CLAUDE.md`: only 0209 and an unrelated D0053 word). So the direction silently reverses three of clause 5's four conditions while claiming to reverse one. Two further consistency constraints on F0's shape: D0306 makes "a Decision whose text enumerates several clauses ... a violation" from 2026-09-05, so F0 is either ONE clause ("clause 5's conditions of deferral are lifted; its keystone condition is restated as this Decision's own clause") or three Decisions with `#DependsOn` (D0303 C, CLAUDE.md §4). And §3.1.6 "The single-writer pin in D0182 is lifted by the clone" reverses an accepted clause — D0182: "launcher concurrency pins to 1 with single-writer-per-tree during a run" (`0182-*.sysml:17`) — with no second `#SupersedeClause` listed anywhere. — EVIDENCE: as cited. — RESOLVES IF: F0 quotes clause 5 whole, states which conditions it reverses (all but the keystone) and restates the keystone condition as its own clause; §4 gains F0b: `#SupersedeClause` on D0182's single-writer clause, both held.

2. [MAJOR] **R1-2 (D0204 cl.2): renamed, not resolved.** I-10's bound is on "unjudged proposals". Under D0312 B a `proposed` result "count[s] as done for nothing until a human judges" (`0312-*.sysml:18`); the set shrinks by exactly one act, the human's. I-10's violation test — "bound reached, ready step still leasable" — therefore makes leasing STOP until the human judges. D0204 cl.2: "Nothing gates or throttles the AI's work on the human's attention cadence - the D0200 clause 5 process-change throttle is removed" (`0204-pull-oversight.sysml:19`). D0200 cl.5, the removed clause: "past the threshold, new CHANGE-route work queues until the debt shrinks" (`0200-*.sysml:18`). I-10 is that clause with "CHANGE-route work" replaced by "leasable steps" and "debt" by "unjudged proposals". Calling it "no metric of the human" is true and beside the point: cl.2 forbids the gate, not the metric. V2 concedes the queue is untouched, which is exactly why the bound is a throttle on the human's cadence. **Rebuts SE round 1** ("I-10 ... is consistent with D0204 (it binds keel's assignment, not the human)"): binding keel's assignment to a quantity only the human reduces IS the cl.2 shape. — RESOLVES IF: I-10 bounds a fact of the work alone — concurrent live leases (a WIP limit on runs in flight) — and the `proposed` depth is an indicator with no threshold (D0088, as REDUCER 9 said); OR a `#SupersedeClause` on D0204 cl.2 is authored and held. Not both halves of I-10 as written.

3. [MAJOR] **R1-3 (gate executor): resolved; I-2's new wording is untestable and unsatisfiable.** §3.1.5 "keel runs the step's `checkedBy` ... and writes the one `TestResult`" answers who runs the gate; `proposed` routes to `awaiting-human`; good. But revision 2 adopted PESSIMIST-1 2's phrase literally: "a step's declared outputs never intersect the read set of its gate". Every `checkedBy` is a guard, a rule, or `gate:<phase>` — tree-wide predicates (D0436: "the process form evaluates tree-wide checks"); `suite --touched` READS the outputs in order to judge them; and no guard declares a read set anywhere (`git grep -i 'read set|readsPaths' -- .engine/docs/guards.md .engine/contracts .engine/schema/core`: none). Read literally, every code step is refused at definition; read loosely, the test cannot be run. Second defect: the exclusion is stated by PATH ("any path under `.tracking/`/`.engine/` that a verdict is read from") while the verdict shares the ITEM's file — `0209-adhere-not-invert.sysml` holds the Decision and `part d0209AcceptR1 : TestResult` in one file; every Decision, Issue, Need a RECORD-route step legitimately produces lives under those paths. Harvest is `git diff --name-only` (§3.1.4) — path granularity — so the invariant and the mechanism are at different grains. Third: "a harvested pass file" names nothing the tree has; a pass is a `TestResult` part inside an item file. — RESOLVES IF: I-2 splits into I-2a (the gate is executed by keel; test: a `TestResult` whose writer is a run, not keel, lands) and I-2b (harvested blobs are PARSED; a blob adding a `: TestResult`, an `outcome =`, an acceptance `Test`, or a `DecisionStatus` change is quarantined and fails the step; test: such a blob lands), and the read-set clause is dropped.

4. [MAJOR] **R1-4 (two claim clocks): the partition is right; the coupling it needs is absent, and slice 1 fails I-6 by construction.** §3.0 commits "the claim" as text and holds "the lease" server-only, then says "A server crash loses leases; the steps become ready again." On every clone that is false: `Claim` has "NO `status`, NO `expiresAt`, NO `released` FLAG, and that absence is the design" (`work.sysml:55`), `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS: i64 = 2` (`claims.rs:15`), the holder is "the earliest claim among those still inside the window" (`claims.rs:66`), and `keel claim` refuses "'{item}' is held LIVE by {holder}" (`claim.rs:80`). A lost lease leaves the step held for up to two days. Slice 1 is worse: §3.1.2 makes `keel claim` the lease itself, so I-6's own test "kill the runner, pass the lease: step not ready again" is failed by a killed `keel run` for two days, with nothing to write a release. §R says "slice 2's lease is stated as its server-side projection" — that sentence exists only in §R line 125; §3.2 never mentions `keel claim` (grep). And it is not a projection: a projection is derivable from its source, and the lease carries heartbeat facts the claim does not. Two clocks, two answers — the D0105 class from round 1, restated. **Rebuts REDUCER 2 as adopted**: "keel claim already has computed liveness" laundered a 2-day clock into a seconds-scale role. — RESOLVES IF: the coupling rule is stated in §3.1/§3.2 (a lease exists only under a live committed claim by the same worker; the run claim's expiry is the step type's maximum lease, a SECOND window on `Claim` — which is a CR on frozen `work.sysml`, listed with the others), and I-6's first test is re-run against slice 1.

5. **R1-5 (F3 pre-resolved): RESOLVED.** §1 "surface for the runner subsystem"; F3 restated with a cost. One residue as finding 10.

###### New in revision 2

6. [MAJOR] **I-5 "C holds" contradicts slice 1 having no proxy.** CONSTRAINED is defined as "no credential the agent can read" (§1); I-5's table says "C holds". Slice 1 spawns the agent in a clone with throwaway `HOME` and no proxy — so for the agent to reach a provider at all, the credential is in its environment, readable. Slice 1 therefore has no CONSTRAINED step in I-5's sense; it has clone-bounded steps (I-3/I-4 hold, I-5 does not). The retirement table's CONSTRAINED column describes a class slice 1 cannot run, and F1's second branch ("restrict UNCONSTRAINED to slice 3's proxy") empties slice 1 entirely — so F1 is open only on the branch the author recommends. — RESOLVES IF: the class splits — BOUNDED (slice 1: clone + harvest, credential readable) and CONSTRAINED (slice 3: + proxy) — the invariant table is redone per class, and F1 is restated against BOUNDED.

7. [MAJOR] **I-11 / §3.1.1 contradict D0436 and §1.1.** "an unbound runnable step is refused at definition, not reported" versus D0436 (accepted): "a process with no bound step is reported unenforceable-by-step, never refused ... refusing on nothing would be the honest-state inversion D0098 forbids" (`0436-*.sysml:19`); CLAUDE.md §5 "Honest-state gates, not completeness gates". A `runnable` MARKER that makes `checkedBy` mandatory is a stored fact derivable from inputs+outputs+checkedBy — §1.1 says derivable = view. **Rebuts REDUCER 4's minimal plan (a)** as adopted. — RESOLVES IF: runnable is COMPUTED; `keel run` refuses to RUN an unbound step (refusing an act, D0436's own shape); `validate` reports.

8. 9. [MINOR] C0's cursor count: `git grep -l -i -E 'keel advance|process cursor' -- .tracking/delivery/` in 664–759 hits 687, 748, 756 — three, all descriptive (a lens description, a fixture), none a use. "none ... except 748" → "three of 96, none a use". Substance holds.

10. [MINOR] F3's cost is one-sided: the subsystem branch costs a schema CR on frozen core plus a `CliCommand` fact for a project the human said "might [be] a totally separate project" (st185), and later separation is a `project-migration` (D0275). — RESOLVES IF: both branches carry a cost line.
