### PEDANT-3 (mechanisms) — round 2

#### PEDANT-3 (mechanisms) — round 2

Method: each revised mechanism walked as inputs/state → wrong outcome, verified against the repository at HEAD f365cb7a and against this host (the machine slice 1 runs on). Nothing written under the repository.

##### Flip-set verification (round-1 findings 1, 2, 3, 6, 7)

- **1 (rotation races harvest) → §3.2.1 fixed identifier, validity computed from the lease record: RESOLVED.** No supersession event exists during a live run, so the lost-reply race is gone; E9 (final report outliving its credential) is closed as long as the runner heartbeats through the upload, and §3.2.5's grace covers the at-deadline exit. One binding gap (finding 6).
- **2 (server pull) → §3.2.5 upload by digest + one `complete`, reap on the later of ack / expiry+grace: RESOLVED.** Two omissions (finding 7).
- **3 (unanswered ≠ rejected) → §3.2.3 local deadline + §3.3 per-call validity check: RESOLVED for slice 3.** The second enforcement point does not exist before slice 3 (finding 3); the deadline's owner is unstated (finding 8).
- **6 (body is egress) → §3.3 body policy, trust boundary stated: RESOLVED.**
- **7 (worktree shares `.git`; harvest filters what is seen) → §3.1.2 clone + throwaway `HOME` + full-environment diff: RENAMED, not resolved.** The git-level sharing (issue249) is closed by the clone. The environment isolation the text claims is not delivered on this host — finding 1 — and the diff's baseline misses exactly the writes the round-1 scenario named.

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] **§3.1.2's scrub is environment-variable-only on the same OS user, and the two tools it names do not read `HOME` on Windows.** — EVIDENCE: on this host, `node -e "console.log(require('os').homedir())"` with `HOME=/c/tmp` prints `C:\Users\WilliamWeatherholtz` (Node resolves `USERPROFILE`; Claude Code is a Node binary). `C:\Users\WilliamWeatherholtz\.claude\` holds `.credentials.json`, `settings.json`, `settings.local.json`; `.claude.json` sits beside it. `gh` state is `%APPDATA%\GitHub CLI\hosts.yml` (present); `GH_CONFIG_DIR` and `CARGO_HOME` are unset, so `~/.cargo` is the real profile. Scenario: an UNCONSTRAINED step under a "throwaway `HOME`" launches `claude.exe`, which reads and writes the real `~/.claude/settings.json`; the full-environment diff over the throwaway `HOME` sees nothing; the agent runs `gh` with the developer's live session; a write to `~/.cargo/config.toml` (absent today, so any content is a poison) runs at the developer's next `cargo build`. Root: same OS user, same filesystem — `C:\Users\WilliamWeatherholtz\claude_code\sysmlv2-ai-toolkit\.git\config` is writable by path whatever the env says, so issue249's outcome needs no worktree. V1's "protects the tree and the developer's global config" is false as stated. Also: `core.autocrlf=true` lives at system scope (`C:/Program Files/Git/etc/gitconfig`), which `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` does not touch; the clone's local config must set `false` (local wins) — say so. — RESOLVES IF: the run executes as a separate low-privilege OS user (the diff baseline is that user's profile; the parent repository is read-only to it by ACL), **or** the scrub list names `USERPROFILE`/`HOMEDRIVE`/`HOMEPATH`/`APPDATA`/`LOCALAPPDATA`/`CARGO_HOME`/`RUSTUP_HOME`/`GH_CONFIG_DIR`/`GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` and F1/V1 state the residual class as "the whole user profile and every repository on disk", not "the network; the provider's own tools".

2. [MAJOR] **A Windows Job Object does not reach the container option the human asked for (st183).** — EVIDENCE: `docker version` on this host: `Server.Os = linux`, kernel `6.6.87.2-microsoft-standard-WSL2`; `wsl -l -v`: `docker-desktop Running`. `serve.rs:1222-1228` (cited correctly by §3.1.3) documents only the `cmd → claude.exe` orphan. Scenario: the step's agent runs `docker run …` or `wsl.exe -e bash …`; at the wall-clock deadline `TerminateJobObject` kills `docker.exe`/`wsl.exe` — Windows clients — while the container runs under `dockerd` and the Linux processes under the distro's init inside the VM. `--rm` cleans up only on container exit; a killed attached client sends no signal. The "kill" is a courtesy that misses the process doing the spending. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.3 states the Job Object bounds Windows-native processes only; the runner labels every container `keel.run=<id>` and the kill path ends with `docker kill` by that label; Linux toolchains run inside a labelled container, never bare `wsl.exe`; leftover containers/volumes by label are part of the environment diff.

3. [MAJOR] **Before slice 3 there is no proxy, so two `[server]` claims have no enforcer.** (a) I-5 "CONSTRAINED reads none" — with no credential-holding forwarder the agent authenticates itself, from `~/.claude/.credentials.json` or an env key it can `echo`; slice 3's own trigger ("a CONSTRAINED step's provider credential must leave this host") admits the credential is on the host in slices 1–2. (b) §3.2.4 "The token/cost ceiling is mandatory per step type and is the control for the looping-under-a-live-heartbeat case (D19)" — with no metering point the runner cannot count tokens; `usage` is visible only to the agent process (the monitored party) or to a proxy. Observable slice-2 counters are bytes under declared output paths and child exits; "accepted provider calls" is slice 3 vocabulary. — RESOLVES IF: the class table marks I-5 C RELAXED until slice 3, or slice 1 includes a one-process loopback credential holder (which *is* slice 3's core — say which); §3.2.4 names wall-clock plus the provider's org-level spend limit as the interim control and lists D19 as OPEN until slice 3.

4. [MAJOR] **A clone violates I-3 for CONSTRAINED steps, and `--shared` adds three hazards of its own.** — EVIDENCE: any clone carries the entire history, so the declared input set is "everything ever committed" (a `.env` once committed included). `--shared` further: (i) exposes every object in the parent's store including unreferenced ones (reflog-only, dropped commits) via `objects/info/alternates`; (ii) sets `remote.origin.url` to the parent path, so `git push origin HEAD:refs/heads/x` (or a tag, or `refs/notes`) writes into the developer's `.git` — `receive.denyCurrentBranch` guards only the checked-out branch — a harvest bypass; (iii) git-clone(1), verbatim: "NOTE: this is a possibly dangerous operation; do not use it unless you understand what it does. … These objects may be removed by normal Git operations (such as git commit) which automatically call git maintenance run --auto. … If these objects are removed and were referenced by the cloned repository, then the cloned repository will become corrupt." — the developer's next commit in the parent can corrupt a run mid-flight. `--separate-git-dir` is just a full clone with its gitdir elsewhere; its `.git` pointer file is rewritable to the parent's gitdir, which is finding 1's same-user problem again. Cost of a full copy: `git count-objects -vH` = 18.31 MiB packed + 59.85 MiB loose — trivial; no reason for `--shared`. — RESOLVES IF: CONSTRAINED materialisation is `git archive` / `git read-tree` of the declared paths at the base tree into a fresh `git init` with no remote (base tree id in the receipt; the diff is against that recorded tree); UNCONSTRAINED is a full clone with `origin` removed; `--shared` is dropped from the text.

5. [MAJOR] **I-2's first clause forbids every gate, and the harvested outputs have no landing rule.** — EVIDENCE: I-2: "a step's declared outputs never intersect the read set of its gate". `checkedBy` names "The guard (a GUARD_NAMES entry) or declared rule" (`process.sysml:29-30`); guards read the tree, and a gate that judges outputs must read them. An intake step's outputs are `.tracking/intake/*.sysml`, which `gate validate .` reads — refused at definition under I-2 as written. The second clause (no `TestResult`, gate result, acceptance, or verdict-read path) is the real rule. Separately, §3.1.4–5 say outputs are "read as blobs" and gated, but never where they land (a ref? a branch? the main working tree?), so I-1's "every write to the tree names the run" has no write to bind to. — RESOLVES IF: I-2 keeps only the second clause plus "the gate's subject is the base tree with the outputs applied in a keel-owned scratch checkout (the D0182 shape); the verdict is written by keel outside that checkout"; §3.1 states the landing (my suggestion: `refs/keel/runs/<id>` committed by keel, merged to trunk only by a gate-green step or the human).

6. [MINOR] **The run credential must bind to its own run's lease record.** §3.2.1 says validity is "computed from the lease record"; if that record is per-step, runner B's acquire after A's expiry makes A's credential compute valid again from B's heartbeats. §3.2.2's run id is the right key — say the credential is a run id and validity reads that run's lease.

7. [MINOR] **§3.2.5 dropped "idempotent" and says nothing about a partial upload.** Round-1 review row 9 had "idempotent per run token"; a `complete` whose 202 is lost will be retried under a still-valid credential and must return the same result, not a fence refusal. A lease expiring after N blobs and no `complete` should land as `expired` with the received digests (the truth partition promises every refusal as text); orphan blobs are GC'd by run id; a restarted runner never resumes an on-disk run — the server's expiry is the truth.

8. [MINOR] **§3.2.3's deadline: formula, owner, and one contradiction.** `received_at + lease_seconds − skew` with `skew = received_at − sent_at` reduces to `sent_at + lease_seconds` on the runner's own monotonic clock; state it that way (skew is diagnostic; the subtraction has a sign hazard). The deadline must be held by the process that owns the job handle, treating heartbeat-child death as `expired`, or round-1 finding 3's variant reopens. And "a rejected heartbeat … harvest nothing" collides with §3.1.3/§3.2.3's kill-then-harvest-as-failed at the step type's maximum lease: distinguish rejected-because-exhausted (harvest as `failed` within grace) from rejected-because-superseded (harvest nothing).

9. [MINOR] **`awaiting-human` has two entries and two exits; `stalled` has no consequence.** Entry via the runner's own `complete` (mid-step question) or via the post-gate `proposed` route (§3.1.5); exit via the answer as an input to a re-run, or via `keel judge-set` to done/failed with no re-run. Stating that the transition is entered only from `complete` or the post-complete gate is what makes "a state transition is a fencing event" race-free with an in-flight upload. `stalled` is "distinct from expired" and then nothing happens on it — name `terminate`.

##### Rebuttals

- **SE 1's resolution (I-2) is wrong as worded** — finding 5. The author resolved a BLOCKER by adding a clause that, read literally, makes every `checkedBy` gate a definition-time refusal.
- **REDUCER 2 / SE 5's resolution ("`keel claim` is the slice-1 lease") does not give slice 1 liveness.** `members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:15`: `pub const CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS: i64 = 2;` and `:37` `let today = crate::queries::repo_today(root);` — day-granular, git-derived. A crashed `keel run` holds its step for two to three calendar days; there is no heartbeat in slice 1; I-6's own test ("kill the runner, pass the lease: step not ready again") is satisfied only after days. Slice 1 needs a run-lease with process liveness (pid + start time — the D0493 stub shape, which C4 itself lists among theme-1 failures, so it must carry a bound test) or a `keel run` expiry in hours. Otherwise write "slice 1 has no liveness; a dead run is freed by hand".
- **PESSIMIST-2 2's resolution ("§3.1.3 Job Object") is unresolved for the container case** — finding 2 supports PESSIMIST-2, not the author.
- The author's "Refuted, with evidence: none" is honest — no round-1 finding of mine was refuted, and none should have been.

##### What survived

- **Fixed credential with computed validity (§3.2.1)** — keel's own invariant applied to the credential; closes the rotation race and E9 in one move.
- **Upload + `complete` + server verify, reap on the later of ack / expiry+grace (§3.2.5)** — the Argo E22 zero-window regression is closed by contract.
- **Local deadline + per-call validity check at the proxy (§3.2.3/§3.3)** — two enforcement points, one server-side; correct once the proxy exists.
- **Body policy and the stated trust boundary (§3.3)**; **by-cause poison counter, cadence invariant with forward-only revision, kill-then-harvest-as-failed (§3.2.3/4)** — round-1 findings 9–11 resolved exactly as asked.
- **[server]/[runner] tagging with "the kill is a courtesy, the fence is the control"**, I-7, and the truth partition — the right shape for a protocol whose runners are outbound-only.

##### VERDICT

OBJECT (minimal flip set: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5)

### REDUCER — round 2

#### REDUCER — round 2

Test unchanged: does the human's goal (st181/st182: tool owns the task, deterministic control, narrow parallel agents with definite I/O, keel still detects discrepancies) fail without the element? Every claim below re-checked at HEAD f365cb7a.

##### Verification of my round-1 flip set {1, 2, 4, 5}

**1 (launcher built, unused) — RESOLVED in substance.** C3 now cites D0181/D0182 and `launcher.rs`; `.keel/runs` and `.tracking/runs` are absent (`ls` on both: "No such file or directory"); §3.1.2 says "This is the launcher's deferred P5.6 finished", and `launcher.rs:18` reads verbatim "Worktree-per-run stays deferred behind its named `DoR` (P5.6)." The delta is named (P5.6 + clone + harvest + typed I/O). One asked-for item is missing: *why* a gate-judged run existed for months and was never taken. C2's hypothesis B ("nobody wants the authoring friction") is the likely answer and slice 1 inherits it — fold it there (MINOR, below).

**2 (lease as a delta to `Claim`) — PARTIAL, renamed.** Slice 1 correctly uses "`keel claim` the step (existing, computed liveness)" and §3.0 partitions the claim (committed) from the lease (ephemeral). But the claim's window is `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS = 2` (`members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:15`) and §3.1 declares a wall-clock deadline in minutes. Nothing shortens the run claim, so a `keel run` killed by the harness's ten-minute cap (issue453/469) leaves its step claimed for two days — a stale stored fact standing in for a live one, in the slice whose C4 says keel refuses that class. §R's "slice 2's lease is stated as its server-side projection" is not in the text: §3.2 never names `Claim`. Slice 2 is deferred so that half is tolerable; the slice-1 half is not. See finding 2.

**4 (schema CR is the load-bearing change) — RESOLVED.** §3.1.1 is item one and says "the load-bearing change". Verified: `cat .engine/processes/*.sysml | grep -c ": ProcessStep"` = 191; `grep -c checkedBy` = 13; `process.sysml:34` `attribute checkedBy : String [0..1];`.

**5 (proxy unasked) — RESOLVED, and the slicing exposed a hole.** The proxy is slice 3 behind a trigger. But the invariant table still says I-5 "C holds … CONSTRAINED reads none" for a slice with no proxy. See finding 1.

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] **Slice 1 claims a CONSTRAINED class it cannot deliver; the residual credential class F1 states for UNCONSTRAINED is slice 1's whole class.** — EVIDENCE: §3.1.2 scrubs "throwaway `HOME`, … no `gh` session, no `.env`"; the launcher spawns `claude -p` (`launcher.rs:4`) and today relies on the human's login under `~/.claude` — `grep -n -i 'env\|HOME\|ANTHROPIC\|credential' launcher.rs` finds no credential handling at all. With a throwaway HOME the only way `claude -p` authenticates is an env var or config dir the agent process reads. No proxy exists (slice 3). So I-5's "C holds" has no slice-1 mechanism and the retirement table's "CONSTRAINED steps" column describes a class that does not exist until slice 3. — RESOLVES IF: slice 1 has ONE step class (scrubbed clone + declared-output harvest + keel-run gate; a run-scoped provider credential the agent can read); the C/U split and I-5's "reads none" arrive with slice 3; the retirement table's left column is retitled "steps run through `keel run`" (its rows hold by harvest regardless of credential). F1 then dissolves for slice 1, as my round 1 said.

2. [MAJOR] **The slice-1 lease has no expiry that matches the run.** — EVIDENCE: above; `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS = 2`; §3.1.3 "wall-clock deadline declared on the step type". I-6's violation test ("kill the runner, pass the lease: step not ready again") fails for two days in slice 1. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.2 states the run claim's expiry = the step's deadline + harvest grace (a second window on the same `Claim`, computed the same way), and a reaper that removes a clone whose `keel run` pid is dead (the D0493 stub pattern) — the slice-1 form of I-6.

3. [MAJOR] **Slice 2's trigger is not an observable as written.** — EVIDENCE: "a second machine or a second runner process is enrolled" — `enrol` is slice 2's own verb (§3.2.1), so the trigger presupposes the thing it gates; and §3.1.6 already runs "N concurrent `keel run` invocations", each a runner process, so read literally the trigger fires on day one. The second clause ("declared inputs cannot be materialised on this host") is fine: a recorded `keel run` refusal. — RESOLVES IF: trigger = a recorded Statement asking for a run on a host without the working tree, or for unattended launch (no human types `keel run`), or the materialise refusal. Slice 3's first clause ("credential must leave this host") is slice 2's trigger restated — drop it; the hostile-agent Statement is the real one.

4. [MINOR] **Four of eleven invariants have no slice-1 referent.** — EVIDENCE: I-7 (run/session token), I-9 (runner, proxy, heartbeat), I-10 (keel's own assignment — in slice 1 a human or cron types `keel run`; keel assigns nothing) and I-6 as written (lease). Binding slice 1's CR to all eleven is padding; binding it to none is the SE's blocker returning. — RESOLVES IF: the table gains a "bound in slice" column: slice 1 = I-1, I-2, I-3 (U-relaxed), I-4, I-6 (finding 2 form), I-8, I-11; the rest by their slice.

5. [MINOR] **The hazard analysis is justified but over-scoped, and §3 has two "firsts".** — EVIDENCE: `stpa-currency` is an existing WARNING-tier guard (`guards.md:113`: "every computed control action … has been walked by at least one recorded `stpa-self` run") — so an STPA run on new control actions is what the engine already asks, not padding. But §3.0 owes it over "controllers (server/scheduler, runner, proxy)" and actions (`acquire`, heartbeat, `retire`) that slice 1 does not build; slice 1 adds one controller (`keel run`, in-process) with materialise / spawn / terminate / harvest / gate. §3.0 calls the run "the first deliverable of slice 1's CR" while §3.1.1 calls the schema CR "the load-bearing change" first. — RESOLVES IF: one `stpa-self` run per slice over that slice's controllers; slice 1's covers `terminate` PROVIDED mid-write and `assign` with a non-closing input set (both slice-1 actions), and `harvest` from a superseded runner version waits for slice 2. Order: schema CR, then STPA over the structure it creates.

6. [MINOR] **"Throwaway HOME" is under-specified on this host, and the full-environment diff is an indicator, not a gate.** — EVIDENCE: Windows tools write under `%USERPROFILE%`, `%APPDATA%`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, `%TEMP%`, none of which `HOME` redirects; issue249 (verbatim: "A linked worktree SHARES the common .git/config, so `git -C <worktree> config core.hooksPath nul` … rewrites the parent repository's config and disarms the real commit gate") is the recorded defect the diff evidences, and a separate clone plus throwaway profile already makes that write unreachable. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.2 names the variable set; the out-of-tree diff is recorded as an indicator (D0088), the in-tree diff is the harvest and the gate.

7. [MINOR] **F0 supersedes more of D0209 than it says.** — EVIDENCE: D0209 clause 5 verbatim: "An opt-in keel run <process> orchestrator is DEFERRED to last, adopted only for transitions where a landed-then-detected bad state is expensive to unwind, only alongside library mode, and only with its own code inside the signed keystone." Slice 1 runs any runnable step, not only expensive-to-unwind transitions, and not "alongside library mode". A `#SupersedeClause` reverses one clause whole (D0398); F0 cannot keep "D0209's own conditions" from the clause it reverses. Cross-check on PEDANT-2 1's resolution. — RESOLVES IF: F0 names clause 5 whole, and says which of its four conditions the direction re-adopts by its own choice (keystone co-commit: yes, the D0070 hard lock already exists — "signed keystone" in this repo means a human-signed `#ProspectiveChange` Decision, not a signature).

8. [MINOR] **The retirement table is contingent and should say so.** — EVIDENCE: every "retired" row holds only for steps run through `keel run`; an interactive session keeps all of it. Hypothesis B (V7) is exactly the doubt that the share is small. — RESOLVES IF: the table's header says "for steps run through `keel run`; share is C2's measured fact".

##### Padding audit of slice 1 (the question put to me)

- Eleven invariants: design-level, fine; padding only if slice 1's CR binds all eleven (finding 4).
- Hazard analysis CR: justified by an existing guard (`stpa-currency`, D0313); over-scoped (finding 5).
- Retirement table: descriptive, no build scope; contingent (finding 8).
- Clone not worktree: justified — issue249 verbatim above; issue577 "Production worktree scratch paths are shared across concurrent keel runs"; issue491 a worktree hook "resolved the MAIN tree's binary". Working tree is 142 MB excluding `target`/`.git`, pack 18 MB — a `--shared` clone is seconds; the cost is the cold `target/`, which V5 already owes as a measured floor.
- Job Object: justified — `serve.rs:1223-1226` verbatim: "`kill_on_drop` reaps only the direct `cmd` child, orphaning the `claude.exe` grandchild; this guard `taskkill /T`-kills the whole tree" — best-effort and PID-reuse-guarded; an orphan that keeps writing into the clone after harvest breaks the harvest. Not padding.
- Full-environment diff: cheap, evidenced by issue249, but under-specified and an indicator (finding 6).

##### Rebuttals

- **OPTIMIST-1 W6** ("C4's whole argument … rests on [the human-attention cap] being built, not deferred"): in slice 1 nothing assigns, so there is nothing to cap; `authority-queue` and `indicators` exist today. No scenario was offered where slice 1 fails without I-10. Defer stands.
- **OPTIMIST-1** ("do not let a reducer make [kill on rejected heartbeat] advisory"): revision 2 made the kill "a spend courtesy and the fence … the control" (§3.2.3). That is correct — the server cannot enforce a kill; it can refuse a harvest — and SE 7 said the same. The wall is slice 2 in any case.
- **OPTIMIST-1 W5** (frozen keystone): stands, and costs nothing — the D0070 hard lock exists and the clone at HEAD pins the definition. Say so rather than implying a new signing mechanism.
- **OPTIMIST-2** ("Harvest-only-declared-outputs must stay in slice 1 against the reducer"): conceded, and never contested — my round-1 minimal plan (b) read "refuse the run if any path outside declared `outputs` changed". We agree.

##### What survived

- Slice 1 is, element for element, the minimal plan I proposed, plus three justified corrections (clone, Job Object, typed exclusion of verdict surfaces in I-2) and one owed analysis the engine's own guard asks for.
- Slices 2 and 3 are behind triggers, and slice 1 contains no daemon, lease server or proxy — the gold-plating objection is answered.
- C1's counted classes and the three "moved defects" of D0425 each map to a slice-1 control that is code, not prose.
- C6 states the untouched 40%; the human is not asked to buy the ledger.

##### VERDICT

### SE (kernel) — round 2

#### SE (systems engineer, kernel) — round 2

##### Flip-set verification (round-1 findings 1, 2, 3)

**SE 1 (I-2 had no mechanism) — resolved in substance, with two holes that re-open the class.** Revision 2 states the invariant, refuses it at definition (§3.1.1 checked at `validate`), fails a run whose diff leaves the declared set (§3.1.4), and has keel run the gate (§3.1.5). That is the shape I asked for. Verified: `checkedBy : String [0..1]` and `producedArtifact : String` at `.engine/schema/core/process.sysml:28-34`; 13 `checkedBy` over 191 steps; `WriteError::ReceiptOwed` raised at `write.rs:618`, message at `:88`. The holes are findings 1 and 2 below: the gate's read set is conflated (subject vs evidence) and the harvest, as a blob copy, bypasses every write-time refusal the direction says it generalises.

**SE 2 (no hazard analysis) — adequate for a direction document, on two conditions (finding 9).** A direction is not a Decision; D0209 cl.2's co-committed `#SafetyChange` binds the CR, and the doc now names EL5 and three UCA candidates. Hazards need not be enumerated here. But the owed run is mis-scoped (it names slice 2's controllers while slice 1 is what the CR charters) and its only automatic trigger is WARNING-tier.

**SE 3 (dual truth) — resolved as asked.** The partition (§3.0) places receipts, claims, refusals and gate results as text, and lease/credential validity/cadence/roster as ephemeral, with I-8's drop-the-store test. Placement gaps (quarantine evidence, runner identity, metered usage) are MINOR (finding 8). The claim↔lease relation, however, is not resolved by the partition (finding 4).

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] **I-2's "outputs never intersect the read set of its gate" is unsatisfiable as written, and the set it excludes is stated three incompatible ways.** A gate must read the harvested outputs — they are its subject. What must be disjoint is the gate's *evidence* read set. I-2 excludes "any path under `.tracking/`/`.engine/` that a verdict is read from"; the retirement table row 2 says `.tracking`/`.engine` "are not declared outputs" at all; the violation test is glob-level (`.tracking/**`). Meanwhile the doc never says WHERE the gate runs. If it runs in the agent's clone (the cheap option), the evidence surfaces are agent-writable: D0371 answers a green `keel guard` from `.keel/metrics/guard-receipt.toml` when the key is equal; D0369's commit-facts cache under `.keel/cache/` is read by guards (`members/keel-guards/src/receipt.rs:15`); D0474 skips a test binary "observed green at the current content". Today `.keel/metrics/` is protected only by the pre-write hook (`claude_surface.rs:80-88`: `(".keel/metrics/", "keel suite / keel land / keel gate - the runs write their own receipts")`) — the very hook the retirement table calls "moot" inside a CONSTRAINED clone. `.keel/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:74`) so a fresh clone has none, and an agent can create one. — EVIDENCE: as cited; PESSIMIST-1 2's class (verifier made the gate green by writing evidence, issue578/488). — RESOLVES IF: I-2 splits the gate's read set into SUBJECT (harvested outputs, applied to a keel-owned base) and EVIDENCE (`TestResult`s, acceptances, `.keel/**`, `target/**`, any receipt); outputs may be subject, never evidence; §3.1.5 states the gate runs in a keel-owned checkout, never the agent's clone, with `KEEL_NO_RECEIPT=1`; the exclusion is stated once and the retirement row and violation test quote it.

2. [MAJOR] **Harvest by blob copy bypasses the write path the direction claims to generalise.** §3.1.4: "declared outputs are read as blobs, never as host-written files"; V6: "the agent never invokes `keel record`". Every refusal C1 leans on is write-time, not validate-time: `ReceiptOwed` (write.rs:612-619, "Refused at the write (issue448): the turn-boundary guard used to be the only check, so the line landed"), `RetroScanMissing`, the AI-actor acceptance refusal, provenance-never-defaulted. `validate` is weaker — "A verifier subagent hand-wrote six TestResults with typed non-hex ids and validate accepted them" (`.tracking/issues-claudeOpus5.sysml:1837`). So either (a) `.tracking` is wholly excluded from outputs, in which case no governance step (author an Issue, a Decision, a Statement — the 68+27+8 issues of C1's class are governance writes) can run under `keel run`, and the class is *excluded*, not governed; or (b) `.tracking` outputs are admitted as files and every write-time refusal is bypassed. — RESOLVES IF: a step's `.tracking` output is a typed proposal (the write API's own input form) that keel replays through `keel record` at harvest, so every write-time refusal fires; raw `.tracking` blobs are never harvested. The launcher precedent (`launcher.rs:7-12`) wrote through write.rs, not by copy.

3. [MAJOR] **Slice 1 does not say who invokes `keel run`, and if it is the primary AI the dispatch defect of D0425 is unmoved.** The clone constrains the *child* agent; the invoker stays a full-tool actor in a real checkout who chooses whether to run `keel run` or `keel record result` directly — exactly "the primary dispatches it" from my round 1, which the author accepted (§R: PESSIMIST-1 1 "three controls named"). The three controls hold for the child; none touches the invoker. Consequences: I-1 "U holds" is false for the invoker (its writes name no run); I-2's violation test is unobservable after the fact, because a keel-written `TestResult` and an AI `record result` are indistinguishable in the tree (the launcher precedent even stamps `judgedBy` the run's actor, `launcher.rs:11`). — RESOLVES IF: §3.1 names the invoker (human via console/CLI, or the process cursor `keel advance`), and adds one write-path control: a `TestResult` verifying a `runnable` step's `checkedBy` is refused unless stamped by `keel run` with its run id — the only path to done for a runnable step is the gate. C1's falsifier then becomes computable: the count of runnable-step results not stamped by a run.

4. [MAJOR] **The slice-1 lease is `keel claim` unchanged, and it fails I-6's own test in slice 1; the two-clock problem stands in slice 2.** `CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS: i64 = 2` (`members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:15`), "Deliberately generous"; holdership = "earliest un-expired `claimedAt`" (`members/keel-guards/src/acceptance.rs:627`; the schema comment at `work.sysml:55-57` says "newest" and at `:63-64` says "earliest un-expired" — the code is earliest). A `keel run` killed mid-step holds the step for two days: "kill the runner, pass the lease: step not ready again" is the stated violation, and slice 1 exhibits it. In slice 2 a seconds-scale server lease that re-assigns cannot make the new runner the tree's holder while the dead run's claim is earlier and un-expired; the new run's writes carry a `superseded` claim. The partition does not touch this; §R's "slice 2's lease is stated as its server-side projection" is a rename of PEDANT-2 4 and REDUCER 2, not a resolution — REDUCER asked for exactly "a second, shorter expiry for a run claim". — RESOLVES IF: a run claim's window is derived from the step type's declared wall-clock deadline (`claimedItem` → step → `maxLease`; a computation change in `claims.rs`, no field on `Claim`, so schema/core stays frozen), and a claim past its window with no receipt is a fault (this is also I-9's missing slice-1 mechanism).

5. [MAJOR] **I-5 "C holds" is false in slices 1 and 2.** No proxy until slice 3; a throwaway `HOME` (§3.1.2) removes the `claude` OAuth session, so the CONSTRAINED agent must hold a provider key in its environment for the whole run — readable, and with no ceiling until §3.2.4 — while F1/V1 concede the bill only for UNCONSTRAINED. — RESOLVES IF: the class table gains a slice column, or I-5 reads "C: the credential is scoped to the run and revoked at reap (slices 1-2); unreadable (slice 3)", and V1 names CONSTRAINED spend in slices 1-2.

6. [MAJOR] **I-10 has no mechanism in any slice.** `I-10` occurs in §3.0 and V2 only; §3.1 and §3.2 never bound a claim on the count of `proposed` results. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.2 `keel claim` (and §3.2.2 `acquire`) refuses while unjudged `proposed` results ≥ the bound declared in `activation.toml`, with the refusal a committed record.

7. [MINOR] **I-3 "C holds" vs `git clone --shared`.** A clone checks out every path and, `--shared`, exposes every object in history to `git cat-file`; "materialises exactly its declared inputs" needs sparse-checkout or blob materialisation. `--shared` also dangles if the source repo gc's. — RESOLVES IF: I-3 for C is RELAXED in slice 1, or the mechanism is named.

8. [MINOR] **Partition and F0 precision.** Not placed: the quarantined violating harvest (should be a committed Issue with evidence, not a server artifact); runner identity (a committed `Actor` — only liveness is roster); metered `usage` (belongs in the receipt list). F0 names "the 'deferred to last / opt-in' clause", but D0209 clause (5) verbatim: "An opt-in keel run <process> orchestrator is DEFERRED to last, adopted only for transitions where a landed-then-detected bad state is expensive to unwind, only alongside library mode, and only with its own code inside the signed keystone." Slice 1 runs every runnable step, not "expensive-to-unwind transitions", and library mode is nowhere in the doc; the `#SupersedeClause` must name clause (5) whole, retaining only the keystone condition (which I-11 restates). Cross-check on PEDANT-2 1's resolution.

9. [MINOR] **Hazard-analysis deferral: adequate, mis-scoped, under-bound.** The owed `stpa-self` run names server/runner/proxy and slice-2 UCAs (`harvest` from a superseded runner version); slice 1's controllers are `keel run` in the invoker's process and the agent in the clone, with actions materialise / spawn / kill-on-deadline / harvest / gate / quarantine. A write-effect `CliCommand` surfaces as a computed action (`members/keel-view/src/view/control_structure.rs:1315`), so `stpa-currency` will name `cmdRun` — but the guard is WARNING-tier (`.engine/docs/guards.md:113`) and holds nothing. — RESOLVES IF: the owed run is scoped to slice 1's structure and bound as a `verify`-linked DoD Test of the CR's sprint.

**Invariant coverage check (asked by the dispatch).** Every §3.1 mechanism serves an invariant (1→I-2/I-11; 2→I-6, I-5, I-8 via digests, I-4 via issue249; 3→I-6/I-2; 4→I-2/I-4/I-8; 5→I-2; 6 is a need, not an invariant). Invariants without a mechanism: I-10 (none), I-9 (none in slice 1), I-1 (none for the invoker), I-5 (C false before slice 3). Overlap: I-4 is I-2 minus the verdict exclusion — merge or keep I-4 as I-2's test. All eleven violation tests are observable except I-2's after-the-fact form (finding 3) and I-4's ("succeeds" inside the clone is not the violation; "is harvested" is).

##### Rebuttals

- **PESSIMIST-1 1** ("defect moved, not vanished") is marked resolved by "three controls named". The controls cover the step author (keystone: a process-def change needs a human-held marked Decision, D0070/D0337) and the harvester (keel code); they do not cover the dispatcher. Unresolved until finding 3 lands.
- **PEDANT-2 3 / PESSIMIST-1 2** are resolved in intent; both used "read set of the gate" loosely and the author copied the looseness into I-2 (finding 1).
- **PEDANT-2 4 / REDUCER 2** are renamed, not resolved (finding 4).
- **REDUCER 1 and 4** are resolved: verified `launcher.rs:1-18` (P5.6 deferred), `.keel/runs` and `.tracking/runs` absent, and the schema CR is now §3.1.1's first item.

##### What survived

- **The verdict-forgery path is closed in the direction's logic**: definition-time refusal, whole-diff MATCH, keel-run gate, `proposed` never done. The residue is where the gate runs and what a harvest may carry — mechanism, not direction.
- **The truth partition is the right cut** and I-8's test is the strongest sentence in §3.0; in slice 1 it holds trivially, which is a point for slicing.
- **I-11 is grounded**: "signed keystone" is the D0070 hard lock generalised by D0209 cl.2 — a real guard, not a metaphor.
- **C0/F0 is honest**: it names the accepted Decision it contradicts and holds the supersession for the human rather than arguing around it.
- **Slicing answered the gold-plating objections without weakening the invariants** — the class table's HOLDS/RELAXED is the correct form; it just needs a slice axis.

#### PESSIMIST-1 (premise) — round 3

##### Flip-set verification (round-2 N1, N2, N3; rebuttal R1)

The third part — "states which credential the agent holds" — is answered with a noun, "a run-scoped provider credential it can read … revoked at reap", and no mechanism. That noun is finding 1 below.

The hook part names the wrong file — finding 2. The third clause of my RESOLVES IF (concede test-authoring-as-own-evidence in §5) is absent; MINOR, finding 4.

**R1 (F0 must name all three conditions) — RESOLVED.** C0 quotes clause 5 whole and verbatim (`0209-adhere-not-invert.sysml:19`), supersedes it whole, and re-adopts the keystone condition as I-11; `:19` is `decision`, `:20` is `rationale`, as cited. One retraction of my own: my round-2 claim that "library mode" appears in D0053 was a case-insensitive false positive on "library ModelingMetadata"; revision 3's "defined nowhere in the tree outside D0209" holds.

Other revision-3 citations re-checked and holding: `write.rs:88`/`:618`; `library.rs:32` (`USERPROFILE` then `HOME`); `serve.rs:1223-1246` (`cmd → claude.exe` orphan); system-scope `core.autocrlf=true`; `git count-objects`: 18.31 MiB packed, 59.85 MiB loose; `claims.rs:15`, `work.sysml:55-67`, `verification.sysml:56`, `process.sysml:28`, `history.rs:11-12`; `KEEL_NO_RECEIPT=1` exists (D0371); D0182 and D0204 cl.2 verbatim; `guards: 77 (68 hard-blocking, 9 warning-only)`; issue453's title is the ten-minute cap; `2026-09-20.md:124/:545/:576/:55` and `2026-09-21.md:78/:106` say what C2–C4 say they say.

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR — the irreducible residue] **Slice 1 runs on a credential that either does not exist on this host or reverses an accepted human directive, and C0's "nothing else moved" omits it.** §1: "the agent holds a run-scoped provider credential it can read"; §3.1.3: "placed in that profile, scoped to the run, revoked at reap"; I-5's slice-1 row: "run-scoped, readable, revoked at reap". — EVIDENCE: D0094 (`accepted`, human confirmation at `:34`), `decision` `:29`: "AUTH (human directive): the agent-bridge pipes to the LOCALLY-AUTHENTICATED `claude` CLI — the user's Claude subscription / ENTERPRISE session — and MUST NOT set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (setting it overrides the subscription and forces pricier per-token API-rate billing)"; enforced at `members/keel-serve/src/serve.rs:1212` and `:1527`. The author's own evidence base says the alternative is barred too: `runner-daemon-landscape-2026-09-21.md:662` row 14, "VULNERABLE - a runner holding a person's `setup-token` violates ToS and dies when they leave; keel must hold API keys or WIF, never subscription tokens"; `:633`, Anthropic's own runner gets "a session-scoped OAuth token for inference" minted by Anthropic's control plane, which keel cannot mint. So on this host the "run-scoped credential" is one of exactly two things: (a) the human's subscription login copied into the run profile — not run-scoped, not revocable (deleting a copy revokes nothing, and egress is open), and the ToS exposure row 14 names; or (b) an organisation API key — a `#SupersedeClause` on D0094's auth clause with a billing consequence the human chose against. Revision 3 knows this: §3.2.1 says "the credential the human holds today, and its eligibility, is stated in the CR before this clause is approved" — for slice 2. Slice 1 spawns the agent first. — RESOLVES IF: §4 gains **F-cred** with both options and their costs in the human's terms, I-5's slice-1 row reads "deleted at reap, not revoked" under (a) or "D0094 auth clause superseded" under (b), and C0's held set lists D0094 beside F0/F0b. This is the one item I would hand the human: *whose credential does the agent in the clone hold, and is the billing change acceptable?*

2. [MAJOR] **Removing `.claude/settings.json` from the clone does not remove the hooks; the launcher loads them from the plugin.** §3.1.3: "The tracked hook configuration (`.claude/settings.json` …) is removed from the clone"; retirement row 1: "not present in the clone". — EVIDENCE: D0296 `:19`: "keep the repository's settings.json as the DECLARATION only. (1) A keel PLUGIN (… hooks/hooks.json …) carries the enforcement copy … (2) The launcher - the console's claude -p spawn in launcher.rs, and a `keel claude` wrapper … passes --plugin-dir to the shipped plugin and --settings with 'disableAllHooks': false, so a key already on disk at launch is overridden above project scope". `members/keel-serve/src/launcher.rs:47-48`: "`--plugin-dir <root>/.engine/claude-plugin` loads the plugin rendering - the hook set a repo-scope settings file cannot remove, since hook lists merge across scopes"; `:63`, `:66` push the args. `.engine/claude-plugin/hooks/hooks.json` is tracked and clones with the tree. §3.1 names `keel serve` (D0181) as the invoker; if `keel run` spawns through the launcher's shape, all eight hooks fire inside the clone with settings.json gone, and the issue578 block-loop I described in round 2 is back. The correct mechanism is the spawn: no `--plugin-dir`, hooks off by `--settings` — which is a new spawn shape on the enforcement surface (D0209 cl.2, D0296 layer 2) and therefore a co-committed `#SafetyChange` Decision, not a file deletion. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.3 says the run's spawn passes neither the plugin nor the hook pin, names it as a `#SafetyChange` in the CR, and drops the settings.json sentence. Author-resolvable; not a human fork.

3. [MINOR] **C1's falsifier is uncomputed without a guard, and the §R2 row for PESS-2 4d over-claims.** "The count of runnable-step results not stamped by a run" has no home: the write refusal covers the API path only, CLAUDE.md §4 allows "direct edits for what it doesn't cover", and issue454 (`issues-claudeOpus5.sysml:1837`) shows `validate` accepting hand-written results. The §R2 row says "I-1's write-path refusal covers a `ROOT` pointed at the parent" — it covers a `TestResult` against a runnable step; a `record decision --from F <parent>` from inside the clone is a write past the harvest with no refusal, and the body already concedes it as F1(a)'s residual. — RESOLVES IF: C1 names the guard (results against a `checkedBy`-bound step carry a run stamp; forward-only, D0388 pair) and the §R2 row cites F1(b), not I-1.

4. [MINOR] Two counts and one concession. issue454 is six hand-written results of which four had non-hex ids ("six … with non-hex ids" over-counts). `.claude/settings.json` binds nine `hook` verbs (`kill-switch` under `ConfigChange` beside `config-change`) across seven Claude events; "eight hook kinds" is the retirement table's count, not the file's. §5 does not concede that when the gate is `suite --touched` over declared inputs, the agent writes the tests that judge it — V3 is adjacent but names bound predicates, not self-authored ones. — RESOLVES IF: the counts are fixed and V3 gains one clause.

##### Rebuttals

- **REDUCER 2 / SE 4 (run-claim window) — resolution holds.** A window computed in `claims.rs` from the step type's deadline needs no field on `Claim`; `work.sysml:55-59`'s "NO `expiresAt`" is preserved. No rebuttal.

Findings 3 and 4 do not hold the verdict.)

### PESSIMIST-2 (operations) — round 3

#### PESSIMIST-2 (operations) — round 3

Method: every slice-1 mechanism revision 3 added (§3.1.3 knob set, hook-file removal, `refs/keel/runs/<id>` + keel-owned checkout + shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, the replay) checked on this host at HEAD f365cb7a, including one scratch clone in the scratchpad. **Disclosure:** my first probe's `git clone` failed at checkout ("Filename too long", see N3) and, because the chain's `cd` never ran, its step (c) executed in the repository root: it set `skip-worktree` on `.claude/settings.json` and deleted the file. I reversed both at once (`update-index --no-skip-worktree`, `checkout --`); `git ls-files -v` shows `H`, `git diff --stat HEAD -- .claude/settings.json` is empty, `git status --porcelain` equals the session's opening snapshot. Nothing else under the repository was touched.

##### Findings

###### A. My round-2 flip set {2, 3, 4} against revision 3

1. **[Finding 2 — one honest class, Windows knobs] RESOLVED in substance; the knob set it introduced has a defect (see N1).** §1 declares one class, BOUNDED; I-3/I-5 carry "relaxed" in the slice-1 column; §3.1.3 names the variable set, states the same-OS-user residual, and F1(b) offers the ACL boundary. That is what I asked for.

2. **[Finding 3 — where the ladder runs] RESOLVED.** §3.1.5: gate in a keel-owned checkout of the run ref, `KEEL_NO_RECEIPT=1` (exists: `members/keel-fs/src/fsx.rs:78`), shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` as a named component, gate parallelism 1 with D0182's pin kept for the gate. Two MINOR residuals for the CR, not the verdict: (a) cargo's `-C metadata` hash includes a path package's absolute path, so a shared target dir at a *different* checkout path shares registry deps only and rebuilds every workspace crate — the keel-owned checkout must be one fixed, persistent path updated in place, not a fresh path per run; and cargo's build-dir lock serialises the gate behind any interactive `cargo` in the main tree (issue-1565's 2.3x tail under contention). (b) `keel run` must execute from a copy of the binary, never `target/release/keel.exe`, or the gate's own cargo cannot relink it (issue150, CLAUDE.md §6).

3. **[Finding 4 — hooks inside the clone; run-id refusal in slice 1] HALF.** The run-id refusal is in slice 1 (I-1 column "1"; C1's fourth control) — accepted. The hook half was resolved by a mechanism that fails on this host: see N2.

###### B. New in revision 3

**N1. [MAJOR] The §3.1.3 knob set, as written, launches an agent with no Rust toolchain, no login and no permission to use a tool.** — EVIDENCE: (i) `RUSTUP_HOME` pointed at an empty directory: `cargo --version` → "error: rustup could not choose a version of cargo to run, because one wasn't specified explicitly, and no default is configured" (run on this host; the proxies in `%USERPROFILE%\.cargo\bin` stay on PATH but resolve no toolchain). (ii) `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` pointed at a throwaway drops `~/.claude/settings.json`, which holds **50 `permissions.allow` rules** and 0 deny; the tracked project file holds only `permissions.deny` (ten skills) and no allow list; the launcher passes `-p ... --max-turns` (`members/keel-serve/src/serve.rs:1554`) and **no permission flag anywhere** (`git grep -E "skip-permissions|permission-mode|allowedTools"` over `members` and `keel-cli` is empty), although D0182 promised "explicit permission mode". Headless mode cannot answer an ask — keel's own `KEEL_RUN_ID` branch exists for exactly that (`members/keel-hooks/src/lib.rs:511-522`, "headless run … DENIED and queued"). So inside the run every Bash/Edit/Write is an unanswered ask. (iii) A throwaway config dir has no `.credentials.json`; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is unset on this host and the launcher's own error text says "do NOT set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY — that forces API-rate billing" (`serve.rs:1563`). — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.3 splits the set into *redirected* (`USERPROFILE`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`) and *seeded* (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` written by keel with the credential and a permission grant; `RUSTUP_HOME`/`CARGO_HOME` either left at the host's read-only toolchain or seeded), and `keel run` passes an explicit permission mode or allow-list to `claude -p`. Docker needs nothing: both `\\.\pipe\docker_engine` and `dockerDesktopLinuxEngine` exist, so the CLI works on the `default` context without `~/.docker`.

**N2. [MAJOR] "The tracked hook configuration is removed from the clone" fails the harvest, re-arms through the plugin, and reddens the clone's own gate; and the diff it relies on is the agent's lever.** — EVIDENCE (scratch clone, origin removed, `core.autocrlf=false`): (a) `rm .claude/settings.json; git diff --name-only HEAD` → `.claude/settings.json` — under §3.1.5 "any changed path outside the declared outputs fails the step", every run fails at harvest. (b) The launcher passes `--plugin-dir .engine/claude-plugin` whenever `hooks/hooks.json` exists (`members/keel-serve/src/launcher.rs:55-70`); that file is tracked and carries all seven events (`ConfigChange, PostToolUse, PreToolUse, Stop, SubagentStart, SubagentStop, UserPromptSubmit`) — with `keel serve` as the §3.1 invoker, removing `settings.json` removes nothing. (c) `keel sync-claude --check <clone>` on that clone: exit 1, "DRIFT: settings.json: keel-owned entries differ from this binary's generator" — the `claude-surface-drift` guard (`.engine/docs/guards.md:81`, violation tier) goes red, so a plugin-armed `stop` hook blocks every turn in the clone. (d) `git update-index --skip-worktree .claude/settings.json; rm …; git diff --name-only HEAD` → empty. The bit hides the removal — which is the fix, and also the attack: an agent with git in the clone hides any edit the same way, and `.git/info/exclude` (untracked) hides new files from `status`/`add -A`. An index-mediated diff cannot be the harvest. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.3 says the hooks are disarmed by *not passing `--plugin-dir`* and by either `--settings` with keel's own file or a documented removal mechanism; §3.1.5 says the harvest compares the working tree against the base tree through **keel's own fresh index** (`GIT_INDEX_FILE` temp + `add -A --force`, or a filesystem walk), never the clone's index or exclude file; and the gate's `sync-claude --check` runs in the keel-owned checkout of the ref, where `settings.json` is present.

**N3. [MAJOR] A full clone "into a path carrying the run id" fails checkout on this host unless long paths are enabled.** — EVIDENCE: `git clone` into the scratchpad (174-character prefix): 60 × "error: unable to create file … Filename too long", "fatal: unable to checkout working tree", exit 128; `git config --system --list` has no `core.longpaths`. Same clone with `git -c core.longpaths=true clone`: exit 0. keel already documents this class for its throwaway worktree: `members/keel-guards/src/history.rs:52-62` ("`git worktree add` EXITS 0 WHILE FAILING TO WRITE FILES — on Windows it reports 'Filename too long'") and `:111-113` (moved to the system temp dir for the same reason). — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.3 sets `core.longpaths=true` on the clone command *and* names a short run root (the temp path under `AppData\Local\Temp\…` is the long one), and the materialise step verifies the checkout is complete (`git status --porcelain` empty against the base) before spawning — the history.rs "verdict UNKNOWN" branch, reused.

**N4. [MINOR, but for the human] "Run-scoped, readable, revoked at reap" describes a credential this host does not have.** — EVIDENCE: login is the seat's OAuth (`%USERPROFILE%\.claude\.credentials.json` present, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` unset); the only mintable/revocable per-run credential is an org API key, which the deliverable's own launcher text forbids (N1 iii). A seat token copied into the run profile is the human's whole seat, refreshable, and "revoked at reap" means deleting the copy. — RESOLVES IF: I-5's slice-1 cell and V1 say "the agent holds a copy of the human's seat credential; deletion at reap, no scoping" and F1's acceptance text carries it; or F1 gains the API-key option with its billing consequence named.

**N5. [MINOR] "Typed proposals in the write API's own input form" names a form that does not exist.** — EVIDENCE: the write API's input is argv plus prose files (`cli_facts.rs:69`); `git grep -i journal -- members/keel-write/src` is empty. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.5 names it: the run-aware keel inside the clone journals each `keel record` invocation (verb, args, file digests) and the harvest replays the journal against the ref; hand-authored proposal files would be D0224-class friction.

The honest cover is the stated F1(a) residual ("writable by path whatever the environment says") and F1(b); say that, not I-1.

##### What survived

- **The boundary is code, not prose.** Harvest-fails-on-undeclared-change, replay through `keel record`, gate in a checkout the agent never wrote, and the run-id refusal at the write are each a refusal keel already knows how to make (`ReceiptOwed`, provenance-never-defaulted). N2 sharpens *how* the harvest reads the tree; it does not weaken the idea.
- **Slice 1 with no daemon, lease server or proxy** is still the right operational order for one laptop; the two-day-claim, where-the-ladder-runs and single-class objections are closed.
- **V5 is a real falsifier.** Every one of N1–N3 fails the "one `keel run` end to end on this host" measurement the direction already owes before approval — the recipe has bugs, the direction has the test that finds them.
- **Clone over worktree, blob digests, `autocrlf=false`, Job Object** all re-verified; the long-path fix (N3) is a one-flag change keel's own history.rs already made once.

If the author declines any of N1–N3, my verdict is OBJECT with that item as the residue: a slice-1 recipe that cannot complete one run on this host is not one the human can approve as "measured".

##### Findings (all introduced by revision 3 unless marked)

1. [MAJOR] **Removing the tracked hook configuration from the clone makes every run fail its own harvest.** `.claude/settings.json` is tracked (`git ls-files .claude` lists it). §3.1.3 removes it from the clone; §3.1.5 harvests by "`git diff --name-only` of the clone against its recorded base tree" and "any changed path outside the declared outputs fails the step". The deletion is a changed path outside every declared output set. — RESOLVES IF: materialise commits the scrub (hook config removed, local config set) as the run's *materialised base*, the receipt records both the parent tree id and the materialised base id, and the harvest diffs against the latter. One paragraph; no decision needed.

2. [MAJOR] **"Governance outputs ... replayed through `keel record`, never blob-copied" excludes most of what a keel step writes under `.tracking/`, and contradicts I-2's own violation test.** `keel record`'s sub-verbs (`.engine/cli/commands.sysml:34`, synopsis verbatim): "a Decision, an Issue with its resolver, a human's words verbatim, the story translating them, a task with its Definition of Done, a sprint's ceremony scaffold ..., a verdict on a task's DoD or a ceremony gate, a review batch as linked critiques, an indicator measurement or a dated snapshot, fresh gate results at HEAD". Not covered: editing an existing item's text, `satisfy`/`verify`/`allocate`/`#DerivedFrom` edges, a Need or SystemRequirement, `estimatedPoints`, a `#Supersede` beside a CLI fact — CLAUDE.md §4 says so itself ("Write API only; direct edits for what it doesn't cover"). Under §3.1.5 as written those steps cannot declare their outputs, so they stay interactive — and C2's measured fact will read "hypothesis B" for a reason the design created. Meanwhile I-2's violation test speaks of "a harvested blob adding `: TestResult`, `outcome =` ... written rather than quarantined", which presumes blobs under `.tracking` ARE harvested. This is an over-tightening of W1, not a dilution, but it shrinks the wall's reach to the API's current footprint. — RESOLVES IF: two harvest paths are stated: (a) proposals the API covers are replayed (every write-time refusal fires); (b) other `.tracking` blobs are harvested under `validate` plus the I-2 evidence-surface quarantine (`: TestResult`, `outcome =`, acceptance `Test`, `DecisionStatus`, process files), with the count of path-(b) harvests a slice-1 indicator that drives API growth. **This is a human-decidable fork**: refuse (b) and force API coverage first (friction, D0054), or accept (b)'s weaker check — the one issue454 exposed (`.tracking/issues-claudeOpus5.sysml:1837`).

3. [MAJOR] **The run-claim window "computed in `claims.rs` ... so a dead run frees its step in minutes" is not computable on the clock `claims.rs` uses.** `repo_today` is `git log -1 --format=%cs` (`members/keel-model/src/queries.rs:551-561`) — the committer *date*, ten characters; `ClaimView.age_days: i64` (`claims.rs:24`); every committed `claimedAt` is a date (`.tracking/claims/claudeOpus5.sysml:18`: `"2026-08-15"`). D0013: "commits are the engine timeline" (`0013-*.sysml:11`). A window of deadline-plus-grace in minutes needs a `claimedAt` with time of day AND a clock that is not git ancestry — the invoking host's wall clock — which is a D0013 clause this direction would then be reversing silently, the shape F0b was written to avoid. The live fact slice 1 actually has is the pid (the D0493 stub shape, `0493-*.sysml:17`). — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.2 reads "run liveness in slice 1 = `keel run` pid + start time, checked at every `keel run` and frontier read; the `Claim` keeps its two-day window as the coarse backstop" — no clock change, no D0013 collision; OR the run claim carries a second-resolution `claimedAt` judged against the host clock and D0013 joins the held set as F0e. Either way I-6's violation test is run against a killed `keel run`.

4. [MAJOR] **Slice 1's "run-scoped provider credential ... revoked at reap" has no mechanism with the credential the human holds.** The launcher authenticates as "the user's `claude` CLI subscription/ENTERPRISE session — the server NEVER sets ANTHROPIC_API_KEY" (`members/keel-serve/src/serve.rs:1211-1212`, `:1527`). A subscription session cannot be scoped to a run. The Admin API lists, retrieves and updates organisation API keys and "never returns the key's secret value"; no create-per-run endpoint is documented ([Admin API docs](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/admin-api), [Retrieve API Key](https://docs.anthropic.com/en/api/admin-api/apikeys/get-api-key)). So I-5's slice-1 row ("relaxed: run-scoped, readable, revoked at reap") is a claim without a mechanism; §3.2.1 already concedes this for slice 2 ("the credential the human holds today, and its eligibility, is stated in the CR"). — RESOLVES IF: I-5's slice-1 cell reads "UNHELD: the agent holds the credential the CR names; if that is the human's session it outlives the step; scoped at slice 3 by the proxy", and V1 says so. Honest, and W1/W2 do not depend on it.

5. [MAJOR] **The slice-3 trigger has already fired.** §3.2 end: "a recorded Statement asking for the first CONSTRAINED step (a container, no readable credential)". st183, recorded (`.tracking/intake/intake-2026-09-20.sysml:29`): "Let's just do both. A constrained container option and and an unconstrained agent option." §1 even says "The CONSTRAINED class st183 asked for ... arrives in slice 3". The human asked for both; the direction sequences one third and writes its trigger as if it had not been asked. For st189 ("convince me") this is the one place the text can be read as quietly dropping half the ask. — RESOLVES IF: the trigger is replaced by a sequencing statement — "st183 is the statement; slice 3 follows slice 1's measured run because a container without the harvest is Docker; the human confirms the order" — held beside F1 as the human's call.

6. [MINOR] **C1's sentence "a working copy the agent cannot write past" holds only under F1(b).** Under F1(a) §3.1.3 itself says the parent "and every other repository on disk are writable by path". — RESOLVES IF: C1 reads "a harvest of declared paths cannot be talked past; under F1(b) neither can the working copy". And F1's hedge "(b) if the CR's measured floor allows it" hedges on the wrong cost: (b)'s cost is one-time (an OS user, ACLs, a spawn-as-user path), not per-run floor. Name that cost instead.

7. [MINOR] **I-10 names the bound's home but not its enforcer.** — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.6 adds "`keel run` refuses when live run claims (pid-live, finding 3) ≥ the bound; the refusal is a committed record".

##### Findings (revision-3-introduced only)

1. [MAJOR] **`KEEL_NO_RECEIPT=1` on the keel-run gate (§3.1.5) forfeits D0474 for no integrity gain and multiplies slice 1's per-step gate cost by roughly fifteen on a persistent checkout.** The receipt D0371/D0474 honour lives in `.keel/metrics/`, a pre-write protected surface written only by keel; in a checkout the agent never wrote, a receipt keel itself left there is keel's own fact, keyed on content — the exact property the gate wants. On a fresh checkout there is no receipt to read, so the flag is redundant; on a reused keel-owned checkout (the natural home for the shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` §3.1.5 already names) it forces the full set every run. Measured cost of that difference in the tree: `touched-receipt.toml` at f365cb7a, `skipped = []`, 84 rows, `seconds = 866`; sprint 706's DoD: "the first run executed 63 binaries in 1246 s and kept 60 observations, the second executed the 3 that had been red (56 s, 12 passed) and skipped 60". With gate parallelism pinned to 1 (§3.1.5, D0182 kept for the gate), N parallel `keel run`s (§3.1.6) queue on a serial 14–21-minute gate: about four ladder-gated steps an hour, versus tens with the skip. — EVIDENCE: `.keel/metrics/touched-receipt.toml` (`seconds = 866`, `skipped = []`); `.tracking/delivery/sprint706_*.sysml:18`; D0474 decision text ("The receipt files ... join the pre-write protected surfaces ... so only keel writes them"); `members/keel-fs/src/fsx.rs:78`. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.5 replaces `KEEL_NO_RECEIPT=1` with "the gate's checkout is keel-owned and persistent; receipts in it are keel-written and honoured (D0371/D0474); no receipt from the agent's clone is ever read because the clone is never the gate's tree", and V5's measured floor is stated with and without the skip.

2. [MAJOR] **Replay-through-`keel record` bounds slice 1's runnable set to the thirteen `record` sub-verbs, and the body does not say so.** `commands.sysml:34` (the D0271 authored fact; `view_verbs.rs:455-467` is its dispatch) covers decision, issue, statement, story, task, sprint, result, gate-result, review, measurement, indicator-snapshot, reverify, mint. There is no verb for a Need, SystemRequirement, Persona, UseCase, Brief, Architecture item, Risk, Release or Actor. I-2 forbids blob-copying anything under `.tracking/`, so a Business or Architecture step whose declared output is a Need cannot be harvested at all in slice 1 — it is not runnable, and `keel run` correctly refuses it. This is fine for payback (see below) but it is a scoping fact with a cost attached: either the CR extends the write API by one verb per item type (not `schema/core`, so no frozen-schema gate, but authoring-friction risk D0054 applies to each), or slice 1 is Delivery-only and says so. — EVIDENCE: `.engine/cli/commands.sysml:34` invocation string; `git grep -E '"(need|requirement|persona|usecase)"\s*=>' -- members keel-cli/src` returns no dispatch arm. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.5 or §3.1.1 states "replayable governance outputs = the `record` sub-verbs; a step declaring another `.tracking` item type is not runnable until a verb exists", and the CR's scope names which it adds, if any.

3. [MINOR] `acceptance.rs:627` is a blank line; the holder rule the sentence quotes ("holdership = earliest un-expired `claimedAt`") is at `:629`. Citation only.

##### Does the harvest replay or the keel-owned checkout change the payback order or cost of slice 1?

**Order: no.** The classes that pay are R1 (68: agent recorded its own result), R2 (27: monitor modified by the monitored) and R3a (8: records eaten by the shell) — 103 of the 129 — and every one is Delivery ceremony: sprint records, DoD results, gate results, Decisions, Issues. Those are exactly the item types `record` already writes. The replay therefore covers the paying set without extension; finding 2 only bounds what slice 1 can *additionally* run, and the Business/Architecture steps it excludes have no counted defect class behind them. The keel-owned checkout is what makes I-2's second clause real (the gate reads nothing the agent could write), and it closes R2's four examples above, which the harvest wall alone did not (a gate run in the agent's clone reads the agent's `.claude/settings.json`, `.keel/`, and `target/`).

**Cost: yes, two ways, both stated now and both cheap to fix.** (a) Finding 1: the forced run is a per-step tax of 800–1200 s that the direction imposes on itself with one token; at parallelism 1 it is also the throughput ceiling of §3.1.6. Without the flag, the gate's cost is the same ladder the verifier runs today (`verify-receipt.toml`: 351 s total, 261 s of it the touched rung) — i.e. slice 1 pays what is already paid, minus the re-runs retros 737/738/742 record. (b) Finding 2: any write-API verb the CR adds is authoring cost before the first non-Delivery step runs. Neither moves slice 1 out of first place; slice 1 remains the only slice with a counted defect class and a measured cost behind it.

##### Other panelists' resolutions — checked

- **PEDANT-1 1 → C6 "about a third".** Confirmed; and I own the "over 40%" — my round-1 finding 4 wrote it from 292 + 80 over 667 without doing the division.
- **SE 2 → §3.1.5 replay.** Right on the evidence: issue454's six hand-written `TestResult`s with non-hex ids passed `validate` and `guard` (`:1837`, verbatim: "four of the six were not hex"). Replay through the write API is the only path on which `ReceiptOwed` (`write.rs:618`, kind-of check on the judging actor) fires. My finding 2 is the cost of that resolution, not a rebuttal of it.
- **PESSIMIST-2 3 → shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, parallelism 1.** Consistent; finding 1 is the consequence the revision did not price.
- **PED-3 4 · SE 7 → full clone, `--shared` dropped.** The store is small enough (the body's 18 + 60 MiB) that the clone is seconds; no ROI objection.

##### What survived

- Slice 1 is still the one slice whose payback is counted (103 of 129 in three Delivery-ceremony classes) and whose cost is already paid today in ladder seconds; nothing in revision 3 moves that.
- The subject/evidence split in I-2 is buildable: authoring steps' outputs validate, evidence surfaces are refused, and the gate reads a tree the agent never wrote.
- The run-claim window computed from the deadline removes the hypothesis-B bypass path I named in round 2 (a step held dead for two days is a step the human routes around).
- The write API is the right harvest landing, and its coverage is an authored fact (`commands.sysml:34`), so finding 2's boundary is checkable by the `cli-surface-declared` guard rather than by prose.
- C6 states the denominator; the human can see that about a third of the ledger is untouched by any runner protocol, and that F3 decides whether it travels.

##### VERDICT

ACCEPT — conditional on two named, verifiable changes: (1) §3.1.5 drops `KEEL_NO_RECEIPT=1` in favour of "keel-owned persistent checkout; keel-written receipts honoured; the agent's clone is never the gate's tree", with V5's floor stated with and without the skip; (2) §3.1 states that replayable governance outputs are the `record` sub-verbs and names any verb the CR adds. Both are edits to the artifact, each checkable against `fsx.rs:78` and `commands.sysml:34`. My round-2 flip set {3, 5} is resolved in substance; the numbers attributed to my file are correct; neither revision-3 mechanism changes the payback order, and the two cost effects they introduce are stated above for the human at the size the tree measures them.

##### Findings

###### Round-2 flip set {1,2,3,4,6} and finding 7, against revision 3

1. [MAJOR] **R2-1 (F0 scope): resolved in shape, wrong in the condition it says it keeps.** The shape is right: `#SupersedeClause` "reverses one clause of Y named in X's text and leaves Y in force" (`0398:20`); clause 5 is one clause, reversed whole; D0306 one-clause-per-Decision and D0303 layering fit. But clause 5's fourth condition is "only with its own code inside the signed keystone" (`0209:19`) — the ORCHESTRATOR's code. I-11 puts *process definitions* in the keystone, which D0070 already does ("HARD LOCK ... any staged change to a process-def file (.engine/processes/* or .engine/workflows/*) MUST be co-committed with a Decision", `0070:26`). The `process-change` guard's lock set is `.engine/processes`, `.engine/workflows`, `members/keel-guards/src/`, `guard_names.rs`, `.githooks/`, `.github/workflows/*.yml` (`.engine/docs/guards.md:50`). `keel run`'s materialise/harvest/replay/gate code — the control C1 rests on — is under none of them unless placed there. So all four of clause 5's conditions are dropped while C0 says one is re-adopted; the same silent-reversal defect as round 2, on the fourth condition. — RESOLVES IF: I-11 (or the CR) puts the run code under the process-change lock as a listed prefix (the D0504 shape), or F0 states all four conditions dropped, the fourth with its reason.

2. **R2-2 (I-10 vs D0204 cl.2): RESOLVED.** The bound is on concurrent live runs; a run ends when `keel run` exits at harvest/gate and §3.2.4 makes `awaiting-human` release the lease, so the count does not shrink by the human's act. `proposed` depth is an indicator. F0c holds (`0204:19` untouched); F0d holds — the D0192 acceptance words at `0192:26` are "option A is fine. this hasn't been a real issue to date" (chat, 2026-08-22), as C5 quotes.

3. [MAJOR] **R2-3 (I-2): the subject/evidence split is right; the governance grain is wrong, newly.** Revision 3 reads "Governance outputs under `.tracking/` are typed proposals replayed through `keel record`". keel's own definition of a governance surface is `PROTECTED_PATHS` (`members/keel-write/src/claude_surface.rs:79-86`): `.tracking/issues`, `.tracking/backlog.sysml`, `.tracking/critiques`, `.tracking/delivery/`, **`.engine/decisions/`**, `.keel/metrics/`. `keel record decision` writes to `.engine/decisions/` (`write.rs:1921`) and the acceptance `TestResult` lives in the same file (`0209:26`). I-2 excludes `.keel/**`, `target/**` and "any process definition" — not `.engine/decisions/`. A step declaring the path `.engine/decisions/0600-x.sysml` is a "non-governance output ... read as blobs and committed" (§3.1.5) carrying a typed `: TestResult { judgedBy = "wweatherholtz" }` — the issue454 shape the artifact itself cites (`validate` accepted six typed results, `:1837`). I-2's violation test names this case; §3.1.5 has no parse of non-governance blobs, so test and mechanism sit at different grains again. Item-vs-path: outputs are "item names or repo paths" (§3.1.1); MATCH is stated for paths only (`git diff --name-only`), and nothing checks that a replayed proposal's *verb* matches the declared item type (a step declaring one Decision whose proposal is `record result`). — RESOLVES IF: governance for harvest is defined BY REFERENCE to `PROTECTED_PATHS` ∪ `CONTROL_PLANE_PATHS` (one home, D0105); a declared output path under them is refused at definition; item outputs are declared by `record` verb and the replay refuses an undeclared verb.

4. [MAJOR] **R2-4 (two clocks): renamed, not resolved — and it now contradicts D0013.** §3.1.2: "the run claim's window is computed in `claims.rs` from the step type's declared wall-clock deadline plus harvest grace ... so a dead run frees its step in minutes". In `claims.rs` the clock is `today = repo_today(root)` = `git log -1 --format=%cs` (`queries.rs:551-561`, a `YYYY-MM-DD`), age is `days_between(&at, &today)` (`:526`), eligibility `(0..=CLAIM_EXPIRY_DAYS).contains(&age)` in whole days; every `claimedAt` in the tree is a date (`"2026-08-15"`). Two consequences: resolution is one day, and the clock advances only when a commit lands. Minutes cannot be computed there. Computing them needs wall-clock now, and D0013 says "Stay git-only: commits are the engine timeline ... NOT the engine tracking clock" (`0013:11`) — so the sentence reverses D0013 for run claims with no held Decision: the D0105 dual truth of rounds 1–2, moved inside one function. Three attached inconsistencies: `claims.rs` reads no item type, and the "step type's declared wall-clock deadline" has no field (`process.sysml:25-36`: `triggerCondition, actionText, producedArtifact, owner, checkedBy`) — absent from §3.1.1's CR; `Claim` carries "claimedItem/claimedBy/claimedAt/claimedAgainst and NOTHING ELSE" (`work.sysml:55-70`), so a run claim cannot name its run (I-1) — "no field on `Claim`, `schema/core` untouched" and I-1 cannot both hold; `TestResult` has `outcome/judgedAt/judgedAgainst/judgedBy/notes` (`verification.sysml:53-58`), so "stamped with the run id" is a `notes` convention or a frozen-schema field, unlisted. — RESOLVES IF: EITHER (a) the tree-side run claim keeps the git clock and I-6's minutes-scale liveness is the HOST fact (pid + start, read by `keel run`/`orient`), with I-6's slice-1 test restated ("kill `keel run`: the step is offered again once the pid is dead; the claim expires on the git clock"); OR (b) a held `#SupersedeClause` on D0013 scoping wall-clock to run claims. Either way §3.1.1's CR lists the deadline field and where the run id lives.

5. [MAJOR] **R2-6 (I-5): resolved as a class split; one unsupported mechanism remains.** §3.1.3: the provider credential is "scoped to the run, revoked at reap". §3.2.1 defers to the CR whether the credential the human holds today ("a personal subscription OAuth token") is even eligible. Slice 1 asserts per-run scoping and revocation of that same unknown credential with no proxy and no named provider API. Slice 1 claims what slice 2 admits it does not know. — RESOLVES IF: I-5's slice-1 column reads "not enforced: the host's credential as it is; spend uncontrolled (V1)", or the revocation mechanism is named.

6. **R2-7 (I-11 vs D0436): RESOLVED.** `runnable` computed; `keel run` refuses an act; `validate` reports — D0436's own shape. One residue, MINOR: `keel run <process>.<step>` names a step directly, and §3.1.7 maps `keel advance` to "the assignment order", but nothing says `keel run` is refused for a step `advance --to` would refuse ("REFUSED (exit 1) if any earlier step bound to a guard or rule is red", `0436:19`). — RESOLVES IF: `keel run` applies D0436's refusal.

###### New in revision 3

7. [MINOR] **I-1 vs I-7.** I-1: "every write to the tree names the run that produced it — including the invoker's". I-7: human judgment "enters by the human's own identity". The human's `keel accept` is a write to the tree naming no run. The violation test is narrow and right; the prose is over-broad. — RESOLVES IF: "every write a runnable step produces names its run".

8. [MINOR] **§3.1.5 "an AI-examined method lands `proposed`" names a case I-2 forbids.** Every `checkedBy` shape is computed — a guard, a rule, or `gate:<phase>` (`guards.md:72`, D0435/D0436) — and I-2 says the gate is keel's computation. Either name the shape that yields `proposed` (a `gate:<phase>` whose phase `Test` carries a demo/analyze/inspect method) or drop the sentence.

9. [MINOR] **Citations.** "pid + start time, the D0493 stub shape": D0493's stub carries `pid` only, liveness by `tasklist` (`0493:17`); start time is the artifact's addition — say so. "eight hooks fire in any clone": `.claude/settings.json` dispatches nine `keel hook` verbs (config-change, kill-switch, post-edit, pre-bash, pre-write, stop, subagent-start, subagent-stop, user-prompt); the list omits `kill-switch`. F0b names D0182's single-writer clause; the same sentence at `0182:17` opens "Committed mode is in-place execution", which the clone also reverses — under D0398 the clause reverses whole, so name it, and the "kept for the gate" portion is a re-adopted clause, as F0's is. §3.0's receipt (digests, model id, usage) has no type in `schema/core` (no Run/Receipt/Lease def exists); the launcher's precedent is a `Test`+`TestResult` pair at `.tracking/runs/run-<id>.sysml` (`launcher.rs:7-10`) — the CR must say which.

##### What survived

- F0's instrument and shape are consistent: `#SupersedeClause` on one clause, whole; D0306/D0303 layering; D0209's rationale reserves clause 5 for the inversion; HELD under D0337. Only the *content* of the re-adopted condition is wrong (finding 1).
- I-10 no longer gates on the human; F0c and F0d verified — nothing else in D0204 or D0192 moved, and the D0192 words are verbatim.
