##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] **Slice 1's "run-scoped provider credential … revoked at reap" cannot be minted or revoked per run on this host's provider.** — EVIDENCE: the Admin API's API-key surface is `list` and `update` only (`/v1/organizations/api_keys` — `.api_keys` - `list`, `update`); "Admin API keys are created in the Claude Console by organization admins." The credential present on this host is `C:\Users\WilliamWeatherholtz\.claude\.credentials.json` — an OAuth subscription token (the one §3.2.1 says slice 2 will REFUSE). Scenario: keel places that file in the throwaway profile; the agent (or an orphan it left, finding 5) copies it; at reap nothing is revoked because there is nothing to revoke — the token is the developer's identity, refreshable. I-5's slice-1 violation test ("a run credential is accepted after its window") has no keel-side enforcer and no provider-side one. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.3 names the credential form: (a) a pre-provisioned pool of organisation API keys, one leased per run and deactivated at reap by Admin `update status=inactive` (requires an Admin key and an API-billed org — stated as a slice-1 prerequisite), or (b) with a subscription token, "the credential is the developer's own; I-5 is VOID in slice 1, not relaxed", and V1 says so.

2. [MAJOR] **A replayed `record decision` proposal lands ACCEPTED under standing consent — an agent inside a run can author an accepted Decision, including one that supersedes another.** — EVIDENCE: `members/keel-view/src/view_verbs.rs:432-449`: `auto_accept_under_consent` calls `accept_decision(…, judge, author, &note)` with `judge` = the single declared decider (the human) and `author` = the actor running `record decision`; `members/keel-write/src/write.rs:612-618` `refuse_ai_judgment` passes when `kind_of(&root, judged_by)` is `Some("human")` — it checks the NAMED judge, not the caller. The only holds are the D0439 marker words and the fork shape (`write.rs:1969`). I-2's own violation test names "a `DecisionStatus` change is written rather than quarantined"; §3.1.5's replay produces exactly that through the API's legitimate path, and `--supersedes` (D0352) rides with it. — RESOLVES IF: the replay runs with standing consent SUSPENDED — every replayed Decision lands `proposed` into the authority queue, and `--supersedes`/`--supersedes-clause` from a run are refused at replay (a Decision retiring another is the human's or the console's act); or the direction states, as a held clause, that standing consent extends to runs.

3. [MAJOR] **The typed-proposal form is forgeable through the actor field unless replay refuses it.** — EVIDENCE: `members/keel-actor/src/actor.rs:10-11`: resolution order is "1. an explicit argument from the caller (`--judged-by` / `--author` / `--by`) 2. the `KEEL_ACTOR` environment variable"; the check that stops an AI writing a human's judgment (`refuse_ai_judgment`) reads the kind of the name supplied. The control that matches `--by` values against `Person` names is the D0178 Bash carve-out (`actor.rs:74-75`) — a `pre-bash` hook, absent in the clone by §3.1.3's own design and absent at replay (keel's process, no hook). Scenario: a governance output in "the write API's own input form" carries `--judged-by WilliamWeatherholtz`; the write layer sees a human judge and lands it. Same shape for a run-id field: the agent knows its run id (the clone path carries it), so any `record` verb that accepts one is I-1's bypass. — RESOLVES IF: §3.1.5 states that a proposal carrying an actor, date, run id or `judgedAgainst` field is REFUSED (recorded as a quarantine Issue), and keel stamps the run's `Actor` (kind `ai`) and the harvest date itself; and that no `record` verb accepts a run id — the stamp exists only on the `keel run` harvest code path.

4. [MAJOR] **Revision 3's removal of `.claude/settings.json` from the clone fails its own harvest on every run.** — EVIDENCE: `git ls-files .claude` lists `.claude/settings.json` (tracked, with `.claude/.keel-surface`); §3.1.5: "`git diff --name-only` of the clone against its recorded base tree; any changed path outside the declared outputs fails the step". Keel's own deletion is a changed path outside every declared output set. Second, smaller: `git clone` checks out under the system `autocrlf=true` before any local config exists, so a file the agent merely touches re-hashes CRLF against an LF blob. — RESOLVES IF: keel commits the materialisation edits inside the clone as the run's base commit and records THAT tree id as the diff baseline (the receipt carries both ids), and the clone is `git clone -c core.autocrlf=false`.

5. [MINOR] **"Labelled `keel.run=<id>`" is asked of the agent; an unlabelled container is invisible to the kill and the diff.** — EVIDENCE: `docker ps -a --filter label=keel.run=x` works on this host (rc=0), but the agent runs `docker run` itself with the same user's pipe (`docker context show` → `desktop-linux`, `DOCKER_HOST` unset) and need not label. Three unrelated containers (systems-mortise, syson ×2) are live here, so "leftover containers by label" is the right filter for THEM and the wrong one for the agent's. — RESOLVES IF: keel takes a container census (`docker ps -aq`) at spawn and at reap; anything new is killed and fails the step; the label is a convenience, not the control.

6. [MINOR] **`TEMP`/`TMP` are not in the scrub set** and point at the real profile (`C:\Users\WILLIA~1\AppData\Local\Temp`) — the harness's own scratchpad lives there; the profile diff misses it. Add both.

7. [MINOR] **F1(b)'s floor is not only seconds.** In this shell the developer is `not in Administrators`; a local low-privilege user, its ACLs, and running a child as it (a stored secondary credential or a service account, plus `docker-users` membership for the pipe) each need elevation. The CR's measured floor for F1(b) should include the one-time admin step or the human chooses (a) by default without knowing it.

8. [MAJOR] **A gate on `refs/keel/runs/<id>` and a later merge to trunk leaves "done" on a tree nobody gated — and SUSPECT will not catch it.** — EVIDENCE: `members/keel-model/src/suspect.rs:1-7`: the walk marks a done task suspect when a dependency's `DoD` criterion TEXT changed, propagates up the dependency graph, and marks manifest deliverables "whose own source drifted" — never because the tree around a result changed. §3.1.6 + F0b: N runs from one base each pass their gate on their own ref; §3.1.5 "Merge to trunk is by a gate-green step or the human". Scenario: two green runs touch adjacent `.tracking` files; the merge validates red (a duplicate name, a broken edge) and both `TestResult`s stand, `judgedAgainst` their own refs. This is the merge-queue problem the D0182 single-writer pin existed to avoid, reopened by lifting it for the run. — RESOLVES IF: the gate's subject is the candidate `merge(trunk HEAD, run ref)` in the keel-owned checkout, serialised at the gate parallelism 1 the design already has; landing is a fast-forward of trunk to that gated tree; trunk moving during the gate re-queues the run. One clause; I-2 then means what it says at trunk.

##### Findings

1. [MAJOR] **The run-claim window in minutes is unimplementable on the claim substrate: the claim clock is HEAD's commit DATE.** `keel claim` writes `claimedAt` from `git log -1 --format=%cs`, filtered to ten characters (`members/keel-write/src/write.rs:3141-3150`, `claimedAt = "{at}"` at `:3164`); liveness is `days_between(&at, &today)` with `today = repo_today(root)` = the same `%cs` of HEAD (`members/keel-model/src/claims.rs:66-70`; `queries.rs:526-561`, a Y-M-D parser). Consequences: (i) the smallest expressible window is "the same git-day", and the git-day advances only when a commit lands — a `keel run` killed on Friday holds its step until a later commit carries a later date, whatever the deadline says; (ii) every claim written against one HEAD ties on `claimedAt` and is ordered by UUID; (iii) I-10's live-run count, if read from claims, counts dead runs all day. §3.2.2's "one clock, the tree's" is therefore inverted: the tree has no minute clock. — RESOLVES IF the human picks one: **(A)** the run's liveness is NOT a tree `Claim`: it is the D0493 stub (pid + start, wall-clock, machine-local; `is_alive` via `tasklist` already exists, `members/keel-suite/src/touched.rs:776-779`) plus the committed run record; `keel run` refuses a step with a live stub on this host; slice 1 is single-host, so at-most-one-holder is a host fact in slice 1 and the server's fact in slice 2 (the partition already puts the lease server-side); the run's claim, if written at all, is a day-granular fact for the human's frontier and says so. **(C)** the reaper authors a `#Supersede` edge on the dead run's claim and `claims()` honours supersession (today `claim_rows` reads no edges) — a mechanism change in `claims.rs`, no schema change. Not recommended: **(B)** time-of-day in `claimedAt` and a wall clock in `claims()`, which abandons git-derived time (D0013) and makes clones disagree (K15). I recommend A; §3.2.2's coupling then reads "the lease is the clock; the tree records the claim's day and the receipt or refusal that closed it".

2. [MAJOR] **"A checkout the agent never wrote" holds until the third rung: the gate executes harvested code as the invoker, in the keel-owned checkout, with a target directory shared across runs.** The ladder is Validate, Guard, Clippy, Probe, Touched (`members/keel-suite/src/verify.rs:369-374`); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` compiles the subject's build scripts and proc-macros — every crate has `build = "../build/provenance.rs"` (`keel-cli/Cargo.toml:15`; sprint 757) — so a step whose declared outputs include `build/**`, a `Cargo.toml` or a proc-macro runs arbitrary code at rung 3 with the invoker's OS identity, before the probe rung reads the checkout, with the parent repository and the invoker's credential store reachable by path (F1's residual, which §3.1.3 states for the RUN only; F1(b) as written leaves the GATE running as the invoker). The shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (§3.1.5) is a cross-run channel: a build script in run A's gate can write into artifacts run B's gate links; cargo fingerprints inputs, not a build script's side effects. `KEEL_NO_RECEIPT=1` covers the receipt reads, not this. — RESOLVES IF: I-2 is reworded to "a checkout the agent holds no handle to, in which the agent's code executes only under the run's boundary"; the cargo rungs (clippy, probe when it is a cargo command, touched) run under the same Job Object and F1 identity as the run, keel reading only exit codes and digests; the tree-side rungs (validate, guard) run before any harvested code executes (they already do); and the human decides the target-directory trade: per-run `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (isolation; a cold build per gate — V5's minutes become tens of minutes) or shared with the channel stated in V1.

3. [MAJOR] **The run-id stamp has no landing, and the fourth control covers one of the three `checkedBy` kinds.** `TestResult` carries `outcome, judgedAt, judgedAgainst, judgedBy, notes` and nothing else (`.engine/schema/core/verification.sysml:53-59`); §3.1.1's schema CR names only `ProcessStep`. And `checkedBy` resolves to a guard name, a declared rule, or `gate:<name>` (`members/keel-guards/src/enforcement.rs:1261-1298`; e.g. `gate:refine` at `.engine/processes/agile-workflow.sysml:85`). Only a `gate:` check has a result to refuse (`append_gate_result`, `write.rs:1299-1322`, `GateNotFound`); a guard- or rule-checked step is done iff the predicate holds over the tree — no result exists, and any actor's tree edit makes it done. So "the only path to done for a runnable step is `keel run`" and C1's falsifier ("runnable-step results not stamped by a run") are true for gate-checked steps only. — RESOLVES IF: the CR names the stamp's home — a `TestResult` attribute (schema/core, so in the CR) or a `// RUN: <id>` line in `notes` in the D0424 `// RAN:` shape, parsed at the write — and §3.1.5 says what `keel run` writes for a guard/rule-checked step (the run receipt; done = the predicate computed by keel over the harvested tree at the gate, I-2-compliant), and C1 scopes the falsifier to gate-checked steps.

4. [MAJOR] **I-5's slice-1 cell says "revoked at reap"; nothing revokes, and the provider forbids minting.** The Admin API documentation: "Can I create new API keys through the Admin API? No. You create API keys in the Claude Console. The Admin API can only read, rename, and change the status of existing keys." (platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/admin-api, FAQ; `status: "inactive"` is the update). A per-run key therefore comes from a Console-minted pool, and inactivating one at reap needs keel to hold an admin-capable credential — a more powerful secret than the one it revokes, which must live outside the run profile. — RESOLVES IF either: I-5 slice 1 reads "deleted from the run profile at reap; the key itself outlives the run; V1 covers it" (the honest default), or the CR names the pool (size = I-10's bound), the `status=inactive` call at reap, and where the admin credential lives.

5. [MINOR] **I-1 overreaches.** "Every write to the tree names the run that produced it — including the invoker's" is false for the human's own writes (`accept`, a Statement). The violation test is correctly scoped; scope the sentence to "every write a step produces, and the result that makes a runnable step done".

6. [MINOR] **The replayable governance surface is exactly the `record` sub-verb set, and three of its verbs are evidence.** `cliRecordVerbs` (`.engine/cli/commands.sysml:34`) offers decision, issue, statement, story, task, sprint, result, gate-result, review, measurement, indicator-snapshot, reverify, mint — no generic typed-edge verb, no item-edit verb (the recorder's `REFUSED:` line, D0473, is the proof). Say it: a step's governance outputs are proposals in this set MINUS result / gate-result / reverify (I-2's evidence exclusion applied to verbs); widening the set is a `CliCommand` fact (D0271), never a blob.

7. [MINOR] Citation check, all verbatim: D0209 clause 5 and D0182's clause quoted exactly; `library.rs:32` resolves `USERPROFILE` then `HOME`; `.gitignore:74` ignores `.keel/`; issue454 at `.tracking/issues-claudeOpus5.sysml:1837`; `serve.rs:1223-1246` is the `taskkill /T` TreeKiller (the doc says it *documents* the orphan, which is accurate — the Job Object is new); eight hooks = seven event keys with two `PreToolUse` matchers in `.claude/settings.json`.

##### Coverage check (asked by the dispatch)

| Slice-1 mechanism (§3.1) | Serves |
|---|---|
| 1 schema CR: inputs/outputs, computed `runnable`, evidence exclusion at validate/run | I-2, I-11 |
| 2 claim window + dead-run reaper | I-6, I-9 — **not computable as stated (finding 1)** |
| 3 clone with no `origin`; `autocrlf=false`; hook config removed; profile scrub; run credential in profile | I-1 (no push path), I-8 (digests), I-3 relaxed, I-5 relaxed; hook removal serves no invariant (harmless; it removes the agent's own turn gate) |
| 4 Job Object; labelled containers; deadline kill; refusal carries no instruction | I-6, I-9, I-2 (issue578) |
| 5 MATCH both directions; replay through `record`; keel-owned checkout + `KEEL_NO_RECEIPT`; `judgedAgainst` pin; run-id stamp; `proposed` never done; model id; profile-diff indicator | I-4, I-2, I-1, I-8, I-7, I-5 observation — **checkout claim false after rung 3 (finding 2); stamp unlanded (finding 3)** |
| 5 shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, gate parallelism 1 | F0b constraint, no invariant — and a cross-run channel (finding 2) |
| 6 per-actor write targets; concurrency bound in `activation.toml` | I-6 (many steps, one holder each), I-10 (enforcer unnamed) |

Every invariant bound in slice 1 has a mechanism except as marked: I-6's is not computable in minutes (1), I-5's "revoked" has none (4), I-10's enforcer is unnamed (1). I-7's slice-1 mechanism is the existing write-layer refusal (`write.rs:857-860`; `accept` "refused for an AI actor") applied to the run's `Actor` — adequate. I-8 trivially. I-11 rests on the `process-change` guard (`guards.md:50`: a process-def change co-commits a marked Decision), the evidence exclusion, and the fact that no `record` verb writes a process — adequate. All eleven violation tests are observable, with I-1's observable only once the stamp has a home (3).

##### Rebuttals

- **OPT-1 3 · OPT-2 5 · PED-2 4 · PESS-2 5 · REDUCER 2 · SE 4** are marked resolved by §3.1.2's `claims.rs` computation. Renamed, not resolved (finding 1): the substrate has no minute clock. REDUCER 2 asked for "expiry matching the deadline" — that is exactly what a date-granular, commit-advanced clock cannot give.
- **PESS-2 3** (where the ladder runs, its cost) is marked resolved by the shared target directory and parallelism 1. The cost answer is right; it opened finding 2.
- **PED-3 3** (no proxy, so I-5 has no enforcer) is marked resolved by "relaxed, stated". Stated, yes; "revoked at reap" still claims an enforcer that does not exist (finding 4).
- **PESS-1 N1 / F0d**, **PED-2 1 / F0**, **PED-2 7 / I-11**: resolved as the map says; D0209 clause 5 is quoted whole and verbatim, D0182's clause verbatim, `runnable` computed and `validate` reporting is consistent with D0436/D0098.

##### What survived

- **The direction.** Assign → materialise → run → harvest declared outputs → replay governance through the write API → keel computes done in its own checkout → the agent's verdict is never an output. Nothing in three rounds has found a way for the child agent to make its step done by testimony; the residue is where keel's own code executes and how liveness is clocked.
- **I-2's subject/evidence split** is the right cut and the evidence surfaces enumerated are the ones the tree actually reads (`guard-receipt.toml`, `touched-receipt.toml`, `verify-receipt.toml`, all under `.keel/metrics/`).
- **Replay through `keel record`** is verified against the four write-time refusals it needs, all of which are write-path facts, not validate-time ones.
- **C0/F0/F0b are honest and verbatim**; the four-condition clause is superseded whole and the keystone re-adopted — the D0070 lock generalised by D0209 cl.2 is a live guard, not a metaphor.
- **The truth partition, the slicing, the per-slice hazard binding** hold; the `stpa-self` record shape the binding needs already exists.
- **The Windows-correct scrub** is right about the host (`USERPROFILE` first, `HOME` fallback).

##### VERDICT

OBJECT (minimal flip set: 1, 2, 3, 4)

Irreducible residue for the human, stated to be decided: **(1)** run liveness is a host/server fact (option A) or a superseded tree claim (option C) — the tree's clock is the commit date and cannot be the run's clock; **(2)** the gate's cargo rungs run under the run's boundary, and the target directory is per-run (isolation, minutes more per gate) or shared (a stated cross-run channel); **(3)** the run-id stamp's home — a `TestResult` attribute in the CR or a `// RUN:` line in `notes` — with the fourth control scoped to gate-checked steps; **(4)** I-5 slice 1 reads "deleted from the profile, not revoked" or the CR names the key pool, the inactivation call and where the admin credential lives. None is a fork of direction; each is a one-paragraph revision-4 change. With those four sentences landed as written above, I accept without a further round.
